- feat(reports): **#1861** — the CCLI Usage Report gained an **Organisation** filter (narrow to one org, or to unattributed views) alongside the existing date-range report, and usage that could be credited to either a personal or an organisational CCLI licence now prefers the organisation's — fixing real under-attribution for orgs whose members also hold personal CCLI numbers. A brand-new self-serve **`/manage/my-ccli-report`** lets an organisation admin pull their own org's figures without needing the system-wide report's permission.
- feat(editor2): **#1862 (epic #1863)** — the Song Editor's Metadata tab now **derives** several fields instead of collecting them by hand: the copyright line shown on the public page previews live as you type Copyright Year(s)/Holder ("Displayed as: …"), with a free-text override only for a genuinely custom statement; a Public Domain suggestion hint (from a credited writer's/composer's death date, or an admin-configurable publication-year fallback) offers a one-click "Use" next to the checkbox but never auto-ticks it; and the Audio/Sheet-music indicators are now a read-only line derived from what's actually attached on the Media tab, with the old manual checkboxes removed entirely.
- feat: **#1863 pickers wave** — six more registry-referencing fields across the app became find-or-create search-select pickers instead of free-text boxes that could mint spelling-variant duplicates: Works' Tune Name + Copyright Holder (#1864), a Songbook's Publisher (#1865), Collections' "Add a song" (#1866), the admin Song-Request's Resolved SongId + the public request form's Songbook field (#1867), Group members + the Publisher-merge picker (#1868), and the Song Editor's Structure-tab section-type dropdown, now sourced from the `tblSongPartTypes` registry instead of a fixed, redeploy-to-grow list (#1869).
- build(ci): per-commit build number (`git rev-list --count HEAD`) is now injected into `infoAppVer.php`'s `Version.Build.Number` at deploy time, alongside the existing SHA/date injection — `NULL` on a local/un-deployed checkout.
- feat(versioning): **#1899** — moved to a tag-derived version scheme: MAJOR (hand-edited, rare), RELEASE (automated at beta→main promotion by `promotion-deploy-bridge.yml`), and BUILD (monotonic per-commit id). Baseline reset to `v1.0.0`; the old auto-bumper (`version-bump.yml`) is retired.
- feat(ccli): **#1897 write-coverage widening** — Service-Mode logging + the org-scoped #1861 report now includes SetlistId for multi-song metrics.
- feat(search): **#1039** — song/songwriter/tune/place search now folds accents and smart apostrophes into their base characters, so "Café" matches "cafe" and "don't" matches "dont".
- fix(security): **#1906** — a security-hardening pass: registration/email-code brute-force protections now actually engage (the registration throttle was dead code; the email-code check was per-IP only), a session-fixation gap on cross-surface admin sign-in is closed, the admin area and social-card endpoint gained security headers/CSP, copyrighted lyrics no longer leak via the share-image endpoint when content-locking is on, and several heavy public endpoints gained rate limits. The `X-Powered-By` header now advertises our own **`iHymns/<version>`** identity instead of leaking the PHP runtime version (the PHP version — the value that maps a host to known runtime bugs — is suppressed at source). No user-visible change; entirely defensive.
- fix(security): **#1905** — the app now returns a real **404** for a made-up path (a `/wp-admin/` scanner probe, or any unknown URL) instead of a misleading HTTP 200 with the app shell. Bad-actor WordPress/scanner scans are 404'd at the web-server edge (and everything else by the front controller), so probes no longer look "valid", no longer log as successes, and search engines don't index dead URLs. The valid-route list is **derived** from the app's own pages, so new pages are recognised automatically — a CI guard keeps it in lockstep with the client router so a real page can never accidentally 404.
- fix(admin): **#1667** — organisation admins can now find the Service Mode pages (Projector Screen, Lead a Service) in the admin menu. They were always allowed to use them, but the menu only showed the links to holders of the broader "manage organisations" permission, so an org admin could reach the pages by URL but never discover them.
- fix(import): **#1673 / #1896** — bulk song imports now keep the copyright line, CCLI number, ISWC, and public-domain flags the source file provides, instead of silently saving blanks for every format. This also un-breaks two downstream things: the CCLI usage report no longer undercounts imported songs, and imported songs now auto-link to their Work by identifier (#1860). Writers/composers credits remain a follow-up (#1904).
- fix(security): **#1699** — a shared **live** set-list link now stops serving once the owner's per-set-list expiry passes. Previously the live read honoured the share link's own expiry but ignored the underlying set-list's `ExpiresAt`, so an expired set-list kept serving content (and later edits) on the anonymous share/social-card surfaces — which could never trigger the owner's lazy clean-up. An expired live share now resolves to "no longer shared" (410 / empty card) without deleting the owner's data.
- fix(settings): **#1710** — a signed-in user is no longer told to "Sign in to sync your selection across devices" on the Settings page. api.php now resolves the signed-in viewer (`$currentUser`) for non-cacheable page fragments — the Settings fragment could never see who was signed in, so its sync notice always showed the signed-out copy. Cacheable fragments are deliberately never personalised (shared-cache safety). A mutation-proven CI guard keeps the fragment↔router viewer contract from silently breaking again.
- docs(api): synced `api-docs.yaml` for the ILID dual-addressing behaviour above, added the canonical `?page=musician` path (previously documented only under its deprecated `person` alias) plus the five `iswc`-sibling identifier pages (`ccli`/`bowi`/`isrc`/`ipi`/`isni`), and added two tree-derived CI guards: an `api-docs.yaml` <-> `infoAppVer.php` version-lockstep check, and a coverage guard ensuring every `/manage/*` nav destination has a matching Help & Guides section (closing the exact gap that let Tunes/Venues/My CCLI Report ship without one).

## [0.5250.0] — 2026-08-14 (alpha)

Site-wide transport-routing fix (every browser POST to a literal `/manage/**.php` silently lost its
request body) + a v2 Song Editor "confidence" pass that restored the admin chrome, adopted the shared
toast, and compacted the arrangement editor. Issues #1855 (closes #1847), #1856, #1857; follow-up
#1858 filed. Owner-directed minor bump `0.5200.0 → 0.5250.0`.

- fix(routing): **every browser POST to a literal `/manage/**.php` URL was silently lobotomised**
  (#1855, closes #1847). `manage/.htaccess` 301-redirects the `.php` form of every `/manage` URL to its
  extensionless sibling for cosmetic URL-hiding — but a browser replays a 301'd POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE as
  a **body-less GET** (only 307/308 preserve method + body, RFC 7231 §6.4). So every write aimed at a
  literal `.php` URL reached PHP with an empty `$body`: the v2 editor's metadata saves and the ENTIRE
  arrangement editor (empty `songId` → 400), plus server-PDF download, bulk import, activity-log geo
  enrichment and place/city upsert. Reads (GET) survived the redirect untouched, so the song loaded
  fully and the failure masqueraded as a client bug for months, driving the #1846/#1847/#1851
  symptom-chasing. Fixed at **both layers**: (a) `manage/.htaccess` gains
  `RewriteCond %{REQUEST_METHOD} ^(GET|HEAD)$` so a write is never redirected; (b) every browser call
  site is normalised to the extensionless URL (v1's proven, years-live convention) — `api-client.js`
  (the headline), `import2.php`, `print.js`, `print-templates.php`, `editor.js` song-links,
  `activity-log.php`, `place-search.js`, plus five tree-derived extras (`metadata-tab.js` tune search,
  `renderEntityPicker.js`, `setup-bulk-runner.js`, `bulk-import-progress.js`, `api2.php` poll URLs).
  Guarded by a new mutation-proven `tests/test-manage-php-urls.js` (tree-derived; bans any browser
  request to `/manage/**.php`), and `tests/browser/router.php` now mirrors the 301 so browser-smoke
  reproduces live Apache semantics (CI's `php -S` router previously did not, which is why this was never
  caught).
- fix(editor2): **restored the shared admin chrome** the v2 rewrite had dropped (#1856). Added the slim
  editor navbar (brand → `/manage/`, Dashboard, Home, Logout — a real way to *exit* Song Editor mode),
  included `admin-footer.php` (the footer copyright/version **and** the shared bottom-right
  `window.iHymnsToast` — transient editor errors/success now surface there instead of the top-of-page
  alert that was invisible when scrolled), and de-double-loaded Bootstrap in the same pass. `#v2-shell`
  now subtracts the navbar + footer heights, so the tabs pane no longer renders as a stray window-edge
  scrollbar.
- fix(editor2): **compacted the arrangement editor** (#1857) — the five presets fold into one dropdown,
  the two stacked labels become inline captions, and the move/remove controls shrink (~100px shorter) —
  while keeping the accessible move-**buttons** (never drag: a full revert to v1's SortableJS would
  regress both SRI #1587/#1647 and WCAG 2.5.7 #1644). The pool/strip chips also gained real styling
  (they had had none). No behaviour removed.
- docs: filed #1858 — the *legacy* editor (`editor/index.php`) double-loads Bootstrap the same way
  (its own emit plus `admin-footer.php`'s); out of scope for this PR, tracked separately.

## [0.5200.0] — 2026-08-14 (alpha)

Musician-profile migration fix + v2 Song Editor cluster + editor/shell hardening + public-shell CSP &
privacy fixes. Merged to `alpha` in #1853; owner-directed minor bump `0.5160.0 → 0.5200.0` sized to a
large feature + hardening batch.

- fix(migrations): **musician-profile P1 migration no longer errors once the #1741 P2 rename has
  landed** (#1824). #1741 P2 renamed the seven `tblCreditPe*`/`tblCreditPerson*` BASE TABLES to
  `tblMusician*` and left UPDATABLE VIEWS under the old names, so the P1 `migrate-musician-profile.php`
  `ALTER TABLE tblCreditPersonMembers ADD UNIQUE KEY …` hit MySQL error 1347 ("not BASE TABLE") on every
  env P2 had reached — and the registry probe read a view's absent index as "missing", pinning the card
  pending forever so "Apply all" retried the failing ALTER each run. Both the script and the probe now
  short-circuit to clean success when `tblMusicians` exists (⟹ P1 already applied on the renamed base
  tables), mirroring P2's own Step-0 idiom. No schema change; `test-migration-registry.php` stays green.

- fix(editor): **credit autosave no longer mints junk `tblMusicians` rows on every keystroke pause**
  (#1843). The Credits tab's per-keystroke debounced `credit_upsert` find-or-created a registry musician
  by exact name, so typing "J" → "Jo" → "John" left two orphan rows behind the real one. `credit_upsert`
  and `credit_delete` now reap the orphaned auto-minted previous-name row post-commit (own transaction,
  best-effort, same-row guard by Id not name), and the debounce rose 500ms → 1000ms to cut churn. Shared
  `musicianReapOrphanedAutoRow()` + `musicianCreditUsageCount()` (extracted from two inline copies);
  guarded for un-migrated installs; new `test-musician-orphan-reap.php`, reap added to the merge-core
  singleness allow-list.

- feat(editor): **v2 Song Editor is now usable on phones and tablets** (#1845). The song-list `<aside>`
  became a Bootstrap 5.3 responsive offcanvas (`.offcanvas-lg`) — unchanged two-pane layout at ≥992px, a
  slide-in drawer below it opened by a `d-lg-none` hamburger; picking a song closes it, and the eight
  tabs became one horizontally-scrollable row instead of wrapping to three. `mountSidebar()` untouched.

- feat(editor): **manual Save button that flushes pending autosaves** (#1846). Autosave is unchanged;
  the new header Save button fires every tab's pending debounced write immediately and confirms
  ("Saving…" → "All changes saved."), via a `ctx.registerFlush(fn)` registry (a plain injected callback,
  not a DOM event — rule #35) that each of the five autosaving tabs (metadata/structure/credits/links/
  media) hands a `flushPending()`; same rebuilt-per-mount / reset-on-teardown lifecycle as `teardowns`.

- feat(editor): **the Language field is once again the IETF BCP 47 live-search picker** (#1849). The
  #1200 v2 rewrite had left it a plain text box; it now re-mounts the shared `ietf-language-picker.js`
  (#681) — the Language/Script/Region typeahead v1 and `/manage/songbooks` already use — seeded from the
  song's current tag and saved through the tab's normal debounced field-save. Reused, not re-forked.

- feat(editor): **single-line song rows in the v2 sidebar with a songbook-coloured stub** (#1850). Each
  row is now `#<number> - <title>` on one line (title ellipsis-truncates) with the songbook abbreviation
  as a right-aligned pill coloured from the app's `--songbook-*` palette; the full title stays in the
  button's accessible name so screen readers always get it (WCAG 1.4.13 / 2.5.3) — the `title` hover
  tooltip is a mouse-only enhancement.

- fix(editor): **the metadata-save error is now two self-explaining messages instead of one ambiguous
  400** (#1847). "songId and at least one known field are required" split into a missing-songId message
  and a no-known-field message, so the real cause (a stale/behind editor build mounting the tab with an
  empty songId) is legible; the underlying fix for such a client is a hard refresh / fresh deploy. Issue
  left open pending the owner's verification.

- fix(csp): **the public shell's CDN→`/vendor` stylesheet fallback is CSP-safe** (#1832). The enforcing
  nonce CSP refused the inline `onerror=` fallback on the Bootstrap / Font-Awesome / Bootstrap-Icons /
  Animate `<link>`s, so a CDN outage silently loaded no local copy. Replaced with one nonce'd `<script>`
  that swaps each `<link>` to its `/vendor` copy when `link.sheet` is null (mutation-proven guard).

- fix(editor) + fix(routing) + fix(bootstrap) + test: **editor/shell hardening — ten fold-in fixes**
  (#1851), most of them regressions this batch's own new features introduced, fixed before first release:
  silent edit loss on song-switch/delete; section/credit "resurrection" from a stale debounced save after
  delete; the language picker's clear-noop and blur-not-saved; the manual Save button's unconditional "All
  changes saved." (now a per-tab failure count); dead controls after deleting the last song; a
  `$out` TypeError guard in `ihymns_bootstrap_js_script()`; a `/vendor` 404 so a missing asset fails loudly
  instead of masquerading as HTTP 200 (rule #1566 class); and a tree-derived reap FK-coverage guard.

- fix(privacy): **Microsoft Clarity session-recording no longer loads under Do-Not-Track** (#1852). The
  shared analytics gate loaded Clarity under DNT with no mitigation (unlike GA4's IP-anonymise / Matomo's
  `setDoNotTrack`); the Clarity gate is now `consent === 'granted' && !dnt` — DNT switches session
  recording off entirely, since it has no privacy-safe mode. Dormant unless a Clarity id is configured;
  mutation-proven guard.

## [0.5160.0] — 2026-08-12 (alpha)

Song-of-the-Day lyric-preview fix (#1841) plus the matching minor version bump.

- feat(song-of-the-day): **the lyric preview is a complete-thought phrase, not
  just the first line** (#1841). A hymn's lyric lines break where the song is
  sung, so its first sung line is very often identical to the title — which made
  the Song-of-the-Day snippet read as the title again (e.g. `"Restore, O Lord,"`).
  A new shared, pure heuristic `lyricLinesJoinPreview()` (on the ONE lyric-line
  read path `includes/lyric_lines_read.php`, rule #25) joins the opening lines
  into one flowing phrase, stopping at the first real sentence end (`. ! ?`) past
  a minimum length, or a character/line cap — never a mid-word cut. `SongOfTheDay`
  now sources its snippet from `lyricLinesPreviewPhrase()` (mirror path) / the
  same joiner (un-migrated LinesJson fallback); the `song_of_the_day` API keeps
  its `firstLine` field name (contract stable, a strict content improvement for
  every consumer, native apps included). The client (`song-of-the-day.js` +
  `.sotd-quote` in `app.css`) renders it on ONE line and truncates to the card
  width with an ellipsis (`text-overflow: ellipsis`), with a `title` attribute
  carrying the full phrase. Guard: `tests/php/test-sotd-preview-phrase.php`
  (mutation-proven — comma-as-boundary, dropped min-floor, and neutered budget
  each reddens a specific case).

- chore(version): **bump 0.5150.0 → 0.5160.0** (owner-directed minor). Lockstep
  across `includes/infoAppVer.php`, `api-docs.yaml`, `manifest.json`, `README.md`,
  `WHATS-NEW.md`, `PROJECT_STATUS.md` and the `.claude/` docs. A small,
  single-feature bump.

## [0.5150.0] — 2026-08-12 (alpha)

Org-branding round on the `claude/issue-sweep-fixes-89` branch: per-organisation logos for Print
Templates (#1830), the Missing Numbers hidden-held highlight (#1829), and an owner-directed
plain-English + minimal-disclosure rewrite of the in-app Help/Guides. Owner-directed **minor version
bump** (0.5100.0 → 0.5150.0) sized to a real feature plus the docs pass.

- feat(org-logos): **per-organisation logo uploads for Print Templates** (#1830). A church can now
  upload its logo (SVG preferred, PNG/APNG also accepted) in any of ten brand-guide shapes — primary,
  combined, wide, stacked, symbol-only, name-only, alternative, single-colour, light-on-dark, and app
  icon — from `/manage/organisations` (system admins) or `/manage/my-organisations` (org admins). SVG
  uploads pass through a brand-new, dedicated hardened sanitiser (`includes/svg_sanitizer.php`) —
  default-deny XML rebuild, XXE-safe (entity loader nulled, DOCTYPE rejected twice, no entity
  substitution), a 19-element allow-list that drops (never unwraps) anything not on it, and a
  render-bomb node/depth budget — before ever being stored; logos are served by a new standalone
  `org-logo.php` endpoint (mirrors `qr.php`/`og-image.php`, `default-src 'none'; sandbox` CSP) and are
  **never inlined** into any page, always a plain `<img src>`. A new `logo` block in the Print Template
  editor lets a curator choose which brand shape to print, or leave it on "Automatic" to walk a
  ladder (primary → combined → wide → stacked → …) and use the best one the org has actually
  uploaded — an explicit choice with nothing uploaded renders nothing, never a broken image. Scope for
  this PR is print only; the app header, projector and OG-image surfaces are deferred to a follow-up
  (the schema, endpoint and alt text already carry everything they'll need). Ships with a
  mutation-proven functional truth table for the sanitiser (`tests/php/test-svg-sanitizer.php`) and a
  tree-derived wiring guard (`tests/php/test-org-logo-surfaces.php`) that bans any surface from
  inlining SVG or reading the dormant unsanitised original. The `logo` block renders in **both** the
  browser Print dialog **and** the downloadable server PDF — the PDF payload relativises the block's
  same-origin image address so it survives the server's HTML safety filter and is inlined as bytes
  (this also fixes the same latent gap for the #1767 QR block); guarded by
  `tests/test-print-pdf-img-src.js`.

- feat(missing-numbers): **Missing Numbers flags numbers held only by a hidden (deleted) song** (#1829,
  merged as #1837). The report still counts a soft-deleted song's number as PRESENT (filling the gap
  and later restoring the original would mint a duplicate number — the #1694 non-unique-index trap),
  but now ALSO surfaces the numbers whose ONLY song is hidden: a "Held by hidden songs" panel, a count
  tile, a per-songbook badge and a link straight to Deleted Songs, so a curator can restore or purge
  them. A number with even one visible song is never flagged. Classified through the ONE gated
  `songVisibleSql()` predicate (degrades to a live-only count on an un-migrated docroot). Guarded by
  `tests/php/test-missing-numbers-hidden.php` (source-scan + transactional behavioural).

- docs(help): **plain-English + minimal-disclosure pass on the in-app Help/Guides** (owner-directed).
  The admin `manage/help.php` and public `includes/pages/help.php` carried GitHub issue-number
  citations, database table names, internal config-flag names and raw permission keys in copy that
  curators, moderators and non-technical admins read. This pass strips ~98 issue-number citations from
  the admin help's visible copy (kept only in the required developer-annotation comments), rephrases
  internal identifiers into plain English, applies the renamed Manage items (Edit History, Find
  Duplicates, Membership Tiers, Role Permissions, Content Access, …), documents the new Missing Numbers
  highlight and the organisation-logo feature, and corrects a stale public-help claim that only admins
  could delete a song (it is available to editors and above, and is recoverable). Display copy only —
  no section anchors, links or logic changed; `php -l` clean.

- chore(version): **bump 0.5100.0 → 0.5150.0** (owner-directed minor). Lockstep across
  `includes/infoAppVer.php`, `api-docs.yaml`, `manifest.json`, `README.md`, `WHATS-NEW.md`,
  `PROJECT_STATUS.md` and the `.claude/` docs.

## [0.5100.0] — 2026-08-12 (alpha)

Follow-up round on the `claude/issue-sweep-fixes-89` branch after the 0.5050.0 batch merged: a
migration deploy-path hotfix, three post-merge CI fixes, a plain-language rewrite of the user-facing
**What's New** page, and an owner-directed plain-English overhaul of the whole **Manage** admin area
(sidebar reorganisation + relabel, and every page header). Owner-directed **minor version bump**
(0.5050.0 → 0.5100.0) reflecting a smaller but real amount of further work.

- fix(migrations): **resolve shared includes via `IHYMNS_INCLUDES_DIR`, not a hardcoded
  `/public_html/` literal** (#1816, `1c73110f`). The deployed docroot is renamed per channel
  (alpha → `public_html_dev`, beta → `public_html_beta`, main → `public_html`), so six migrations that
  did `require_once dirname(__DIR__).'/public_html/includes/X.php'` fatally failed on alpha/beta — a
  bare "HTTP error" / "Failed opening required …" that stopped the whole *Apply all* batch on
  `/manage/setup-database`. The runner now defines `IHYMNS_INCLUDES_DIR` (= the real docroot's
  `includes`); each migration resolves shared includes through it, with the literal kept only as a
  standalone/CLI repo fallback. `tests/php/test-deploy-paths.php` now scans `.sql/` too and bans the
  column-0 literal-path require (block-comment-stripped, mutation-proven). Documented as CLAUDE.md
  rule #41.

- fix(ci): **three post-merge CI reds cleared** — a `html_sanitizer.php` doc-comment reworded so a
  backticked `<?xml` no longer trips the §9 embedded-PHP grep (#1811, `b31ec51f`); the dead
  `makeCompare` import dropped from `admin-table-sort.js` (ESLint `no-unused-vars`, #1814,
  `7b65d0c4`); and `protobufjs` pinned to `^8.7.2` (matching the ProPresenter static encoder) plus
  `protobufjs-cli` added so `build:proto-static` runs again — fixes the ProPresenter static-CSP byte
  mismatch (#1820, `25efa63f`).

- feat(whats-new): **plain-language user release notes from a dedicated `WHATS-NEW.md`, not the
  developer changelog** (#1818, `5d60c0c1`). The in-app What's New page was rendering raw
  `CHANGELOG.md` — file names, table names, issue numbers and jargon, a genuine information-leak
  concern (owner-reported). `deploy.yml` now prefers `WHATS-NEW.md` (hand-written, "average Joe"
  wording per `.claude/whats-new-style.md`) and falls back to `CHANGELOG.md` only if it is absent;
  `tests/test-whats-new-extraction.js` gained a no-internals scan over the extracted output.

- feat(admin): **plain-English overhaul of the whole Manage admin area** (#1822, `da371f6c` +
  `9035a5a7`). Part 1 — the sidebar was reorganised (live-service surfaces moved out of *People* into
  a new *Live Services* group; new *Songs / Song Library / Access & Permissions / System & Reports*
  groups) and relabelled in plain words (e.g. *Duplicates & Links* → *Find Duplicates*,
  *Entitlements* → *Role Permissions*, *Schema Audit* → *Database Structure Check*, *Gating No-op
  Verify* → *Content Lock Safety Check*), and a blank icon was fixed (`bi-git-compare`, a GitHub
  Octicon, → `bi-files`). All 46 `(id / href / entitlement)` tuples are byte-preserved, so the #1587
  nav-gate parity guard and every deep link still hold. Part 2 — the visible title + intro
  description on 36 jargon-heavy `/manage/*` pages rewritten to plain English (display copy only; no
  logic, gates or structure touched). House style captured in `.claude/admin-plain-english.md`.


- chore(version): **bump 0.5050.0 → 0.5100.0** (owner-directed minor). Lockstep across
  `includes/infoAppVer.php`, `api-docs.yaml`, `manifest.json`, `README.md`, `WHATS-NEW.md`,
  `PROJECT_STATUS.md` and the `.claude/` docs. The `infoAppVer.php` comment now records why alpha
  batches must be hand-bumped: the version-bump Action is configured for alpha but never fires there,
  because auto-merged alpha PRs push via `GITHUB_TOKEN` and GitHub does not re-trigger workflows on a
  `GITHUB_TOKEN` push.

## [0.5050.0] — 2026-08-11 (alpha)

The consolidated `claude/issue-sweep-fixes-89` batch — 214 commits, one PR — landing the #89
issue-sweep alongside several feature epics: the **#1765** songbook/catalogue enhancements (+ **#93**
Publishers registry), the **#1769/#1778** content-gating program, the **#1767** print-templates /
server-PDF remainder, **#94** IA-reconcile Phase 1, **#1770/#1792/#1798** Live-Follow work, **#1791**
set-list share-by-link, **#1786** public multi-level list sort, and the **#1785** musicians dedup
family. Owner-directed **significant minor version bump** (0.4100.0 → 0.5050.0). All new schema ships additive,
dormant, and forward-looking (rule #20); content gating stays entirely dormant behind
`content_gating_enabled='0'`.

- chore(version): **bump 0.4100.0 → 0.5050.0** (#91, owner-directed significant minor bump). Lockstep across
  `includes/infoAppVer.php`, `api-docs.yaml`, `manifest.json`, `README.md`, `PROJECT_STATUS.md` and
  `appWeb/CHANGELOG.md`; the stale "v1.x = local-JSON phase" comment is retired (DB-direct since epic
  #1010). `service-worker.js.php` auto-derives its cache key, so the bump rolls the PWA cache with no
  manual edit.

- feat(songbooks): **songbook/catalogue enhancements epic core — disable flag, public-domain flag,
  ARK/OpenLibrary identifiers, Google Books provider, and MARCXML import/export** (epic #1765; docs
  deferred here to #91). Pure foundations (`b0cdbd27`); the additive publication-metadata migration
  batch — `tblSongbooks.IsDisabled`/`IsPublicDomain`/`OpenLibrary*`, `tblSongbookSeries` +
  identifier columns, `tblCatalogues.ArkId`/`OpenLibrary*` (`d64dd1a7`); a public read sweep hiding
  disabled songbooks behind the shared `includes/songbook_visibility.php` (`songVisibleSql` AND
  `songServableSql`, `ca6d8120`); the admin Add/Edit surfaces + a shared form-fields partial with a
  parity guard (`05026c92`); a Google Books external-link provider (`7992e541`); and MARCXML
  export (`99390d91`) + import (`cbeeeba0`) on songbooks / series / collections via the pure,
  DB-free `includes/marcxml.php`. Adversarial-review fixes (`0ca1a480`, `4b736005`).

- feat(print): **print-templates remainder — schema, HTML/CSS sanitiser, vendored mPDF, server-PDF
  pipeline, Download-PDF affordance, CCLI print-usage, uploadable custom layouts, and batch set-list
  PDF** (#1767 remainder, plan `.claude/print-templates-1767-remainder-plan.md`). P1 schema —
  `tblPrintTemplateCustomLayout` + `tblPrintTemplates.OrgId` (`2ce2daa0`); P2 allowlist HTML/CSS
  sanitiser (`e0b357bd`); mPDF ~8.3 vendored **outside every docroot** (`29ca8a75`) + the block/page
  registry extracted to `includes/print_template_schema.php` (`a99ee6dc`); P3 server-PDF pipeline +
  `manage/print-pdf.php` + the mutation-proven one-renderer / not-public guards (`4a080160`,
  `5402e910`); P4 Download-PDF affordance + serverOnly H-family page options (`e2c884e9`); P5 CCLI
  print-usage — copies prompt + `print_usage_context`/`_log` + enforced footer, licence re-resolved
  server-side (`abd22d79`); P7 uploadable full-page custom HTML layouts (`4bd00097`); P6 batch
  set-list single-PDF — cover page + running order + each song (`ffada413`).

- feat(ia-reconcile): **IA-reconcile Phase 1 — a read-only archive.org OCR audit tool** (#94, plan
  `.claude/ia-ocr-94-plan.md`). Pick a songbook + an archive.org scan, get a report — exact/strong
  matches, the **gap** list (hymns in the scan missing from the book), and songbook songs never found
  in the scan. **Never writes song content** (owner decision D1); gated by `edit_songs` (owner
  decision D2). SSRF-hardened, host-bound `includes/ia_client.php` (`e33f09fd`); the audit-bookkeeping
  migration `tblIaFetchCache` + `tblIaImportCandidates` (`9ce5be66`); the pure OCR segmenter/scorer +
  persistence (`7fdaab53`); the admin page + nav entry (`0aff2d15`); a mutation-proven CI guard
  (`2418eac7`).

- feat(setlist): **set-list share-by-link — the SERVER half of #1791** (client half is the separate
  entry below). Dormant capability columns on `tblSharedSetlists` (`Scope`/`EditAudience`/
  `ShowSharerName`/`RevokedAt`/…, `7679d33d`); the share-id fold + scope-aware resolver + write core
  (`dc3ea408`); the edit-link mint + `setlist_token_update`/`_share_list`/`_share_revoke` endpoints
  (`c5070055`); and the audience-resolution wiring incl. the org-policy clamp
  (`e1f4fb8f`/`341bb4be`/`4f7f2522`/`0a9fbb19`). An edit link's power lives in the row, not the URL;
  the server re-resolves the edit audience on every write (see CLAUDE.md rule #40).

- feat(gating): **#1769 P0/P1 — the dormant foundation the P2–P5 refactor builds on** (branch
  `claude/gating-model-review`). P0 hardened the existing restriction engine (content-restriction
  deny beats allow at equal priority, `2e9aa850`, among others); P1 landed the additive, dormant
  schema batch — the `tblLicenceTypes` registry + per-song/songbook rights-fact columns +
  `tblGatingCapabilities.EnforceJson` (`70739838`). Everything stays a verified no-op until the P6
  master switch (`content_gating_enabled='1'`).

- feat(public-sort): **user-controllable multi-level sort across every public card/list surface, synced to the account** (#1786 Option B — the public half; the admin `<table>` half shipped separately). Ten commits, one PR:
  - **The shared core, extracted not forked** (rule #22) — `js/utils/sort-compare.js` is a new pure, DOM-free module holding `makeCompare()`/`multiKeyCompare()` moved VERBATIM out of `admin-table-sort.js` (behaviour-neutral; `tests/test-admin-table-sort.js` stays green unmodified), plus the new `multiKeyCompareMissingLast()` (a level with no value sorts after every present value, in BOTH directions — the "unnumbered Misc tail stays a tail under any sort" requirement), `titleSortKey()`/`SORT_ARTICLES` (the JS mirror of `ihymns_title_sort_key()`, kept in lockstep with the PHP article vocabulary by a parity guard, not a comment), and `normalizeSortSpec()` (validate/cap-at-3/dedupe a persisted spec against the keys a control currently offers — "a saved layout is a wish, not a contract").
  - **The control** — a card-friendly "Sort ▾" dropdown (≤ 3 level rows: key `<select>` with already-used keys disabled, a direction toggle, remove, "+ then by…", "Reset to default"; ①②③ badges mirror the admin module's own glyphs), emitted identically for every viewer by a new server partial (`includes/partials/list-sort-control.php`, shared-cache-safe per rule #6 — NO inline `<script>`, rule #30) and wired by a new `js/modules/list-sort.js` imported unconditionally from `router.js`'s `afterPageLoad()`. Announces every change via the shared `announce()` (WCAG 4.1.3).
  - **Two adoption modes** — DOM-reorder for server-rendered pages (`data-list-sort-list` container(s) + `data-sort-<key>` item attributes; sorts ALL direct children including hidden ones, one `DocumentFragment` re-append per apply) and array/server mode for JS-rendered/paginated pages (`wireListSortControl()`, called by the page's own module). Ten surfaces adopted: `/songbooks` (name/abbreviation/song count), `/songbook/<abbr>` (number/title/writer — absorbing `songbook-index.js`'s old single-level, unpersisted, unannounced "# Number / A-Z Title" toggle, a second competing sort authority the modularity rule exists to stop), `/tag`, `/musician`, `/tune`, `/identifier` (multi-container, per-group), `/publisher`, `/work` (Default = "Work order" — a work's own curated member order is meaningful, unlike every other surface's arbitrary server default), `/favorites` (array mode), and `/search` (server-sort mode — pagination makes a client DOM-reorder dishonest, so sorting is a new `sort=key.dir,…` param `SongData::_searchOrderBy()` maps to HARDCODED `ORDER BY` fragments only, rule #5). The home songbook grid is deliberately excluded (⚑ N6 — duplicates `/songbooks` one tap away, already carries the #448 customise toolbar).
  - **Persistence** rides the EXISTING namespaced `user_settings` endpoint (#1671 F5), namespace `list_sorts` — no new endpoint, no schema, no migration. Anonymous/offline is device-`localStorage`-only; signed-in additionally syncs across devices (account wins per surface on read, write-through of the full map on change, `validateCsrfRequest()` added to the namespaced POST branch — the `live_follow_extend` precedent).
  - New tree-derived, mutation-proven guards: `tests/php/test-public-list-sort.php` (every candidate page's control markup actually matches its `data-sort-*` item attributes — the mechanism against a typo'd key silently making every row tie) and `tests/test-list-sort.js` (comparator units, the PHP⇄JS article-vocabulary parity check, wiring, the account-sync literal contract).
  - Follow-ups filed rather than silently absorbed: `settings.js`'s legacy whole-blob `user_settings` push still clobbers every namespace including `list_sorts` on its next save (a known, pre-existing exposure `cardLayouts` already carries); a `TitleSortKey` generated column so search's SQL `ORDER BY s.Title` becomes article-aware like every other surface; optional home-grid adoption (⚑ N6).

- feat(setlist): **set-list collaboration via share-link — the CLIENT half of #1791** (server landed ahead in an earlier commit on this branch). The owner's bare share-toast is now a Share dialog (`renderShareDialog()`, setlist.js) offering THREE distinct ways to let someone else see or work with a setlist: a **view link** (unchanged, anyone can open, read-only), a new **edit link** (anyone with the link — or, per link, people signed in to iHymns — can reorder/remove songs with no account of their own) with an "Active links" list + per-link **Revoke**, and the pre-existing email-invite Collaborators panel. Minting an edit link sends the owner's `editAudience`/`showSharerName` choice but reads back what the server actually STORED (rule #35) — an organisation may clamp a requested "anyone" edit link to "signed-in required", and the dialog explains that rather than assuming its own request was honoured. On the public `/setlist/shared/<id>` page, when `setlist_get` reports `canWrite:true` the song list becomes an editable staged-copy surface (reorder/remove, optimistic UI, aria-live save status, revert-on-refusal) pushing to `setlist_token_update`; a `401 {reason:'signin_required'}` downgrades it to a sign-in prompt (reusing the app's existing sign-in modal) instead of a generic error. The move/remove row markup + wiring is ONE shared pair of helpers (`sharedSetlistRowsHtml()` / `bindSharedSetlistRowControls()`) consumed by both this new surface and the pre-existing email-collaborator detail view — not a second fork. Org admins get a new "Set-list edit links" preference on `/manage/my-organisations` (No preference / Default to signed-in / Require signed-in), modelled on the #1770 idle-timeout control's shape and CSRF path. New mutation-proven CI guard `tests/test-setlist-share-client.js` pins the 401/signin_required contract, the mint-response read-back, `apiFetch` usage on the three new endpoints, and the shared row-helper consumption. `setlist_token_update`/`setlist_share_list`/`setlist_share_revoke` — allowlisted as server-ahead-of-client orphans since the earlier server commit — now have real callers and were removed from `tests/php/fixtures/orphan-allowlist.php`. Add-song on the edit surfaces (plan §3g.3) remains deferred, as the plan explicitly allows.

- feat(musicians): **merge/dedup follow-ups — a third credit-table list collapsed, empty survivor fields backfilled on merge, and a create-time duplicate hint** (#1800, follow-on to #1785/#1796). Three of the four items scoped; the fourth assessed and deferred (see below):
  - **`credit_search`'s third table list, collapsed.** `manage/editor/api2.php`'s `credit_search` autocomplete carried its own hand-typed six-table `$kindToTable` map, a THIRD copy of the same fact alongside `ED2_CREDIT_TABLES` (same file) and `MUSICIAN_CREDIT_ROLE_TABLES` (`includes/musician_helpers.php`) — flagged as "discovered, not fixed" in `#1785`'s own guard doc-block. Now reads `MUSICIAN_CREDIT_ROLE_TABLES` directly (its singular keys already match this endpoint's `kind=` convention, so no transform was needed). `tests/php/test-musician-credit-tables-single-list.php` gained a PART C that isolates the `credit_search` case block by source position and asserts no bare table-name literal remains and the shared constant IS referenced — mutation-proven both directions.
  - **COALESCE-fill on merge.** `musicianMergeExecute()` used to silently drop every biographical fact recorded ONLY on the losing side of a merge. It now backfills the survivor's EMPTY `Biography` / `MusicBrainzArtistMBID` / `Disambiguation` / `BirthDate`+`DeathDate` (with precision) from the source before deleting it — never overwriting a non-empty target value. New `musicianMbidColumnExists()` gate joins the existing `musicianProfileColumnsExist()`/`musicianDatePrecisionColumnsExist()` probes (rule #9). The fill runs AFTER the source row's `DELETE`, inside the same transaction the function already owns — `MusicBrainzArtistMBID` carries a UNIQUE key, so filling the target before the source is gone would collide with the source's own still-live value. Identity fields (First/Surname/Suffix/Maiden) are deliberately excluded; ISNI/IPI aren't single columns on this table (multi-row `tblMusicianIdentifiers` children, already covered by the existing `keepIpiIds` carry-over). The report gains an additive `fieldsFilled` key, surfaced in all three call sites' activity logs plus `manage/musicians.php`'s success banner and `api.php`'s JSON response. Runtime-verified against the live scratch DB (17 checks, three disposable-row scenarios, cleaned up with no residue) — `musicianMergeExecute()` owns its own transaction, so this couldn't be wrapped in an outer rollback; verification used explicit cleanup `DELETE`s instead.
  - **Create-time fold-match hint.** The `/manage/musicians` "Add person" drawer's Name field now debounce-checks a new `musicianFindFoldMatches()` helper (reusing the SAME `ihymns_sim_name_normalise()` exact-fold test the duplicate-review page's Bucket A already uses, rule #22) via a new read-only `?action=name_fold_check` endpoint. A soft "possible duplicate of X (view)" hint appears — linking to the matched person's public `/musician/<slug>` page — but never blocks Save. Only wired while the field is editable (Edit mode locks the Name input; renames go through the separate Rename action). New guard `tests/php/test-musician-name-fold-check.php`: source-scan (route wired, hint element rendered) always runs; a behavioural part (exact variant found, unrelated name rejected, `exclude_id` honoured, blank/punctuation-only input never throws) runs against a reachable DB inside a rolled-back transaction. Mutation-proven in both directions.
  - **Deferred: unifying `/manage/musician-duplicates` with `/manage/musicians-bulk-promote`.** Assessed against the `#1215` (`duplicate-songs` absorbing `song-link-suggestions`) precedent and judged NOT a clean, contained merge: the two pages work over fundamentally different candidate universes — bulk-promote finds song-credit names with NO registry row yet (register/merge/skip against an in-use NAME); musician-duplicates finds pairs of EXISTING registry rows that look like the same person (merge/dismiss against a registry ID pair) — a distinction the pages' own code comments already draw explicitly. Unlike `song-link-suggestions`, which was the SAME "compare two songs" concern with a different source, there's no natural absorption here; forcing one would mean reconciling two action vocabularies across ~1,650 combined lines of actively-used bulk POST logic, for a real regression risk. Shipped items above instead; a real unification would need a design pass on the combined candidate/action model before any code moves — a separate, scoped piece of work.

- feat(live-follow): **declare a Quick session's length at "Go Live", and extend a running one mid-service** (#1798, follow-on to #1770/#1792). Today a Quick session auto-closes after a fixed idle window — right for an ad-hoc rehearsal, wrong for a leader covering a whole service including the sermon (a genuinely quiet stretch that would trip the auto-close). Two additions, Service Mode untouched (it's bounded by the scheduled service end, never idle):
  - **Session length at create.** `goLive()` now offers a lightweight duration picker — 30 min / 1 hour / 2 hours / "until I end it" (240, the existing 4-hour hard ceiling) — reusing the established `bootstrap.Modal` pattern already used directly by several `js/modules/*` files (never `app.js`'s `showChoice()`, which only ever offers two options). `live_follow_create` reads the optional `idleTimeoutMins`, clamps it to [5, 240], and — the load-bearing rule — caps it at the host's own ENFORCING org lock when one applies (the org's lock always wins for the host, even when they ask for longer). `serviceMode_resolveIdleTimeoutMins()` grew an additive by-reference out-param (`&$enforcedMinsOut`, default null) that exposes just the enforced layer it already computes internally, so create/extend can each apply the same cap without a second reader of `LiveIdleTimeoutMins`/`EnforceIdleTimeout` (the A3 guard in `test-live-follow-idle.php` polices that single-reader invariant).
  - **New endpoint `live_follow_extend`.** Widens a RUNNING session's window and resets the idle clock to now, so the full chosen window applies from the moment of extending — the fix for "the leader's phone died during the sermon". Authorised for the session's own HOST, an admin/owner of an organisation the HOST belongs to, or a site admin/global_admin — resolved via the HOST's OWN `tblOrganisationMembers` rows, **never the session's own `OrgId`** (Quick sessions keep `OrgId = NULL`, load-bearing for CCLI gating, rule #26). The authorisation DECISION is extracted into `serviceMode_liveFollowExtendAuthorize()` — a real, independently-testable function behind the otherwise-inline dispatcher case, mirroring #1792's `serviceMode_codeOnOtherChannel()` "testable primitive behind a thin case" shape. A HOST extending is capped at their own org's enforced lock (same as create); an org-admin/site-admin extending on the host's behalf is capped only at the global [5, 240] band — they hold the authority to override it. Existence-gated on the #1770 idle-timeout migration (409, not 404/400, so the client can tell "not migrated yet" apart from "wrong code", rule #35); channel-scoped like every other live/service query (rule #26); `validateCsrfRequest()` belt-and-braces (rule #29); rate-limited 60/hour/user (rule #1636 two-call contract). The host bar gained an **Extend** button (same picker, POSTs the new action, toasts "Kept live for …", branches on `409` for "not migrated" vs the generic failure toast).
  - **Org-admin surface.** `/manage/my-organisations` gained a "Members' live sessions" card — active Quick sessions whose host is a member of an org the viewer administers, each with an Extend control hitting the same endpoint — for exactly the dead-phone-mid-sermon case, without needing the leader's own device. Column-existence-gated on `serviceMode_idleColumnsExist()`; hidden entirely pre-migration or with nothing to show.
  - New guard `tests/php/test-live-follow-extend.php` (Part A static: the endpoint's real source contains the channel-scoped resolve, the authorize() call, the [5,240] clamp constants, the idle-columns 409 gate, CSRF + rate-limit pairing; Part B live-DB behavioural: host/org-admin/unrelated-user authorization outcomes via the REAL `serviceMode_liveFollowExtendAuthorize()`, the create-override + enforced-cap formula via the REAL resolver's out-param, out-of-range clamping, and channel scoping — a code belonging only to another channel's session resolves to nothing from this one). Mutation-proven: short-circuiting the org-membership check inside the real authorizer flips the "unrelated user" assertion red (a genuine behavioural catch, not a parallel copy); removing the clamp constants from the real `live_follow_extend` case body flips the static A2 check red. PHP 143→144 / node 53 (unchanged).
  - Docs: `api-docs.yaml` (`live_follow_extend` path + `live_follow_create`'s new `idleTimeoutMins` request field), `help/live-follow.md` + the in-app help topic (the "sermon gap" callout + the new "hasn't been updated for session lengths yet" troubleshooting row).

- feat(admin): **sortable-headers adoption sweep — every admin data-list table, every column, actually wired** (#1786 sweep, closes #1799). The shared multi-column sortable-table module (`js/modules/admin-table-sort.js`, #1786 core) previously reached ~24 admin pages, and even those only keyed some of their columns. This pass:
  - Found and fixed a silent-no-op class of bug on **twelve** already-`cp-sortable`-tagged pages (activity-log, ccli-report, data-health, languages, missing-numbers, notifications, publishers, requests, revisions, tags, tunes, works) that never loaded the module at all — every header click did nothing, with no visible symptom. Same shape as #1799, found on more pages by deriving the check from the tree instead of trusting the existing tag.
  - Completed per-column coverage on four partially-wired tables (feature-gating.php x2, groups.php, licence-types.php, musician-duplicates.php) and fully wired five previously-unsortable pages (api-keys.php, catalogues.php, entitlements.php, my-organisations.php, venues.php) plus two orphaned tables (organisations.php's edit-view Members list, tags.php's canonicalisation-suggestions list).
  - **#1799**: `musicians.php` and `musicians-bulk-promote.php` tagged their table `mus-sortable`, which the module's default selector never matches; `musicians.php` called `bootSortableTables()` into the void, `musicians-bulk-promote.php` never called it at all. Both retagged to `cp-sortable` and fully keyed. Also fixed `musicians.php`'s row id, which read the always-undefined `$p['Id']` instead of `$p['registry_id']` — every row silently collided on `id="mus-person-0"`.
  - New tree-derived, mutation-proven guard: `tests/php/test-admin-tables-sortable.php` (rule #34) — derives its candidate page list from `manage/*.php` minus a documented exclusion allow-list (status/report/dashboard pages), and fails if a table isn't `cp-sortable`, the page doesn't boot the module, or a data column lacks `data-sort-key` outside a small documented exception set.
  - Scope was admin-only by design — the public app has no data `<table>`s (its lists are card/list layouts).

- feat(musicians): **registry-vs-registry duplicate detection, a new review page, and disambiguated merge affordances everywhere** (#1785, follow-up to #1784, under epic #1787). Ten commits, one PR:
  - **The scan** — `includes/musician_duplicates.php`'s `musicianFindRegistryDuplicates()` finds registry rows that are probably the same person, live-computed on every page load (no precompute table — the registry is two orders of magnitude lighter than the song corpus, so `tblSongLinkSuggestions`'s staleness trade-off isn't worth paying here). Blocking (an exact fold, or a metaphone of the first/last name token) keeps this sub-second even at thousands of rows. Three buckets: **A** — fold-equal ("Eddie James" vs "Eddie  James") — high confidence, no scoring; **B** — fuzzy candidates scored with the shared `ihymns_sim_name_score()`; **C** — a registry row whose fold matches ANOTHER row's curated alias. No silent caps (rule #35) — an over-cap block is skipped and the skip is reported, never swallowed.
  - **The review page** — `/manage/musician-duplicates`, the musician-registry sibling of `/manage/duplicate-songs` (#1215): byte-variant groups as cards, fuzzy pairs as a sortable table, one-click Merge (delegating to the ONE shared `musicianMergeExecute()` core, never re-implemented), Dismiss/Undismiss (`tblMusicianDuplicatesDismissed`), a `force=1` + type-to-confirm guard on any pair whose recorded lifespans disagree (the musician-registry analogue of #1218's same-official-songbook guard), and keyboard navigation (j/k/Enter/d/s, `?` for a shortcut legend). CTAs on `/manage/musicians` and `/manage/musicians-bulk-promote` point at it.
  - **"Which is merging into which?"** — the confusing "Eddie James → Eddie James" merge prompt now shows WHY the two look alike (a byte-variant badge: whitespace / punctuation / case / Unicode-form) and each side's id, lifespan, type and credit count. Landed in the merge-target typeahead, the `/manage/musicians` Merge modal (whose credit-preview widget also now shows all SIX roles, not five — `total` already included artist credits, the five visible pills didn't sum to it), and `/manage/musicians-bulk-promote`'s match options.
  - **The shared scorer, extended not forked** (rule #22) — `includes/song_similarity.php` gained a NAME-similarity section (`ihymns_sim_name_score()` etc.) built on the same primitives the song-duplicate scorer uses; the private fork this replaced (`manage/musicians-bulk-promote.php`'s `_musBulkSimilarity()`) is deleted.
  - **The merge core, extracted then hardened** — `musicianMergeExecute()` (`includes/musician_helpers.php`) is now the ONE place a musician merge happens; both pre-existing surfaces (`/manage/musicians`'s modal, the admin API) delegate to it. Hardening closed two live defects found during the extraction: a merge silently stranded `tblSongArtists` credits (the editor already promotes artist names into the registry, but every registry-side cascade/count was missing the sixth table — `MUSICIAN_CREDIT_ROLE_TABLES` is now the ONE list, re-pointing nine forked five-table copies across the app), and a merge silently cascade-deleted the source's aliases and relations with no way to recover them (both now carry onto the target; the source's own spelling survives as a target alias).
  - One migration (`tblMusicianDuplicatesDismissed`, mirrors `tblSongLinkSuggestionsDismissed`'s shape). Six mutation-proven guards (a pure name-similarity/classifier fixture matrix; the shared-scorer allow-list; the six-credit-table single-list check, extended in this pass to cover the two new files; the scan's no-silent-caps + Disambiguation-exclusion behaviour; the merge-core singleness check, keyed per-statement-shape so a missing DELETE can't hide behind a still-present UPDATE).

- feat(live-follow): **Go Live gets a persistent host bar, leader-idle auto-close, a host-CCLI unlock for followers, and an external presentation-app driver** (#1770, owner-directed Option A rework). Seven requirements, delivered as one PR (C1-C8):
  - **Persistence made visible (req #1)** — a fixed red **LIVE** bar now stays pinned to the bottom of every page while hosting (not just the song page), carrying **Show code** (a large code + QR, `/qr.php`-backed per rule #38), **Console**, and **End**.
  - **Leader-idle auto-close (req #2/#5)** — a Quick session now closes itself after a stretch with NO genuine leader interaction (the automated 30s keepalive alone never counts, only a real pointerdown/keydown, a broadcast, or session create). A three-layer precedence chain (app default → org override/lock → user preference, `enforced ?? (user ?? (orgDefault ?? appDefault))`, clamped 5-240 min) is resolved once and stamped on the session at create. New settings surfaces: `/manage/configuration` (app default), `/manage/organisations` + `/manage/my-organisations` (org override + lock), `/settings` (personal preference).
  - **Host-CCLI unlock for Quick followers (req #3/#6)** — joining a Quick session can now additionally mint a `tblServicePresence` row (an additive POST mode on `live_follow_join`, GET stays byte-identical), letting followers read gated lyrics riding the HOST's own CCLI licence for the life of the session — the same mechanism Service Mode congregants already use, entirely dormant behind `content_gating_enabled='0'` + `require_licence:ccli` rows.
  - **External presentation-app driver (req #4/#7)** — a new `service_drive` API action (key-authed via durable, org-scoped `tblServiceDriverKeys` credentials) lets a ProPresenter-class shim / Companion webhook / curl loop drive a church's Service Mode session, writing through the SAME `serviceMode_applyBroadcast()` core `service_broadcast` uses. Free-text section labels ("Verse 2", "Chorus") resolve against the song's render-order arrangement; unresolvable falls back to song-level. An org-admin mints/lists/revokes keys from a new "Presentation-app control" card on `/manage/service-projection`.
  - **`?svc_code=` deep link now actually works (rule #33)** — the Service-Projection join QR has emitted this param since #1339 with nothing reading it back; `service-follow.js` now reads it on every boot and opens a one-tap confirm prompt (never an automatic join).
  - **Optional drive-sections console for Quick hosts (req #6)** — reuses the SAME `ServiceBroadcaster` module Service Mode's operator console uses via a transport adapter, never a fork.
  - One migration (`migrate-live-follow-quick-capable.php`: `tblLiveFollowSessions` idle columns, `tblOrganisations` override columns, the new `tblServiceDriverKeys` table), fully dormant/additive and column-existence-gated throughout. Six new mutation-proven guards (channel-filter presence across the walled tables; the idle predicate's single-source-of-truth + a live-DB resolver-precedence matrix; the host-CCLI branch's reachability + `OrgId`-NULL invariant; the one-broadcaster-core; rate-limit keying for the two new endpoints; the `svc_code` emitter/reader contract). Docs: `api-docs.yaml`, `help/live-follow.md` + the in-app help topic, the wiki. The live two-device verify (#1339/#1792) remains the one genuinely outstanding item — it needs two real devices on one channel and has never once been run.

- fix(live-follow): **joining with a code from a *different* iHymns environment now says so, instead of a dead-end "wrong code"** (#1792). The instinctive test of Go Live / Join Live — start the session on a laptop's dev/preview URL, join on a phone's live PWA — is exactly the cross-channel case the rule-#26 Channel wall refuses (the three docroots share one MySQL but each only sees its own live sessions), and the generic *"Session not found"* / *"That code has expired"* toast made it read as "the feature is broken". Both `service_join` and `live_follow_join` now, when their channel-scoped resolve misses, call a new **`serviceMode_codeOnOtherChannel()`** existence probe (covering both the Quick `SessionCode` and Service rotating-code families with the same active+fresh predicates the real joins use, rule #22 — an existence check, not a fork of the resolver's disambiguation) and, on a hit, return the ONE shared **`SERVICE_MODE_WRONG_CHANNEL_MESSAGE`** — *"That code belongs to a different iHymns environment … make sure both devices are using the same web address."* This does **not** weaken the anti-probe opacity (rule #26 I6): it fires only for a code the caller already holds validly *somewhere else*, so it reveals nothing about a current-channel session's liveness, grants no access, names no specific environment, and rides the same per-IP join rate limit (same 404 status; only the message differs, rule #35). Docs put the same-address testing procedure front-and-centre (`help/live-follow.md` callout + the in-app help topic + the wiki). A signed-in cross-channel *bridge* (deliberately driving a session across environments) was considered and **not** built — it would poke a hole in the wall that exists to stop prod-stale leakage; the same-address procedure + the clearer error solve the actual testing need (surfaced as a non-blocking owner decision on the issue). Guard `tests/php/test-live-follow-cross-channel.php` (tree-derived + live-DB behavioural, mutation-proven: helper `<>`→`=` reddens the Channel-semantics checks; dropping the call from either endpoint reddens the wiring checks). PHP suite 135→136. The live two-device verify (#1339) still needs two real devices on one channel.
- fix(live-follow): **a Quick "Go Live" code now stays valid for as long as the session is alive** (#1792, owner direction — the Kahoot/Mentimeter model). The Live Follow code is *fixed* (never rotates); the only thing limiting it was the join/poll gate requiring a host heartbeat within 180s, a crude host-alive proxy from before #1770's idle-auto-close existed — and it silently locked followers out whenever the leader's phone backgrounded the tab (mobile browsers pause timers). `live_follow_join` + `live_follow_poll` now gate on the new **`serviceMode_liveFollowAliveSql()`**: on a migrated install the session is "alive" as long as the leader hasn't gone idle past the **configurable** `IdleTimeoutMins` (default 15 min, an org can set it to hours), so a backgrounded host phone no longer blocks joins and a new follower can join at *any* point while the session runs; un-migrated installs keep the 180s heartbeat fallback (no idle-close there to bound abandonment). The idle predicate stays single-sourced through `serviceMode_idleFreshSql()` (rule #26 — the wrapper is a thin regime-selector; the G2 guard now asserts join/poll route through it and that it applies the idle predicate). Runtime-verified: a 10-min-stale-heartbeat + idle-fresh session is joinable, an idle-expired one is not. Service Mode's rotating venue code is deliberately unchanged (proof-of-presence for the CCLI unlock, not a fixed code). Supersedes the earlier "widen 180→600s" idea. PHP 143 / node 53.

- feat(gating): **new `/manage/gating` hub — the content-gating family in one place, with an activation-readiness checklist** (#1778 / #1769 P6). Gathers Content Restrictions, Access Tiers, Licence Types, Feature Gating and Entitlements behind one overview, shows the master-switch state (read-only — the flip stays on Configuration, one write path), and runs a readiness checklist (schema present, tier-caps JSON column present, no-op verified, and the six owner decisions #1772–#1777 as attestation rows). The actual enforcement flip and those decisions remain a deliberate owner-only action, exactly as the program requires — the hub never turns anything on. `manage_configuration`-gated (admin-gate-parity green).
- feat(print): **print templates can now be cloned, exported/imported as JSON, and set as the system default** (#1767 Z + J). On `/manage/print-templates`: a per-row **Clone** (duplicates the layout into a fresh row), **Export** (downloads `{name, blocks, pageOptions}` as a JSON file), a collapsed **Import** form (paste that JSON back — it goes through the SAME block/page-option sanitiser as a normal save, so an unknown block type or option is dropped rather than trusted), and **Set default** (enforces the single-default invariant in a transaction and forces the chosen template active). No schema — reuses `tblPrintTemplates`. Verified against a live DB (clone independence, single-default invariant, export/import round-trip). The remaining #1767 scope (multi-page running headers, uploadable layouts, server-PDF, org branding, CCLI print-usage) stays batched on four owner decisions on the issue.

- fix(setlist): **a shared set-list link is now playlist-first — tap a song (or "Start") and Prev/Next just follow, no Import, no "Use"** (#1790). The owner reported the recipient of a shared link had to (1) Import → (2) click "Use" → (3) tap a song before the Prev/Next bar appeared. Investigation (Fable deep-plan, `.claude/setlist-sharing-1790-1791-plan.md`) found the behaviour was **already built** — since #1533 the shared page arms the playback context on tap with no sign-in and no local write (`setlist.js initSharedSetListPage`) — but the page's own copy actively steered users the wrong way: *"Someone shared this set list with you. Import it to use it."* plus **two** prominent "Import to My Set Lists" buttons and **no play affordance at all**, so nobody discovered the tap-to-play that already worked. Fixed as pure client presentation (no schema, no server): the banner now reads *"Tap any song to start — Prev/Next will follow this list. No sign-in needed."*, a primary **"▶ Start set list"** button arms the context and jumps to song 1 (extracted so tap and Start arm identically — modularity rule), and Import is demoted to a single secondary **"Save a copy to My Set Lists"**. The `test-dom-target-integrity` guard caught two now-dangling id lookups during the change (the removed top button) — both cleaned up. node 50/50. The bigger **collab-by-link** rework (#1791 — an edit-scope capability token in the same `tblSharedSetlists`, no email invite/account) is the planned follow-on (schema+server+client), with owner-decision gates G1–G4 pending.
- fix(export): **ProPresenter 7+ export works again — CSP-safe protobuf encoding** (#1788). Every PP7 export (single song AND whole songbook) died with *"Evaluating a string as JavaScript violates … 'unsafe-eval'"*. Cause: `.pro` files are binary protobuf, and the exporter loaded the schema as a **reflection descriptor** (`protobuf.Root.fromJSON`) whose encoder protobufjs builds lazily via `new Function()` on first `.encode()` — refused by the enforcing nonce CSP (#117; no `'unsafe-eval'`). Only PP7 hit it (every other format is plain text/XML). Fixed with protobufjs's own answer for eval-disallowed environments: a precompiled **static** encoder (`pbjs -t static`, new `protos/pp7-proto-static.js` + `tools/build-proto-static.js`) that writes the encode path out longhand against the hand-written `protobuf.Writer` — **zero `new Function`/eval**. `propresenter-export.js` now prefers that static tree (reflection kept only as a Node-test fallback); the three browser surfaces (v1 editor, v2 editor, public `export-ui.js`) load it between the runtime and the exporter. **Proven** by `tests/test-propresenter-static-csp.js`: the static encoder's wire bytes are **byte-identical** to the reflection encoder's for the same payload (so the file ProPresenter opens is unchanged), it round-trips through the decoder, the artifact contains no eval, and a static `.encode()` still runs with `Function`/`eval` poisoned (CSP simulation) — all mutation-proven (bump a wire tag → red). NOT done by adding `'unsafe-eval'` to the CSP (that would gut the app-wide script-injection defence for one format). The `protobufjs-cli` needed to regenerate is a documented one-off install, deliberately not a committed devDep (the generated artifact is committed; runtime + CI never need the CLI). PHP 130/130, node 50/50.
- fix(setlist): **a printed set list no longer includes the song page's screen-only chrome** (#1789, interim). `SetList.printSetList()` injects each song's full `?page=song` fragment into the print document, which carried the "Report a missing song" link + "Suggest a correction" form (and media/external-link chrome) into the printout — messy. The feedback block is now tagged `song-page-feedback d-print-none` in `song.php`, and the set-list print stylesheet (which opens its own Bootstrap-less window) re-declares `.d-print-none` plus the block's class/id, so anything the app marks screen-only is honoured on the printed set list too. The **full** remedy — rendering each song through the #1767 print-template engine so the set-list Print also offers/honours a chosen template (it currently offers none) — is tracked under #1767.
- fix(musicians): **the "1 person is credited but isn't saved yet" counter can no longer get stuck, and the confusing "merge X into X" is fixable** (#1784). Root cause (the weeks-old "Eddie James" case): a song-credit row and its registry row hold the SAME name spelled with different **invisible bytes** — a trailing space, an NBSP. The Musicians list links a credit name to its registry row by **exact byte match**, so it never linked those two and the counter stuck on 1 forever; the fuzzy matcher, which folds the invisible difference, then showed the nonsensical "Merge Eddie James → Eddie James". And the merge button couldn't fix it either: it `trim()`med the candidate, saw it now equalled the target, and **skipped as "nothing to do"** — so the credit row kept its stray bytes. New credits never hit this (every write path trims the name before both the credit-table insert and the registry upsert), so it is purely a **legacy-data** clean-up. Fixed three ways: **(1)** a shared reconciliation core `musicianReconcileCreditNameBytes()` + `migrate-reconcile-credit-name-bytes.php` (Apply-all card + drift-detecting probe) that rewrites every legacy credit row to its registry row's exact spelling (or normalises + auto-registers a name with no registry row at all) — **identity-preserving**, so it can never merge two DISTINCT people (a genuine registry duplicate is reported, not touched); **(2)** the "Add all N now" button now runs the same reconciliation inside its transaction, so it actually drives the counter to zero; **(3)** the fuzzy-merge branch re-points on the collation (`Name = ?` with `BINARY Name <> BINARY ?` to skip already-correct rows) so a stray-byte variant IS caught instead of silently skipped. The register-list query is aligned to the same **byte-exact** definition the banner uses (rule #35 — they can never diverge again). **Verified** end-to-end against a live DB: an injected trailing-space credit row makes the probe pending, reconciliation rewrites it (adopt) + auto-registers the pre-existing unregistered names (register), the probe clears, and a second run is a clean 0/0 no-op; migration-registry + schema-coverage + component-json guards all green. Also **terminology**: the live page still said "Credit People" while the sidebar (correctly, since #1741 P2-B) said "Musicians" — the stale display labels are swept to **Musicians** across `musicians.php`, `musicians-bulk-promote.php` (now "Add Musicians in Bulk") and the songbook editor's person picker, in plainer language that drops the "registry"/"promote" jargon (internal identifiers — `tblMusicians`, `manage_musicians`, `/manage/musicians`, the migration *card* titles — stay unchanged, same UI-copy-only pattern as Catalogue→Collection rule #24). Registry-vs-registry duplicate detection, an easier merge UX that disambiguates same-looking names, and app-wide multi-column table sorting are the larger follow-ups (#1785 / #1786, under epic #1787), planned separately.
- feat(editor): **duplicate a song as a starting point for a new songbook** (#1783). A new **Duplicate** button (and a `?duplicate=<sourceId>` deep-link) copies an existing song — lyrics, chords, credits, tags, external links, arrangement, key, alt titles, metadata — into a hidden staging book and opens it in the editor with the **Songbook and Song number fields empty**; picking a songbook assigns it (the existing #1679 re-key) and it becomes a brand-new song. Reuses the revision-restore copy engine (`ed2_applySongSnapshot`, no second copy loop). Resets on the copy: Number, Verified, recording-level Isrc, media flags (media isn't copied); fresh PublicId; a `'duplicate'` revision. The copy also re-anchors **per-line enrichment** — translations/romanizations (`tblLyricLineTranslations`), Genius-style annotations (`tblLyricLineAnnotations`) and scripture refs (`tblSongScriptureRefs`) — onto the NEW song's lines via a positional source→new line map (the three tables anchor on `tblLyricLines.Id`, rule #21, so a naive `INSERT…SELECT` would point them at the source), best-effort so an un-migrated optional table never aborts the duplicate. **Verified end-to-end against a live DB** (components/lines/credits/tags copied, resets correct, `load_song` reports the empty-fields state; a separate fixture run confirms translations/annotations/scripture land on the new line ids with `EndLineId`/whole-song-NULL remapped correctly). Guarded by `tests/test-editor-duplicate-contract.js` (client↔server↔shell contract + no client-side `'PENDING'` literal) and `tests/php/test-duplicate-copy-set.php` (copy-set: resets, one apply path, enrichment re-anchored, no move/personal-state leak) — both tree-derived and mutation-proven (rule #34), plus an explicit `?duplicate=` shell-read assertion in `test-editor-deep-links.js`. node 52/52, PHP 132/132. Deferred (for consideration, non-blocking): per-line presenter `Note` carry, a `/manage/duplicate-songs` `?duplicate=` emitter, a `songRelocate()` no-redirect flag for staging-origin moves, and D5 counterpart auto-link (best done at assign-time, needs a source marker).
- feat(song): **inline chord charts + transpose now work on the song page** (#299). The `#btn-toggle-chords` button shipped hidden with zero JS, and `transpose.js` gated its widget on `[data-chord]` elements nothing emitted — so chords only ever appeared in print (#1767 R). New shared renderer `includes/chord_display.php` turns a component chord line into per-chord `<span data-chord>` tokens (the unit `transpose.js` rewrites) with whitespace preserved for positioning; `song.php` renders a hidden `.lyric-chords` row above each lyric line only when `$songHasChords` (song-level, cacheable-safe — rule #6), shows the toggle, and `transpose.js::bindChordToggle()` reveals them via `.chords-visible`. Guard `test-chord-display.php` (mutation-proven).
- feat(song): **song tags now render on the song page** (#288). `#song-tags-container` shipped hidden with no JS, so tags rode `song_detail` and were discarded. Tags are song-level, so the shared-cache fragment now renders them server-side as chips linking to `/tag/<slug>` (rule #33); the block is omitted entirely when a song has no tags.
- feat(setlist): **"Save as PDF" on the set-list detail view** (#302). The dead, ambiguous page-level `#btn-export-pdf` (hidden, no JS) is replaced by a discoverable `#setlist-pdf-btn` next to Print, reusing the existing well-titled print document (cover + running order + full lyrics) → the browser's Save-as-PDF. House-style, dependency-free (no server/client PDF library), shared-hosting friendly.
- feat(print): **the Songbook + number block prints nothing for songs not in a published songbook** (owner directive, #1767 follow-up). A song in an unofficial songbook, a catalogue, or any non-published grouping has no meaningful "Songbook + number", so the whole `subtitle` block is suppressed (new `songInPublishedBook()` in `print.js` — authoritative `isOfficial` when present, else the positive-number proxy per rule #24). Documented in-app (Manage → Print Templates) and in `help/exporting.md`.
- feat(songbooks): **songbook lists alphabetise ignoring a leading article** (#150). "The Australian Hymn Book" now files under A, "A Mighty Fortress" under M. New shared `includes/sort_helpers.php` (`ihymns_title_sort_key()` / `ihymns_sort_by_title_key()`) strips one leading article — same vocabulary as the fuzzy dedup fold but preserving the rest of the title for tie-breaking (rule #22, not a re-fork). Wired into both `getSongbooks()` branches; guard `test-sort-helpers.php` (mutation-proven).
- refactor(search): **removed the dead header-search autocomplete** (#307). `_initAutocomplete` had zero callers — the typeahead dropdown never attached to any input. Removed the whole cluster together (the `search.js` methods, `.search-autocomplete` CSS, the `?action=suggest` endpoint + `SongData::suggestSongs()`, the api-docs entry, and the isolated ARIA test) so no half-feature orphan remains; the live search (`?action=search`) is untouched.
- fix(share): **a numberless song no longer renders "#0"** in the share `og:title`, the JSON-LD breadcrumb leaf and the OG preview PNG badge (#85) — all three now guard `(int)null === 0`.
- fix(offline): **the offline indicator counts deliberately-downloaded songs** (#112). `getCachedSongCount()` counted only the recent-views cache, missing #1597's saved-cache, so a user who downloaded a whole songbook saw "0 cached". Now counts both buckets, de-duped by URL.
- fix(import): **bulk importers now persist parsed credits instead of silently dropping them** (#1736). `_bulkImport_saveSong()` — the shared saver behind EVERY importer (OpenSong / OpenLyrics / ChordPro / VideoPsalm / the #1633 interchange format) — parsed writers/composers out of each source document and then wrote **no credit tables at all**, so a bulk-imported song arrived with no writers, no composers, and no `tblMusicians` registry rows (its own comment documented the gap as intentional). It now INSERTs the five core role tables (`tblSongWriters`/`Composers`/`Arrangers`/`Adaptors`/`Translators`) and calls `musicianPromote()` on each name — the SAME `creditEntryNormalise()` decompose + registry-upsert path `save_song_core.php` uses (rule #22), so an OpenSong `<author>` name-string lands as First/Surname/Suffix in the registry exactly as a curator-typed credit does. **INSERT-only by construction:** saveSong already returns `'skipped'` for an existing SongId / normalised-title match before opening its transaction, so the "re-import: replace vs merge vs leave" design question resolves to *leave* (a re-import never reaches the write) and no DELETE is needed. The tree-derived #960 guard (`test-credit-registry-promote.php`) — which its author had pre-wired to expect `song_importers.php` once it started writing credits — now covers saveSong automatically and verifies the promote call. **Verified** end-to-end against a fresh schema: importing a song with `['Mary E. Byrne','Eleanor H. Hull','mary e. byrne']` writes 2 writers (case-dup collapsed), 1 composer, 3 registry rows with decomposed First/Surname, and a re-import skips with no duplicate credits. Independent one-file fix off `alpha` (branch `fix/importer-writes-credits-1736`), not bundled with the songbook/publisher epic. (The remaining saveSong limitation — copyright/ccli/verified/PD/media flags hardcoded empty on INSERT — is separate and still tracked.)
- fix(backup): **admin "Backup Database" no longer dies with "Interrupted / empty output"** (#1771). Two independent defects. **(1) OOM** — each table was dumped with a default **buffered** `SELECT * FROM <table>`, pulling the whole table into PHP memory before the first INSERT was written; on the real corpus (~14k `tblSongs` carrying MEDIUMTEXT lyrics + `tblSongEmbeddings`) that OOM-fatals under a shared host's `memory_limit`, and the fatal fires *inside* the buffered call before the per-table line prints (hence "Interrupted" + empty pane). Now streams **UNBUFFERED** (`MYSQLI_USE_RESULT`), freeing each result before the next table's query (constant memory per table), batches multi-row INSERTs bounded by BOTH row count (200) and byte size (768 KB) so a statement never nears `max_allowed_packet`, and writes **straight into the gzip stream** (no giant uncompressed `.sql` intermediate to exhaust a disk-constrained host). **(2) Un-restorable VIEW dumps** (a second bug the report didn't catch, found during round-trip verification) — the 8 compat/derived VIEWs (`tblCreditPeople` et al. from #1741, `v_ChristianSongs`) were treated as base tables (`DROP TABLE` + empty DDL + INSERTs into a non-existent table). Now enumerates via `SHOW FULL TABLES` and emits VIEWs as `DROP VIEW` + `CREATE VIEW` in a **second pass after all base tables** (name order alone is not enough — `tblCreditPeople` sorts before its base `tblMusicians`). Runs under `MYSQLI_REPORT_STRICT`, `set_time_limit(0)` at entry, and **removes its partial file on any failure** (a truncated backup must never masquerade as good); the apply-all pre-migration `$finalFile` abort-guard contract is preserved. The plan's `ob_flush()`-for-live-progress suggestion was deliberately **not** applied — the dashboard's `?format=text` AJAX runner captures output via `ob_start()`/`ob_get_clean()` and parses a `STATUS:…---\n{body}` frame, so an early `ob_flush()` would corrupt that frame; the empty-output symptom was purely a consequence of the process dying mid-buffer. **Verified** with a full backup → restore round-trip into a scratch DB (exit 0 both ways, 141 tables + 8 views recreated, row counts match). New guard `tests/php/test-backup-streaming.php` (DB-free, comment-stripped source scan — a behavioural round-trip needs a DB and can't run in CI) locks the unbuffered read, result-`free()`, `SHOW FULL TABLES`, view-last two-pass, gzip-direct and partial-file-cleanup properties; mutation-proven both directions.
- test(publishers): **permanent CI guard + `.claude` rule for the publisher registry (#93 / epic #1765, commit 5)**. New `tests/php/test-publisher-registry.php` — a DB-free, comment-stripped source guard (CI globs `tests/php/*.php`, so it runs automatically) locking the cross-file agreements nothing at runtime enforces (rule #35): the shared cores define the functions + vocab callers depend on; `/manage/publishers` delegates to the shared core rather than re-implementing it (rule #22); the songbook editor's write handler reads the **same** `publisher_ids[]`/`publisher_roles[]`/`publisher_notes[]` POST keys the modal emits **and** validates the Role against the shared `IHYMNS_PUBLISHER_ROLES` (never a hardcoded list); and the `/publisher/` route is wired end-to-end in `router.js` + `api.php` + the page file (rule #33). **Mutation-proven per rule #34, and the first draft was wrong-but-green exactly as that rule warns**: two of five mutations slipped through because `strpos('publisherAdminMerge')` still matches `publisherAdminMergeLOCAL` and `strpos('tblPublisherAliases')` still matches `tblPublisherAliasesXX` (the substring trap) — fixed with a word-boundary `\b` matcher, after which all five real-tree mutations (drop a POST key, drop the route, stop delegating a core fn, drop a schema table, stop using the shared Role vocab) go RED, baseline GREEN, restore byte-identical. Also adds CLAUDE.md rule #37 + a red-flag codifying the registry contract (denorm-mirror-not-authority, idempotent-by-Name-not-slug, no re-fork, no hardcoded vocab, honour the `/publisher/` link). Whole PHP suite (111) + node suite (50) green. Full docs (in-app help, wiki, OpenAPI/`admin_publisher_*` API) are folded into the epic's Step-7 sweep (#91), matching how #1765 deferred its own docs.

- feat(publishers): **Public `/publisher/<slug>` page (#93 / epic #1765, commit 4)**. The registry now has a public face — necessary because the admin list (commit 2) and the editor picker rows (commit 3) already emit `/publisher/<slug>` links, and a URL another surface emits is a contract (rule #33).
