New `includes/pages/publisher.php` (mirroring `/tune/`): resolves the slug via a three-rung ladder — exact `Slug`, PHP name-fold over every `Name`, then the same fold over `tblPublisherAliases` (so a renamed publisher's old-name link still resolves, since a rename banks the old name as an alias) — and renders the publisher header (name + disambiguation + kind badge + subtitle), an "imprint of →" parent link, city, IPI/ISNI chips, aliases, notes, and the list of **songbooks they published** (via `tblSongbookPublishers`, filtered by the public `songbookVisibleSql()` so disabled books are hidden, each a `data-navigate="songbook"` link with a role badge for non-primary roles), plus an inert `application/ld+json` block (Organization, or Person for a person-kind publisher — the rule #30 exemption; no executable inline script). Existence-gated on `tblPublishers` (rule #9) with a friendly not-found / empty state. Wired into the SPA: `case 'publisher'` in `router.js` (→ `params.slug`, forwarded generically as `?slug=`) and in `api.php` (uncached, matching the tune/iswc precedent). **Verified live** against a scratch DB: an 11-point render pass — resolved (name, disambiguation, city, aliases, the linked songbook + its `/songbook/RT` link, JSON-LD), not-found state, empty state, AND both fallback routes (alias-slug + name-fold both resolve to the canonical publisher, rule #33) — all green; `php -l` (page + api.php) + `node --check` (router.js) + fragment-inline-scripts guard (26 files, the ld+json exempt) all green. The songbook page's own registry-publisher chips are a deferred follow-up (its free-text `Publisher` denorm already displays the primary publisher).

- feat(publishers): **Songbook editor multi-publisher picker + Publisher typeahead (#93 / epic #1765, commit 3)**. Wires the registry into the songbook editor — the "multi-publisher copyright" + "typeahead everywhere" deliverables. The free-text `Publisher` field (shared create+edit partial) gains a **registry-backed datalist typeahead** (`?action=publisher_search`, debounced; pre-migration it returns nothing so the field degrades to plain text). The **edit modal** gains a **multi-publisher M:N picker** (mirroring the #831 compilers picker): add publishers by typeahead, each row carrying a **Role** dropdown from `IHYMNS_PUBLISHER_ROLES` (publisher / co-publisher / distributor / imprint / administrator / printer — VARCHAR vocab, not ENUM) and an optional note; DOM order → `SortOrder`. On save the update handler reconciles `tblSongbookPublishers` with a DELETE-then-INSERT inside the existing save transaction, de-duped by `(PublisherId, Role)` (the `uq_book_pub_role` UNIQUE allows one publisher in several roles but never the same role twice), and a bad/absent role coerces to `publisher`. When the picker is non-empty the free-text `tblSongbooks.Publisher` **denorm is re-synced to the primary (first-listed) publisher's name** (the TuneName↔TuneId pattern); an empty picker leaves the curator's typed value untouched. All schema-gated by a `$hasPublishersSchema` probe (pre-migration installs skip the whole picker silently); the reconciled `publisher_ids` are added to the songbook-edit activity-log payload. This is a full admin page (own `<head>`), so the inline picker script is legitimate (rule #30 applies only to api.php SPA fragments); the fragment guard still passes. **Verified live** against a scratch DB: a 7-assertion write-handler behavioural pass — multi-publisher insert with roles, `(id,role)` de-dup collapsing dup + bad-role rows, denorm sync overriding typed text, same-publisher-two-roles allowed, empty-picker clears links but preserves the typed denorm — all green; both new inline scripts node-`--check` clean; `php -l` + fragment-inline-scripts guard green.

- feat(publishers): **Publishers admin CRUD — `/manage/publishers` + shared core (#93 / epic #1765, commit 2)**. Full curator surface for the `tblPublishers` registry, modelled line-for-line on `/manage/tunes` (#1748): list (name, kind, parent, city, songbook-count, alias-count, active) + create + edit modal + delete + merge. **One shared core** `includes/publisher_admin.php` (validate/uniqueness/persist/create/rename-cascade/aliases/usage/delete/merge) backs both the page and the future `admin_publisher_*` API (rule #22/#35 — nothing to drift); `includes/publisher_helpers.php` holds the `IHYMNS_PUBLISHER_KINDS`/`IHYMNS_PUBLISHER_ROLES` VARCHAR vocabularies (not ENUM, rule #20), the slug fold, and the one find-or-create funnel. The edit modal wires **person + location typeaheads** (the #93 headline): a musician-link picker (`?action=musician_search` → `MusicianId`), a parent-publisher picker (`?action=publisher_search` → `ParentId`, self-excluded), and the shared `place-search.js` for the city (→ `CityId`). Every optional table/column is probed once via `publisherAdminProbeGates()` and each FK candidate is existence-checked before store (a stale musician/parent/city id degrades to NULL, never throws); `ParentId` gets a bounded **ancestor-walk cycle guard** (self-parent and A→imprint→A both rejected). A publisher **rename cascades** into the free-text `tblSongbooks.Publisher` denorm on that publisher's linked books (the TuneName-cascade equivalent), and a **merge** repoints songbook links (UPDATE IGNORE past the `uq_book_pub_role`), re-parents imprints, rewrites the denorm source-name→target-name, moves aliases (+ the source's own name becomes a target alias, rule #33), and deletes the source — all in one transaction. `manage_publishers` entitlement wired across all four sites (PHP + JS maps in lockstep, admin-links nav under "Catalogue", the labels map); page gate equals the nav-advertised entitlement (rule #1587). All writes hit the activity log (`publisher.create`/`edit`/`delete`/`merge`) per the owner's "record everything" directive. **Verified live** against a scratch DB: a 20-assertion behavioural pass over the shared core — create, person-persist (kind/musician/city/ipi), FK null-out, parent + cycle + self-parent guards, alias dedup/drop-own-name, usage counts, rename cascade, full merge (repoint/re-parent/alias/denorm), delete — all green; entitlement-parity (PHP↔JS), admin-gate-parity (39 pages) and orphan-inventory (52 checks) all green. The external-links editor panel is deferred (the `tblPublisherExternalLinks` table ships dormant + forward-looking, like `tblTuneExternalLinks` before #1748; merge/usage already handle its rows) — tracked follow-up.

- feat(publishers): **Publishers registry — schema batch (#93 / epic #1765, commit 1)**. Promotes the songbook publisher from the free-text `tblSongbooks.Publisher` scalar to a first-class registry, in ONE forward-looking additive/idempotent schema batch (rule #20): `tblPublishers` (persons + companies via a `Kind` VARCHAR vocabulary; an optional `MusicianId` FK so a musician who also publishes is not duplicated; a `ParentId` self-FK for imprint / **catalogue grouping**, mirroring `tblWorks.ParentWorkId`; `Ipi`/`Isni` NULL-distinct UNIQUE; a `CityName`+`CityId` place-FK mirror; `IsActive` soft-hide), `tblSongbookPublishers` (multi-publisher copyright M:N mirroring `tblSongbookCompilers`, with a `Role` VARCHAR vocabulary + reserved dormant `ValidFrom`/`ValidTo` rights-window columns), and the forward-looking `tblPublisherAliases` + `tblPublisherExternalLinks` (rule #15/#20 — the family never needs a second migration). The free-text `tblSongbooks.Publisher` column is **kept as a JOIN-free denorm display mirror** (same pattern as TuneName↔TuneId), so every existing reader keeps working. New `migrate-publishers-entity.php` backfills the registry from DISTINCT non-empty Publisher strings (CI-folded) and links each book; **idempotency is by Name existence, not slug** — the registry has no `uq_Name` (two publishers may share a name under different Disambiguation), so a naive INSERT-IGNORE-on-slug re-run minted duplicates (`praise-trust-2`); caught by a behavioural round-trip and fixed. Registry card + 4-table OR-probe (rule #19). **Verified live**: fresh `schema.sql` install (4 tables, 8 FKs) clean; migration RUN 1 → 2 publishers + 4 links, RUNs 2–3 → 0/0 (idempotent), case variants folded, empty/NULL skipped. Admin CRUD + editor picker land in the following commits. ⚠️ Owner sub-decision flagged: "catalogue grouping" is implemented as a self-FK imprint hierarchy (the defensible reading, since `tblCatalogues` groups *songs*); trivially changeable to a publisher↔catalogue M:N if that was the intent.
- feat(gating): **#1769 P5 — DM-1 org-licence consolidation (dormant)** (branch `claude/gating-model-review`). Completes the consolidation of org licences into the single authoritative `tblOrganisationLicences` store before the (deferred, gated) legacy-column retire. **Finding on investigation (verify-before-building):** the bulk of DM-1 — backfilling the join table from `tblOrganisations.LicenceType`/`LicenceNumber` — is **already shipped** by `migrate-organisation-licences.php`, and `tblContentLicences` has no writer in the codebase (empty on every normal install). So DM-1 is scoped to the two GENUINE residual gaps, not a from-scratch duplicate: **(A)** that existing backfill never carried `tblOrganisations.LicenceExpiresAt`, so a join row can hold a NULL expiry while the legacy column holds one — `migrate-consolidate-org-licences.php` carries it **fill-NULL-only** (never clobbering a curator's join-row value); **(B)** it consolidates any hand-written org-scoped `tblContentLicences` ghost rows (INSERT IGNORE, **row-count-reported** because a ghost that confers a tier would then be seen by `resolveEffectiveTier()` — a disclosed live effect, per P2-F; zero rows in practice). Verified **no-op for resolution**: `resolveEffectiveTier()` unions on `LicenceType` only and never reads `ExpiresAt`. Data-only (no schema.sql edit); an Apply-all registry card with a data-derived **drift-detecting** probe + a CLI `revert-consolidate-org-licences.php`. Guard `test-consolidate-org-licences.php` (mutation-proven: drop the `IS NULL` fill-guard → red); PHP **121/121** CI-parity; live-DB round-trip verified (carry → idempotent re-run → revert → cascade cleanup; ghost leg confirmed empty). The legacy-column **retire itself stays a separate, gated P6 step** (#1778).
- feat(gating): **#1769 P4 — management surfaces + DM-2 rights-fact derivation** (branch `claude/gating-model-review`, continues P3 below). The write path over the Model-2 vocabulary + facts, all **dormant** (nothing ENFORCES until the P6 master-switch flip). Six commits: **(A)** licence-type CRUD — a new `manage_licence_types` entitlement + `/manage/licence-types` page + four `admin_licence_type_*` API actions over one shared core `includes/licence_type_admin.php` (validate with LicenceKey-immutable-on-update, covers-merge that keeps `{}` stable across a JSON round-trip, delete refusing system/referenced rows, existence-gated reference counts), documented in api-docs.yaml. **(B)** editor **Rights panel** — the two per-song rights facts (`tblSongs.LyricsRightsLicenceKey`/`MusicRightsLicenceKey`) join the v2 editor via `ED2_META_FIELDS` + an existence-gated `metadata_field_update` branch (validates against the live registry, 422 on an unknown key, `admin.song.rights_set` audit) + a DOM-first `rights-panel.js` fed by `window._iHymnsLicenceTypes`; `load_song` emits the songbook-default prefill hint. **(C)** songbook **default rights** — `/manage/songbooks` edit modal sets `Default{Lyrics,Music}RightsLicenceKey` (registry-validated, column-gated) with an opt-in **fill-NULL-only** apply-to-songs sweep (`IS NULL` never overwrites a curator's key). **(D)** **DM-2** `migrate-derive-rights-facts.php` — copies each song's existing `require_licence` restriction into the matching fact column via the ONE shared fold `rightsFactColumnForLicence()` (lyrics/music/none), fill-NULL-only + idempotent, data-only (no DDL), an Apply-all registry card with a data-derived **drift-detecting** probe + a CLI revert tool. **(E)** deferred-P0 tidy — `require_*` restrictions normalise `Effect='deny'` server-side (engine-dead, the value was already ignored) + hide the control; `gating-noop-verify` gates on `manage_configuration` with a matching nav row. **(F)** an **activity-log CI guard** (`test-gating-admin-activity-log.php`) that tree-derives every gating-write admin surface and fails the build if one never calls `logActivity()` — which caught + fixed `manage/tiers.php` logging only on failure (success `admin.tiers.*` now logged). Every gating action is now audited (owner directive). Six new mutation-proven guards (`test-licence-type-admin`, `test-rights-panel-fields`, `test-songbook-rights-defaults`, `test-derive-rights-facts`, `test-restrictions-effect-honesty`, `test-gating-admin-activity-log`) + a `§(g)` fact-column containment lock added to `test-gating-pipeline-structure.php`; PHP 120/120 + node 50/50 CI-parity; DM-2 + the fill-NULL-only sweep verified against a live DB. **Still owner-gated (P6):** the master-switch flip + the `/manage/gating` hub (#1772–#1777, and the dead songbook/feature restriction pickers).
- refactor(gating): **#1769 P3 — emitter adoption + the song-page fork collapse** (branch `claude/gating-model-review`, continues P2 below). The public HTML song page carried its OWN inline copy of the tier/presence/media gating alongside the JSON API's; P3 collapses it onto the shared Model-2 pipeline and closes the shared-cache gap — additive/dormant, a **proven byte-identical no-op** in both gating states. New `includes/song_page_gating.php` (`songPageGatingDecide(array $viewer, …)`) reads the already-resolved caps/presence off the viewer struct; `song.php` now builds ONE viewer via `accessViewerContext(…, [])` (apiKeyScopes=[] is load-bearing — null would gate a bypass-key render) and delegates the whole decision, resolving nothing itself. Proven the P2 A→B→C way: the legacy maths was extracted verbatim, frozen as **4608 goldens** (`tests/php/fixtures/song-page-gating-goldens.json`), and the viewer-driven form proven to reproduce every one (`test-song-page-gating-equivalence.php`). The page's DELIBERATE pre-existing quirk (presence ORs into sheet/midi, not just audio, unlike the API) is **preserved byte-identically**, pinned by the golden replay and filed as an owner decision (#1774). `songbook_export` now builds the viewer ONCE and maps `accessApplySong` (a perf win — was rebuilding the recursive-CTE viewer per song); the single-song emitters stay on the delegates by decision (in-code notes prevent future churn). Cache gap closed: when gating is on, `api.php` marks the viewer-dependent `page=song` fragment `Cache-Control: private, no-store` + excludes it from the shared ETag/SW cache, and the service worker's new `swResponseCacheable()` honours no-store at its incidental put sites (deliberate offline downloads stay saveable, re-gated on next fetch — the #1388 posture). Auth needed NO client change (the `ihymns_auth` cookie already rode `loadPage` fetches; the stale song.php:223-227 note is corrected). `test-gating-pipeline-structure.php` gains the song.php fork-collapse + cache-exclusion guards (its `$viewer[…]` key check is now tree-derived). Full mutation checklist run break→red→restore; PHP 114/114 + node 50/50 CI-parity. **Commit F deferred** (wiring the presence token into `checkBulkAccess` is a state-(b) delta → #1777, owner sign-off). Further P6 first-enable follow-ups filed: #1774 (presence-OR divergence), #1775 (audio-media.php tier gate), #1776 (SW cache-clear on login).

- refactor(gating): **#1769 P2 — one licence registry, one viewer resolver, one enforcement pipeline** (branch `claude/gating-model-review`; P0/P1 landed earlier in this batch). The content-gating logic was scattered — tier/ccli/presence resolution inlined at the top of both `contentGatingApply()` and `contentGatingMediaAllowed()`, the media kind→cap switch copy-pasted, and the licence vocabulary hardcoded in six sites that had already drifted. P2 consolidates to **facts × grants × one resolver × one pipeline**, additive/dormant and a **proven byte-identical no-op** in both gating-off and gating-on states. New: `includes/access_context.php` (`accessViewerContext()` resolves "who is asking?" once into an `ACCESS_VIEWER_KEYS` struct, with the old throw semantics preserved exactly; `accessViewerAssemble()` is the pure DB-free seam), `includes/access_resolver.php` (`accessApplySong()`/`accessMediaAllowed()` make every field decision branch-for-branch identical to the deleted `_contentGating*LegacyCore` seams; `accessResolve()` is the unwired P3/P6 primitive; a per-song rights-fact gate is present but **triple-locked dead** — `SongData` selects neither rights column, no row carries a value, the guard skips null/''), and `includes/licence_registry.php` (the ONE reader of `tblLicenceTypes`, degrading to the byte-exact P1 seeds on any un-migrated install). `contentGatingApply()`/`contentGatingMediaAllowed()` are now thin delegates that lazy-require the pipeline (breaking the require cycle). The six licence-vocab sites (`organisations.php`, `restrictions.php`, `my-organisations.php`, `ccli_validator.php`'s conferral overlay, three schema COMMENTs) now single-source from the registry — the conferral is a proven identity for every input (`mrl`/`custom` confer nothing → the preserved `'free'` fallback). `TIER_CAPS` gains an optional 5th `enforce` element (`tierCapEnforce()`, inert in P2). **Proof:** the pre-refactor outputs were frozen as 384 goldens and replayed byte-identically through the new pipeline (`test-gating-equivalence.php`, DB-free); `test-gating-pipeline-structure.php` + extended `test-gating-noop.php`/`test-gating-registry.php` + the updated `test-media-kind-cap-shared.php` lock the structure; the whole mutation checklist was run break→red→restore (rule #34). Full PHP suite 113/113 CI-parity. Enforcement stays entirely dormant behind `content_gating_enabled='0'`. Two owner decisions deferred to P6, non-blocking: `mrl`/`custom` tier conferral (#1772) and the `RequiresCcli` tier-name-vs-cap gate (#1773). Data migrations (DM-1 org-licence consolidation, DM-2 existing-gating→per-song-facts) and activity logging land in P4–P6.
- feat(qr): **QR codes now come from the CueRCode service via its API — throughout iHymns** (owner directive 2026-08-05; `github.com/MWBMPartners/CueRCode`). QR generation moved off the client-side vendored `qrcode-generator` (used by the #1767 R print block and the #1339 Service-Projection join QR) onto our CueRCode REST API. CueRCode auth is a secret `X-API-Key`, so generation is **server-proxied** and the key never reaches a browser: `includes/cuercode_client.php` (modelled on the battle-tested `intapps_client.php` — SSRF-hardened host-bound URL, size-capped aborting write-callback, no redirects, SSL verify, timeouts, returns null/never-throws, dormant until keyed; `cuercode_api_key` registered in `secretSettingKeys()` = encrypted at rest) calls `POST /api/v1/generate` and decodes the base64 data-URI to bytes. The ONE client-facing surface is **`qr.php`** (mirrors `og-image.php`): `GET /qr.php?data=&size=&format=svg|png&ecc=` streams the QR bytes with `Cache-Control: immutable`, rate-limited (fail-open), and answers **503 (no body)** when CueRCode isn't configured/reachable so callers degrade to the always-shown URL/code text. Both surfaces now emit a plain `<img src="…/qr.php?data=…">` — **no client-side QR library, no async pre-pass** (the print renderer's `qr` block became a pure synchronous `<img>`; CSP already allows `img-src 'self'`). The vendored `qrcode-generator` is fully removed (config `libraries` entry, `download-vendor.sh` step, and `tests/test-qr.js`), replaced by the tree-derived, mutation-proven **`tests/test-qr-cuercode.js`** (both surfaces use `/qr.php`, key stays server-side, no client-side QR fingerprint remains — comment-stripped so retirement comments don't false-positive). ⚠️ **Dormant until an iHymns→CueRCode API key is provisioned** and pasted on `/manage/configuration` (deploy-time, non-blocking — mirrors the intapps #1726 pattern); the live round-trip can't be verified without it, everything else is (syntax, guard, graceful 503 degradation). New CLAUDE.md rule #38. Full design: `.claude/qr-cuercode-integration-plan.md`. **Still to wire (this branch, follow-up):** the CueRCode credential fields on `/manage/configuration`.

- feat(print): **print-template enhancements — the no-schema block/page/visibility system** (#1767, owner-approved full scope A–AM; branch `claude/print-templates-1767`). The song-print system's layout is a `BlocksJson` ordered block list + `PageOptionsJson` (both existing JSON columns), so this whole slice adds **no schema** — the one-pass foundation (#1350 / rule #20) is already paid. Shipped (all verified through the SHARED renderer `renderTemplateBodyHtml()`, the single source of truth for both the printout and the admin live preview): **4 new content blocks** — Scripture reference (N, `tblSongScriptureRefs` via `?include=scriptureRefs`), Tune + metre (O, base `tuneName` + MeterCode via `?include=tune`), Themes/tags (P, `song.tags`), and a QR-code of the permalink (R, vendored `qrcode-generator` via an async pre-pass so the sync renderer + byte-identical preview are untouched; degrades to the URL text if the library fails); **3 block options** — lyrics alignment + font scale (A) and songbook-abbreviation mode (B); **5 page options** — page size A4/Letter/Legal (G), line spacing (F), high-contrast (V), accent colour (AM) and ink-saver mono (AB), built on a new `PRINT_PAGE_OPTIONS` registry mirroring the block-type pattern; and **conditional block visibility** (Y) — a universal `showIf` (hasChords/hasCcli/hasScripture/…) so one template adapts per song. **The load-bearing mechanism (rule #35):** the block-type set, its `renderBlock()` switch, `$BLOCK_SCHEMA`, `PRINT_PAGE_OPTIONS`↔`$PAGE_OPTION_SCHEMA`, and `PRINT_SHOWIF_CONDITIONS`↔`$SHOWIF_CONDITIONS` previously agreed only by a comment; new CI guard `tests/php/test-print-block-registry.php` derives all of them from the tree and asserts agreement (incl. per-type option keys), mutation-proven in every drift direction (its own PHP parser was caught mis-reading a nested `choices` key on first run — red, not silently green, rule #34). Every feature behaviourally verified with node against the shared renderer incl. empty-data grace and bad-value fallbacks. **Decision-gated remainder batched to the owner on #1767** (server-PDF pipeline W/X/AC/AD/AJ; org branding/white-label D/K/L; uploadable layouts E; usage log AK; thumbnails AL) — rule #20 forbids freezing that schema until 4 owner decisions land; none block this slice. Buildable-now follow-ups still open: Z (clone + JSON import/export), J (system default), and the H-family multi-page/pagination. Plan: `.claude/print-templates-1767-plan.md`.

- fix(apple): **9 Swift test factories couldn't compile — `SongDetail(...)` missing the 6 #1752 identity fields** (second Apple-CI hotfix, masked behind the LOC-budget failure). Once the loc-budget step passed, Apple CI got further and hit a real `swift build` error: the #1752 work added `subtitle`/`disambiguation`/`firstPublishedYear`/`copyrightYears`/`copyrightHolder`/`externalIds` to `SongDetail` but didn't update the package's test construction sites, and the struct **deliberately relies on the synthesized memberwise init** (documented in its header — no defaults). Appended the 6 fields (all `nil`) to the 9 stale `SongDetail(...)` calls across `SongDetailMediaTests`, `OfflineStoreSavedSongsTests`, `ProjectionResolverTests`, `SongComparison{ViewModel,Engine}Tests`, `OfflineStorageViewModelTests`, `ProjectionViewModelTests`, `TVProjectionBridgeTests`, `TVServiceFollowBridgeTests` — matching the already-correct shape in `SongDetailTests`. Chose updating the call sites over hand-writing an init-with-defaults because the model's header documents the no-memberwise-init decision AND an ~85-line init would breach `SongDetail.swift`'s own 400-line budget. `Work(...)` was checked and has no equivalent gap (no test constructions; custom `init(from:)`). ⚠️ Swift not compiled here (no toolchain) — verified by reading each construction against the struct's property list; the PR's Apple CI run is the compile check.

- fix(apple): **`SongDetailView.swift` breached the 400-line LOC-budget tripwire; render-site guard hardened to survive the split** (post-merge CI hotfix for PR #1762). The #1752 catalogue work (disambiguation + subtitle lines) grew `SongDetailView.swift` to 420 lines, failing `Scripts/loc-budget.sh` (#1395). Per the file's own documented "every section is its own file" convention (and the `SongDetailView+Handoff.swift` precedent), the header block — a pure function of `SongDetail` — is extracted into a new **`SongDetailHeaderView.swift`** (parent back to 386 lines). This surfaced a rule-#34 gap in `test-native-identity-contract.js`: its render-site check was a hardcoded `{SongMetadataView, SongDetailView}` two-file list, so the legitimate extraction turned it spuriously RED. Rewritten to be **tree-derived** — scan every IHFeatures view file — AND **specific**: it matches the property access `detail.<key>`, not the bare token `<key>` (which for common SwiftUI words like `subtitle` appears in eight unrelated files, making a substring check unable to ever fail). Mutation-proven both directions: deleting the `detail.subtitle` render → RED, deleting `detail.firstPublishedYear` → RED, restore → green. ⚠️ Swift not compiled here (no toolchain) — the extraction is a behaviour-preserving pure move, CI-gated by `swift build`/`swift test`.

- fix(db): **`schema.sql` could not install on MariaDB 11.7+ — `tblSongEmbeddings.Vector` is a reserved word** (post-merge CI hotfix for PR #1762). `VECTOR` became a reserved keyword in MariaDB 11.7 (CI runs 11.8.8), so the unquoted column `Vector MEDIUMBLOB` in `tblSongEmbeddings` (#1090 P11, the dormant semantic-search cache) was a syntax error — the fresh-install test (`test-schema-installs.php`) failed with "created 139 of 140" because the install aborts at the first error. This slipped through the local pre-merge run because that test **skips without a DB** and the local MariaDB was 10.11 (pre-reservation); CI's MariaDB service is what exposed it. Fixed by backtick-quoting the column (`` `Vector` ``) in **both** `schema.sql` and `migrate-song-embeddings.php` (rule #19 byte-identical mirror) — the standard reserved-word remedy, zero name-divergence risk. Proven: the full 140-table `schema.sql` now installs cleanly against a real MariaDB, and the whole PHP suite is 106/106 with the DB present. A tree-scan confirmed `Vector` was the only bare reserved-word column.

- chore(deps): **Dependabot multi-branch version updates + a security-fix backport workflow** — GitHub does two separate things and only one is `dependabot.yml`-configurable. **Version updates** (`.github/dependabot.yml`): `target-branch` is per-entry and singular, so keeping `main`, `alpha` and `beta` all current means one entry per branch — npm (repo root) and github-actions are declared on all three; gradle (`/appAndroid`) and swift (`/appApple/Packages/iHymnsKit`) on `main` (mobile version PRs on the release branches can be added later). All grouped (minor+patch) so each ecosystem/branch yields at most a couple of PRs/week, labelled, weekly on Monday. **Security updates** are NOT configurable there — GitHub auto-opens a fix PR against the default branch (`main`) ONLY, with no fan-out knob — so `.github/workflows/dependabot-security-backport.yml` carries a qualifying merged fix (authored by `dependabot[bot]`, or labelled `security`/`backport`) to `alpha`, `beta` and `release-candidate`: a `pull_request_target: [closed]` trigger (+ `workflow_dispatch` for a manual PR number) cherry-picks the merge commit (`-x`) onto each release branch and opens a PR, a conflict files a tracking issue instead of a broken PR, and a **non-existent branch is skipped gracefully** (`release-candidate` does not exist yet — the workflow activates it the moment it does). ⚠️ PRs opened with the built-in `GITHUB_TOKEN` do not auto-trigger `on: pull_request` CI (GitHub's recursion guard); the header documents adding a `BACKPORT_PAT` secret to have `test.yml` run automatically.

- chore(version): **significant minor bump `0.4001.0` → `0.4100.0`** for the `wave3-fixes` batch — reflecting the scope of the #1741 catalogue-expansion epic follow-ups + the #1201/#1761 API-envelope + OpenAPI redesign, rather than the per-merge `+1` the `version-bump.yml` auto-bumper applies. Bumped by hand in `infoAppVer.php` and mirrored into `api-docs.yaml`'s `info.version`; the alpha auto-bumper will still apply its standard `feat(`→minor `+1` on merge.

- feat(api): **uniform v2 response envelope across the public/PWA API + web PWA + Apple clients** (#1761, completing #1201). The public `api.php` now speaks a versioned contract: a client sending `X-API-Version: 2` (or `?v=2`) gets every response in ONE shape — success `{ ok:true, data:<payload> }`, error `{ ok:false, error:{ code, message, …preserved } }` with a meaningful HTTP status and a stable machine `code` (the #1677 lesson: branch on status/code, never prose). **v2 is opt-in**, so absent/other = legacy v1 bare payloads BYTE-FOR-BYTE — already-deployed and offline-cached web/native clients are unaffected; this is a versioned migration, not a flag day. **One server chokepoint:** `sendJson()` (all ~971 call sites) funnels through the pure, unit-tested `apiEnvelopeWrap()`/`apiEnvelopeError()`/`apiContractVersion()` extracted to `includes/api_envelope.php`; non-`sendJson` responses (streaming/CSV exports, the `health` probe) are legitimately outside the JSON envelope. **Clients updated to match, transparently:** the web PWA's `apiFetchJson()` sends the header (only there — the ~10 raw-`apiFetch()`+`.json()` callers stay v1) and unwraps `data` / throws structured errors with `.status`+`.code`; the Apple `APIClient` sends the header and its transport (`performOnce`) unwraps the envelope once via `unwrapEnvelope()`, so all ~25 `*Decoding.swift` decoders are UNCHANGED. The unwrap condition (`ok:true` + `data`) is collision-free — no natural payload has that top-level shape. **Verified end-to-end against a live server + dev DB:** v1 `songbooks` returns the bare payload; v2 returns `{ok:true,data:{songbooks:…}}` with `X-API-Version`/`Vary` headers; a v2 error returns HTTP 400 `{ok:false,error:{code:http_404-style,message}}`; v1 error stays bare. New contract test `tests/php/test-api-envelope.php` (20 assertions) unit-tests the pure logic AND asserts all three surfaces are wired (rule #35) — mutation-proven across the server wrap, web unwrap, and Apple unwrap. Full PHP suite (106 files) + node suite (50) green; `api-docs.yaml` documents the versioning header + `ApiEnvelope`/`ApiError` schemas. ⚠️ Swift **not compiled** here (no toolchain) — the Apple transport change is defensive/tolerant (non-envelope bodies pass through unchanged) and CI-gated (`swift build`/`swift test`). CSRF-on-every-write and read rate-limiting were already banked; this closes the last breaking part of #1201.

- docs(api): **comprehensive per-action OpenAPI breakout of the v2 Song Editor API** (#1201 core). All **50** `manage/editor/api2.php` actions — previously covered by a single prose block "pending the #1201 breakout" — are now individual `/manage/editor/api2.php?action=…` path items in `api-docs.yaml` (+2246 lines, purely additive), under a new **`Editor API v2`** tag that documents the shared conventions (editor-role + per-action entitlements, the `X-Requested-With` CSRF gate on every POST, the uniform `{ok,…}` envelope, and HTTP-status-based error branching 400/404/409/422). Derived from api2.php's authoritative self-documenting header; the implementer verified every `ed2_requireEntitlement()` by grep (correcting an assumed `edit_songs` gate on `song_external_id_*`, which is editor-role-only) and fixed 5 `operationId` collisions with the legacy v1 editor tag (281/281 unique). The `/manage/api-docs` Swagger UI loads the spec dynamically, so it surfaces automatically. **The contract guard is now widened**: `tests/php/test-openapi-actions-exist.php` replaces its narrow 5-action (#1608) "mentioned in prose" check with a full **tree-derived per-action coverage** assertion — every api2 dispatcher action must have a real path item — with a self-cleaning exemption list (empty today); both directions mutation-proven (delete a path item → RED; phantom doc → RED). **Already-banked scope confirmed** (not re-done): CSRF is on every state-changing endpoint on BOTH surfaces (public `api.php` top-of-file `X-Requested-With` gate + `validateCsrfRequest()`; editor gate), and read rate-limiting exists (rule #28). **The remaining #1201 scope is BREAKING** (uniform envelope across the public read endpoints, versioning, full HTTP-status error normalisation) — it changes response contracts the shipped PWA + native apps depend on, so it is split to **#1761** for an explicit owner decision rather than shipped autonomously. Verified: `test-openapi-actions-exist.php` 10/10 green, `test-orphan-inventory.php` + `test-editor-api2-contract.php` green, YAML parses.

- feat(catalogue): **native apps (Apple + Android) surface the #1741 catalogue expansion** (#1752). Apple (`iHymnsKit`): `SongDetail` decodes the five #1750 identity keys + `externalIds` (all Optional/`decodeIfPresent` so pre-#1750 payloads and persisted offline blobs still decode); one shared copyright fold (`CopyrightDisplay.swift`) for `SongDetail` + `Work` (rule #22); disambiguation/subtitle/rights/externalIds rendered; the `/musician/` deep link now resolves (was the one AASA-claimed path bouncing to Safari) and `CanonicalURL` emits it; `Work` decodes the nine P4b keys; the musician view switches the frozen `credit_person` action → canonical `musician` and renders IPI/ISNI. **Web (the only server change):** `SongData::getMusician()` emits an always-present `identifiers` array from an existence-gated `tblMusicianIdentifiers` read (bound, `try/catch → []`, additive on both envelopes — rule #5/#9), proven live. Android: forward-compatible fields only (no data source yet — the Phase-2 API client is #1756). New guard `tests/test-native-identity-contract.js` (node 49→50) tree-derives the keys, strips nested Swift comments, and holds web↔Apple↔Android in lockstep; a rule-#34 gap (Work-key count hard-`=== 9`) was caught by the adversarial pass and fixed to a `>= 9` floor (mutation-proven). ⚠️ Swift/Android **not compiled** here (no toolchain) — `swift test` / `gradle assembleDebug` are a tracked pre-merge CI step. Deferred follow-ups filed: #1756/#1757/#1758/#1759. No schema change. **This completes the #1741 catalogue-expansion epic's follow-up queue** (#1749/#1751/#1754/#1755/#1750/#1748/#1752) on web + Apple.

- feat(catalogue): **Tune admin CRUD — `/manage/tunes` + `manage_tunes` + `admin_tune_*` API** (#1748). The `tblTunes` family (tunes/aliases/credits/external-links) shipped dormant in #1741 P1 with no curator write path; this adds it (modelled on `manage/works.php`): list/create/edit/delete/merge, aliases + credits (musician typeahead) + external-links sub-panels, all gated on the new `manage_tunes` entitlement (PHP + JS mirror + nav row, held in lockstep by the parity suites). One shared core `includes/tune_admin.php` backs BOTH the page and the four `admin_tune_*` API actions so they can't drift (rule #22/#35). Credit-role vocab centralised (`IHYMNS_TUNE_CREDIT_ROLES`, VARCHAR-not-ENUM); external-links whitelist widened + `IHYMNS_LINK_ENTITY_TYPES` + an AppliesTo tick UI on `/manage/external-link-types`. **Two STRICT-mode correctness bugs the adversarial pass caught + fixed with column-level gating (rule #9):** `tblTunes.Subtitle`/`.Disambiguation` (added by the *tune-enrichment* card) and `tblTuneCredits.MusicianId` (named `CreditPersonId` until *musicians-rename*) were read/written ungated, which throws on a partially-migrated install — now behind a `tuneAdminColumnExists()` probe (`hasTuneEnrichCols` / `hasCredits`-requires-`MusicianId`), **proven live** against a scratch pre-enrichment schema (the ungated pre-fix SELECT throws `Unknown column 'Subtitle'`; the gated path degrades cleanly). Guard `test-tune-admin-surface.php` (tree-derived) hardened to scan the shared core, drop `T_INLINE_HTML` (a JS-comment decoy can no longer satisfy a "PHP references X" check), check both external-links whitelists, and lock in the gating — every assertion mutation-proven. 105 PHP + 49 node green. No schema change.

- feat(catalogue): **public song page + API + JSON-LD surface the #1741 P1 identity fields** (#1750). P1 added `tblSongs.Subtitle`/`Disambiguation`/`FirstPublishedYear`/`CopyrightYears`/`CopyrightHolder` and P5 made them editable, but `song.php` rendered none and the wire lacked them. Now: `song.php` shows a disambiguation parenthetical in the `<h1>`, a subtitle `<p>` (song `lang`/`dir`), a "First published YYYY" line, and a `$copyrightDisplay` **precedence fold** (split years+holder when present, else the legacy `Copyright` denorm — never both) in header/footer/rights-panel; pure server markup, no `<script>` (rule #30). One instance-cached `_songIdentityCols()` probe + one `_songIdentitySelect()` fold feed BOTH read paths (`_fetchSongRow()` + `getSongs()`) with identical always-present normalisers, so the direct and bulk-rendered song paths agree (rule #22). The five keys are now on `song_detail`/`song_data`/`songbook_export`/`bulk_songs` (byte-identical to the editor's `ED2_META_FIELDS` spellings — **this is the #1752 native contract**), ungated by design (`content_gating.php` untouched). New opt-in `include=externalIds` block exposes the recording-ID store (`{idScope,idType,idValue,source}`) while **never** leaking `SourceRef`. JSON-LD gains `alternativeHeadline`/`disambiguatingDescription`/`datePublished`/`copyrightHolder`/`copyrightYear`(bare-4-digit only)/`copyrightNotice`. The song page's tune anchor now prefers the `tblTunes` registry slug (one computation, replacing two duplicated name-folds). New guard `test-song-identity-render.php` derives the column set from schema.sql's `#1741 P1` doctags, comment-strips every source, and (a deliberate upgrade over the P4b guard) uses `tsirVariableUsageCount()` to catch the wrong-but-green where a deleted render survives a bare "quoted key present" check. Verified independently: 104 PHP + 49 node green; live data-layer probe (rolled back) confirmed both read paths + the opt-in block + the empty-case degrade; own mutation proofs (deleted render → RED, exposed SourceRef → RED). No schema change.

- feat(catalogue): **#1749 FULL UNIFICATION — `tblSongExternalIds` is the recording-ID authority, `tblSongs.Isrc` a synced denorm** (epic #1741; owner escalation 2026-08-03 "do full unifications now also"). Builds on the Phase-1 union+dual-write below. **One projection rule** (`SONG_EXTERNAL_ID_ISRC_PRIMARY_ORDER` + `songExternalIdIsrcProjectionSql()` + `songExternalIdUnionArmSql()` — marker-first via `<=>`, then lowest `Id`, `CHAR_LENGTH<=15`) and one store→column "last word" `songExternalIdSyncIsrcDenorm()` (rule #22). `songExternalIdMirrorIsrc()` now returns `?string` and re-projects on **both** return paths — a cleared ISRC **promotes** a manual second-recording row, an inserted one **heals** a pre-cutover mismatch. Panel add/delete + `metadata_field_update` run the sync in-transaction and echo `isrcDenorm` as a response **key-presence** signal (rule #35), consumed by `metadata-tab.js`/`external-ids-panel.js`. The `/isrc/` + `api.php song_by_identifier` + `lyrics_ingest` read paths union the store arm (one predicate, three consumers). New **data-only** cutover migration `migrate-reconcile-isrc-denorm.php` (no DDL) + registry card heals column↔store both directions; its probe doubles as a live drift detector. **Fixes #1755** — the song **merge** now MOVES the duplicate's store rows to the survivor (`UPDATE IGNORE`), DEMOTES its marker so the survivor never carries two, then re-projects, instead of letting `ON DELETE CASCADE` silently wipe them. **D-3 (defensible default, non-blocking):** `tblSongIdentityMap`'s four provider columns stay frozen legacy, not live-synced — shape-incompatible UNIQUE keys, nothing reads them. **Guards (rule #34, all mutation-proven):** `test-song-external-id-mirror.php` assertion 8 rewritten whole-body-count → per-return-path (removing the sync from the cleared path while duplicating it on the inserted keeps the count at 2 but now goes red); assertion 9 rewritten whole-file co-occurrence → per-statement forward-window; new behavioural `test-song-external-ids-reconcile.php` loads the **real** registry probe and drives both drift checks true→false (inverting either turns it red — the probe's drift logic had had no behavioural coverage), skips cleanly with no DB; `test-song-external-ids-backfill.php` slice anchor re-pinned to the entry's own close (the reconcile insertion had made it over-capture). Verified live against the dev DB (rolled-back transaction): mirror/sync/promotion/demotion/merge-collapse/resolver-union-arm all green. No schema change (a D-3 doc-comment only).

- fix(catalogue): **#1741 follow-ups — ISRC resolver union (#1749) · ingest ISRC mirror (#1751) · P4a-3 trio (#1754)**. `/isrc/<code>` now resolves a code held only in the `tblSongExternalIds` store (a curator's manual second-recording ISRC), not just `tblSongs.Isrc` — a parenthesised `OR … IN (subquery)` union, existence-gated, visibility predicate over both arms (#1749). `lyrics_ingest.php` now mirrors its ISRC writes into the store via `songExternalIdMirrorIsrc()` (new optional `$source` param, `'ihymns-ingest'` provenance; the store-path mirrors the **read-back** column value so a COALESCE fill-if-blank never desyncs the store from the column) (#1751). The `/musician/` JSON-LD crawler branch now resolves name-slugs through the shared ladder; `tblMusicianAliases` becomes a 4th resolver rung paired with the musician page's discography widening (an alias URL resolves to the canonical profile without "losing" its alias-credited songs); `SongData::getSongsByCreditName()` documented as the retained scoped-read exemplar (#1754). **The adversarial-verify pass caught three wrong-but-green guard gaps (rule #34) and all were hardened + mutation-proven**: `test-song-external-id-mirror.php` had no comment-stripping (a stale comment satisfied its byte-equality/call-site assertions); the union guard was blind to gate polarity/dead-code (fixed by extracting the pure `_ihymns_resolve_use_store()` decision + a direct polarity test + an assign-from-gate assertion); the musician guard was blind to a discarded resolver return (fixed by a result-flows-to-`$personSlug` assertion). No schema change.

- feat(catalogue): **writer page consolidated into the musician profile** (#1741 P4a-3, owner decision D4) — the old heuristic `/writer/<name-slug>` bare song-list is retired in favour of the registry-backed musician profile. A top-level `/writer/<slug>` hit now gets a **real HTTP 301** to `/musician/<slug>` (in `index.php`, mirroring the `/people|/person` 301); the SPA fragment serves the musician page directly and `router.js` soft-canonicalises the address bar via `history.replaceState` (the #1343-B `data-*` marker pattern). A **single shared resolution ladder** (`musicianResolveLegacySlugDb()` in `musician_helpers.php` — exact registry slug → `slugifyMusicianName()` punctuation/diacritic fold → registry `Name IN (…)`, fail-open, reused not re-forked) maps a legacy name-slug to its registry row; the same ladder also fixes name-slug `/musician/` credit links from song pages. `writer.php` is deleted; its no-registry-row fallback is subsumed by the musician page's six-role discography, widened to the historic writer name-variant set so a hyphenated credited name still lists (no silent 404, rule #33). The sitemap now emits registry-driven `/musician/<slug>` rows. Also fixes **#1753** (the musician-page Edit button, whose reveal had been stranded in `router.js`'s `page==='song'` branch and never appeared). Two new mutation-proven CI guards (`tests/test-writer-musician-route.js`, `tests/php/test-musician-slug-resolve.php`). Zero schema changes. **This completes the #1741 catalogue-expansion epic's development work** (P1–P6 + P4a-3).

- feat(editor): **song-editor identity fields + tune typeahead + TuneName↔TuneId lockstep** (#1741 P5, #1749) — the v2 editor Metadata tab can now enter the #1741 P1/D5 identity columns (ISRC/subtitle/disambiguation/first-published-year/copyright year(s)/holder), each 409-gated on an un-migrated install; an ISRC edit stays mirrored into the D5 `tblSongExternalIds` store (#1749 dual-write, ownership-keyed so manual rows survive). The plain "Tune Name" text box is replaced by a **bespoke live-search control** backed by a new `tune_search` typeahead (alias-JOINed, metre-filtered via the new pure `ihymns_meter_normalize()` fold) and the **one tune write core** `ed2_songTuneApply()` (`song_tune_set`) — so `TuneName` and `TuneId` are ALWAYS written together and a tune edit can never again strand the registry link (the drift this phase retires). The same lockstep rider is wired into the whole-song save (`save_song_core.php`), the bulk importer (`song_importers.php`), **and the revision-restore path** (`ed2_applySongSnapshot()` now funnels `TuneName` through the same core instead of writing it alone via the scalar loop). The shared `place-search.js` module is GENERALISED (additive `searchUrl`/`parseResults`/`pickMode`/`noun` options, byte-equivalent defaults — never a fork) to back the tune control while leaving the four pre-existing place pickers unchanged (#1594 keyboard/ARIA machinery untouched). New tree-derived, comment-stripped, mutation-proven CI guards: `tests/php/test-tune-lockstep.php` (every `tblSongs.TuneName` write site references the funnel, incl. the restore path) and `tests/test-tune-typeahead-ui.js` (the client control + `place-search.js` default byte-equivalence). Zero schema changes.

- feat(editor): **recording external-ID card-list on the Metadata tab** (#1741 P5b) — the D5 `tblSongExternalIds` key/value store gets its first UI read+write path (`song_external_ids`/`_add`/`_delete`), a card-list whose provider dropdown is built entirely from the `RECORDING_EXTERNAL_ID_TYPES` vocabulary (zero hardcoded slugs), 409 on an un-migrated install, `IdScope` server-derived, `isrc` canonicalised. Curator rows (`Source='manual'`) are distinct from the mirror/backfill rows.

- feat(catalogue): **comprehensive recording IDs — `genius` type + grandfathered-data backfill** (#1747 D5, owner-confirmed) — `tblSongExternalIds` is now the comprehensive single home for recording IDs: a new idempotent migration (`migrate-backfill-song-external-ids.php`) mirrors `tblSongs.Isrc` + the four grandfathered `tblSongIdentityMap` columns (`MusicBrainzRecordingMBID`/`SpotifyTrackId`/`GeniusTrackId`/`IsrcCode`) into the key/value store via `INSERT IGNORE` (idempotent, re-runnable, creates no schema object). Adds a `genius` `RECORDING_EXTERNAL_ID_TYPES` entry (the vocabulary that column always lacked). Data-derived migration probe checks every source (no false-green). Grandfathered columns untouched — still read directly; ongoing dual-write consistency tracked as #1749.

- feat(catalogue): **Tune page keyed on the `tblTunes` registry** (#1741 P4c) — `/tune/<slug>` now reads the real registry (Subtitle/Disambiguation/Meter badge + "Tunes with this meter" cross-links, MusicBrainz/Hymnary chips, composer/arranger/harmoniser/source credits, external links) via a lookup ladder (exact slug → name-fold → alias → the original heuristic **preserved verbatim** so every existing `song.php`-emitted `/tune/` link keeps resolving, rule #33). Uncached, no inline script (rule #30). Consumes the shared external-links panel.

- feat(catalogue): **Work page + admin CRUD surface the identifiers** (#1741 P4b) — `/work/<slug>` gains breadcrumb, subtitle/disambiguation, ISWC/CCLI/BOWI chips that cross-link the P3 `/iswc//ccli//bowi/` resolver, a tune link, a first-published/copyright line, and aggregated writer/composer/arranger credits; `/manage/works` gains editable fields for all of those (+ a MusicBrainzWorkMBID field), validated through the shared `WORK_IDENTIFIER_TYPES` vocabulary (no inline regex, rule #35), persisted via one column-gated UPDATE with `TuneName`+`TuneId` kept in lockstep by the new shared `tuneFindOrCreateByName()`. Extracts the categorised external-links panel into one shared partial (was inlined per page).

- feat(catalogue): **Musician profile page + type/relations/identifier enrichment** (#1741 P4a-1/-2) — `/musician/<slug>` gains an entity **Type** (person/group/character/orchestra/other, VARCHAR-not-ENUM), an editable **Biography** (falling back to the internal Notes when empty), a **Disambiguation** line, and **"Portrayed by"/"Portrays"** relations with dates (e.g. a character persona and its performer); its IPI/ISNI identifier chips now cross-link the P3 `/ipi//isni/` resolver. `IsGroup`/`IsSpecialCase` become derived mirrors written through **one** funnel (`musicianTypeApply()`) — no other code writes them (CI-guarded). `/manage/musicians` gains the matching editor fields + a relations sub-panel (`validateCsrfRequest()`). The categorised external-links panel is now the shared partial. (The `/writer/`→`/musician/` page consolidation, P4a-3, is held pending an owner decision.)

- feat(catalogue): **browsable external-identifier alias URLs** (#1741 P3, epic #1741 / #1747) — the D5 external-ID storage (`tblSongExternalIds`, `tblWorks.Bowi`, the `media_identifiers.php` vocabulary) is now reachable by URL: `/iswc/<code>`, `/ccli/<code>`, `/bowi/<code>`, `/isrc/<code>`, `/ipi/<value>` and `/isni/<value>` all resolve through ONE normaliser (`includes/identifier_normalize.php` — a per-scheme canonical fold registry; the ISWC fold is extracted from `manage/works.php`, which now delegates, killing the duplicate strip the old page carried), ONE resolver (`includes/identifier_resolve.php` — Work-first for iswc/ccli/bowi, song multi-match for iswc/ccli/isrc reading `tblSongs.Isrc` not the identity-map's unique key, musician list for ipi/isni) and ONE page (`includes/pages/identifier.php`, which absorbed and replaced the old `iswc.php`) — not five near-copies (the modularity rule the epic called out). The resolver is table/column-existence-gated so a pre-migration install degrades to an empty result rather than throwing under mysqli STRICT. Guarded by `tests/test-identifier-routes.js` and `tests/php/test-identifier-normalize.php`, both tree-derived from the scheme registry and mutation-tested.

- fix(editor): **v2 `create_song` never recomputed `tblSongbooks.SongCount`** (#1742, finding A3 of the #1737 audit) — the v2 editor is the default, and its create handler did no count recompute at all (`grep -c SongCount manage/editor/api2.php` was 0). Two mechanisms keep that cached count honest: the #791/#793 `trg_songs_songcount_*` DB triggers and an app-level recompute — and iHymns targets shared hosting where `CREATE TRIGGER` is frequently denied, so on the actual deployment target the app-level recompute is the *only* one that exists. The result was invisible on every dev/CI/test box (they have the triggers) and wrong in production: a brand-new songbook's home tile never appeared, because the tiles gate their render on the denormalised `SongCount` and it stayed at 0. The same recompute UPDATE existed inline in three places (a modularity-rule violation), so the fix extracts one shared, `songVisibleSql()`-aware helper — `songbookRecomputeSongCount()` in `includes/songbook_count.php` — and routes `save_song_core.php`, `song_relocate.php` **and** the previously-missing `create_song` through it (post-commit, best-effort). Proven live with the three triggers dropped: a bare insert leaves the cache stale (0 vs a real count of 1), the helper corrects it with no trigger present. Guarded by `tests/php/test-songbook-count-recompute.php` (behavioural double + tree-derived wiring guards), mutation-tested four ways.

- fix(import): **rejecting an unparseable OpenSong file still opened a database connection** (#1740) — `_bulkImport_processOpenSong()` called `getDbMysqli()` + a `MAX(Number)` lookup *before* parsing, because the per-songbook auto-number was a plain `int` argument to the parser; its sibling `_bulkImport_processOpenLp()` parses first. The 5th parser argument is now a `callable $autoNumberProvider` invoked lazily — only when the document actually lacks both `<hymn_number>` and a filename-hint number — so a malformed file is rejected with no connection, matching the OpenLp shape. `tests/php/test-xml-import-routing.php` is now genuinely DB-free (proven with MariaDB stopped: all 15 assertions pass), which its doc-block had once falsely claimed.

- feat(editor): **v2 credits tab — structured First / Surname / Suffix entry** (#960) — the v2 editor could not split a credited person's name into registry parts the way v1 could, so credit writes flattened to a single string. `credit_upsert` now accepts `{name, first?, surname?, suffix?}`, and the shared `creditEntryNormalise()` / `creditPersonPromote()` pair (extracted so `credit_upsert`, revision-restore, the legacy whole-song save and lyrics-ingest all promote identically) writes proper `tblCreditPeople` registry rows. The endpoint returns the registry parts it resolved, never an echo of the client's input.

- feat(editor): **v2 cross-book counterparts** (#1608) — the five song-link actions (`song_links`, `song_link_add`, `song_link_remove`, `song_link_suggestions`, `song_link_suggestion_dismiss`) are ported from v1 into `manage/editor/api2.php`, and the Links tab gains a counterparts panel, so declaring that two `tblSongs` rows in different songbooks are the same hymn no longer requires the retiring v1 editor. Documented in `api-docs.yaml` (the v2 prose block, pending the #1201 per-action breakout) and guarded by the v1→v2 action-parity ledger in `test-editor-api2-contract.php`.

- fix(import): **a single `.xml` upload was hard-routed to the OpenLyrics parser** (#882) — so a real OpenSong document always failed with OpenLyrics' own "no `<title>`" error (OpenSong has a title, just not where OpenLyrics looks). A new `_bulkImport_processXmlAuto()` picks the primary parser via the one shared `_bulkImport_looksLikeOpenLyrics()` discriminator the ZIP-import loop already uses (so a single-file and a ZIP-entry decision can never disagree), and on a primary-parse miss tries the other parser once before giving up with a combined, both-formats-named error. Wired into both editors; guarded by `test-import-format-coverage.php` (UI list ↔ api2 set ↔ parsers) and `test-xml-import-routing.php`.

- test(annotation): **a baseline guard so the annotation program (#1158) can't regress** — `tests/php/test-annotation-coverage.php` hard-asserts, tree-derived and green today, that every source file under `includes/`, `manage/includes/` and `js/` (208 files, 100%) opens with a block doc-block, so the measured ~100% doc-block coverage can't silently erode as new files land. Labelled-register (ELI5/Why) adoption is printed as an advisory backlog signal (45%, matching the audit) and never gates the build — raw comment density is a proven-misleading proxy (template shells score low while being fully annotated), so the file-by-file backfill of the large logic files stays a human-reviewed program. Mutation-tested both ways.

- feat(intapps): **the MWBM-IntAppsAPI gateway integration lands, entirely dormant** (Epic #1725) — a server-proxied, cache-first, fail-open client for MWBM's shared feature-flag gateway, shipped with zero observable effect: `tblAppSettings.intappsapi_enabled_channels` ships with no row at all, and with it absent the whole thing is a byte-identical no-op, proved against the real local instance (six endpoints diffed merge-base-vs-branch-tip with an empty result, zero `tblIntAppsSync` references across those requests in MySQL's own query log, and three deliberate mutations — forcing the new `remoteFeatures` field to always emit, removing the enablement guard on the cache read, and pointing a capture at the extensionless `/api` route that has no router script locally — each proven to turn the relevant check red before being reverted). **The signer is the whole point of the exercise**: the gateway's own five bundled client examples sign the WRONG canonical string (`METHOD|PATH|TIMESTAMP|BODY`, filed upstream as MWBM-intAppsAPI#120) — `intappsSign()` is implemented against the gateway's actual verification source (`HmacValidator.php`, pinned commit `6816ed8`) instead, and a checked-in local stub gateway (a faithful, line-for-line port of that same source, structurally unable to ever deploy — it lives outside every directory the deploy workflow mirrors) proves the real client's signature is accepted end-to-end over real loopback HTTP, and that the vendor's own wrong examples are correctly rejected. **The pre-launch stress test's one BLOCKER** — an enabled-but-un-migrated docroot hitting the missing `tblIntAppsSync` table on the public home page under mysqli's STRICT reporting mode — is fixed with a try/catch belt over an INFORMATION_SCHEMA-probe brace on every table access, reproduced and proven live (renaming the table away under load returns a graceful degrade; removing the guard reproduces the real HTTP 500 verbatim in the server log, then the guard is restored). A second real bug was found and fixed while building the end-to-end proof: the single-flight refresh lock was held for its full 10-second window regardless of how fast the actual fetch completed, so the admin "Refresh now" button would report "another refresh in flight" for up to 10 seconds after the PREVIOUS click had already finished — fixed by releasing the lock the moment each attempt's outcome is committed, success or failure. Enablement is a per-channel allow-list (the same mechanism `apple_web_login_enabled` already uses), so alpha can canary the integration without touching production on the shared database. One real consumer ships in the same batch — the Song-of-the-Day card's presence on the home page — to prove the flag pipeline is exercised end to end, not merely renderable, per the stress test's own warning against shipping a kill switch nothing calls.

- fix(web): **three advertised features were silently dead** (#1714) — a pre-release correctness sweep hunting the silent-no-op class, and all three findings share one root cause: **#812 removed a UI element and left its consumers behind.** (1) `/` and `Ctrl`/`Cmd`+`K` did nothing at all, while both are still advertised as "Open search" in the shortcuts overlay *and* on `/help` — and because `preventDefault()` ran first, `/` did not even type a slash. (2) **On the last song of every songbook, `→` navigated backwards**: the handler selected positionally with `a:first-child` / `a:last-child`, and since the next slot rendered as *nothing* rather than a placeholder, the container held a single child — which satisfies both selectors. Users oscillated between the final two songs. (3) Pressing **Enter in the `/search` box reloaded the app and discarded the query**: the form has one field, no submit button and no `action`, which per the HTML spec's implicit-submission rules means Enter submits it, and the Enter handler was bound to the field #812 deleted. The form now also carries `action`/`method`/`name`, so the no-JS path is correct rather than destructive. Three guards added, each derived from the tree — and **two were wrong-but-green until mutation-tested**: one counted markers in raw source and was being satisfied *by its own explanatory comment*, which contained both literals it looked for; the same weakness then turned up in a second guard, where **23 ids in this tree exist only inside comments**. Also found and left as product calls: the public song page fetches `$row['tags']` on every read and never renders them (#288), and search autocomplete (#307) is fully implemented, endpoint and all, but reachable from nowhere — **with a test that has always passed, because it builds its own input and drives the function directly.** A passing test over unreachable code is the most misleading artefact in this repo.

- fix(a11y): **turning High Contrast ON made the app less legible** (#1150, #1151) — the WCAG 2.2 AA pass for the public release, and its headline finding is that the accessibility feature was *causing* the accessibility failure. `accessibility.css`'s high-contrast block only ever redefined **Bootstrap's** `--bs-*` tokens, while `app.css` carries a second parallel set (`--text-primary`/`--text-secondary`/`--text-muted`, `--glass-bg`, `--card-border`) that dozens of components read directly and which stayed at light-theme values. The bottom navigation — the most-used control in the app, on every page in every auth state — computed to **~1.8:1**, down from a compliant ~4.7:1 in ordinary light mode; every header icon to **~1.44:1**. The cascade detail is worth keeping: `.btn-header-icon` declares its *own* `color`, and an ancestor's `!important` only wins for elements the ancestor selector matches, so `.navbar { color:#fff !important }` never reached it. Fixed at the shared source rather than per-component, and scoped entirely inside `[data-ihymns-contrast="high"]` so the normal light/dark design cannot be affected. Second critical: **card reordering had no keyboard alternative at all** (2.5.7 Dragging Movements, new in 2.2) — and `enterEditMode()`'s own doc-comment claimed "Move up/down" buttons existed. `git log -p` confirms they never did, since the original #448 commit; a comment describing a feature that has never existed is worse than no comment, because it stops the next reader checking. Real Move buttons added on both layout surfaces, mirroring the already-compliant `setlist.js` pattern. Also: `home.php` had `aria-hidden="true"` over a strip hosting real focusable buttons, so a screen-reader user could land on a control announced as nothing at all; orphaned radios and checkboxes with no accessible name (a `<th>` labels a *column*, never a control); ~25 icon-only buttons relying on `title`, which is never exposed on keyboard focus; and register/reset password fields advertising `current-password`, which discourages password managers from offering to *generate* one. **#1150/#1151 stay open deliberately** — there is no browser in the build container, so every ratio is derived from the CSS cascade and no real screen reader was involved; closing them on a static pass would be exactly the false completeness this branch keeps finding.

- fix(security): **a same-origin hole the sweep was not sent to find** (#1709, #1712) — fixing #1709's non-standard-port bug surfaced a second, more serious one in the same function: because the port was dropped from `Origin` and never compared, `https://example.com:9090` matched a deployment on `example.com:443`. Port is part of an origin (RFC 6454 §4), so anything an attacker could get answering on another port of the same host inherited the ability to forge state-changing writes. The fix resolves the port against the scheme the header itself states, never one we infer — behind a TLS-terminating proxy the origin process cannot reliably tell http from https, and guessing would reject every legitimate write. Also fixed: the debug cookie was minted without `Secure` behind a TLS proxy — **the third site of a defect #1648 already fixed**, whose own comment says the two cookie helpers disagreeing "was the actual defect" (a fix applied at two of three sites is how a class survives being fixed); an incomplete shared HTML escaper; and `delete_song` using `validateCsrf()` alone, which added no security at all (the file-level gate already requires the robust check) while contributing rule #29's sporadic stale-token failure — now much more visible since #1695 widened that endpoint to editor+. Verified clean across the board: zero SQL injection over 463 interpolated sites, zero auth gaps across all 64 admin pages, zero XSS, uploads stored outside the docroot with byte-sniffed MIME and no zip-slip.

- fix(ci): **the ESLint step had linted nothing for its entire life** (#1711) — a CI step named "Lint JavaScript (ESLint)" probed for a config, found none (there never was one), and took an `else` branch running config-less `eslint@latest`, which since ESLint 9 does not lint but *crashes*. `|| exit 0` swallowed it, so the job went green. `package.json` advertised four lint scripts while eslint, stylelint and htmlhint were **none of them** dependencies. A green tick for zero checks, on every run. Now real: a bug-catching config (deliberately not a style enforcer — a mass auto-format would bury real history in `git blame` for zero correctness gain), CI fails on error, and the gate was proven able to go red. `require-atomic-updates` is off with the reason recorded, because it flagged the idiomatic disable-button-await-re-enable pattern six times, all correct code. Eight real findings fixed, one of them a trap: deleting an "unused" `const format = readInt(2)` in the MIDI parser would have **corrupted every parse**, because `readInt()` advances the cursor.

- feat(songs): **curators can delete songs again, and the widen actually takes effect** (#1695, stage 3 of epic #1692) — `delete_songs` returns to editor+. It was narrowed to admin-only in stage 1 for one stated reason — a delete was permanent and took the revision history with it — and that comment ended by promising to revisit the line once deletion became recoverable. #1694 made it recoverable, so the reason is gone. The parity suite supplied the mutation drill for free: flipping the two maps and running it **before** touching any fixture turned the E1 baseline row and MUT-6 red unprompted, which is precisely what "the suite said so, loudly, the moment the default moved" was written to mean. MUT-6 has now flipped direction for the second time, and the regression it catches is henceforth a silent *narrowing*. **The half the issue itself missed:** `effectiveEntitlements()` lets a stored override replace the code default outright and `saveEntitlementOverrides()` writes the whole map on every save, so on any install where `/manage/entitlements` has ever been saved, changing the default in PHP is a **silent no-op** — the code says editor+, the row says admin+, and the row wins. Hence a data-only migration, and it is deliberately **conditional**: in the database, "the interim value we shipped" and "the operator locked deletion down on purpose" are the same bytes, so a blanket clear would silently *widen* a privilege on a site that had decided otherwise. It prunes only the exact stage-1 value; a `global_admin` lockdown, a custom set or an already-widened value are all preserved, and unparseable JSON is reported rather than rewritten. Deletion and restore now **notify every `purge_songs` holder** from inside the write core rather than from the endpoints — a safeguard invoked by callers is one a future funnel forgets, which is exactly how #1694 found the delete cascade duplicated across two endpoints. It is post-commit and never fatal: a missed notification is a degraded safeguard, a failed delete is a broken feature. The actor is excluded, so a lone admin deleting a song notifies nobody. One bug caught before shipping: filtering the settings push-kind list server-side on `$currentUser['role']` would have hidden the switch from *everyone* including admins, because `api.php` never sets `$currentUser` (now filed as #1710) — the server states the requirement and the client, which knows the auth state, applies it.

- fix(tests): **the shared unit walker mis-named closures and minted phantom `match` units** (#1703, #1707) — `tests/php/lib/php_source_units.php` had **no test of its own** while already underpinning four suites, which is the worst possible place for an untested component: a wrong answer is invisible in every consumer at once, and each consumer reports its own green tick. Three defects, two root causes. The naming rule accepted the next `T_STRING` after `function` wherever it landed, so `function ($a, int $b)` became the unit `int` and `function (): bool` became `bool` — a name can only sit *between* `function` and its `(`. And a `match` arm's `default =>` tokenizes as `T_DEFAULT`, the same token `switch`'s `default:` produces, so the case-splitter minted a phantom unit part-way through a real case; now tracked by match brace-depth, ORed with a `=>`-vs-`:` lookahead because the two signals are independent and each alone has a blind spot. **Both issues judged these latent; they were already live on the tree** — measured before and after, 10 files minting type-named units went to zero, and `case default` units went 34 → 18, i.e. **16 phantom units removed** (`api2.php` alone 5 → 1). Nothing was producing a wrong *result*, which the issues correctly observed: where a `match` split a case, the SQL and the marker vouching for it happened to land in the same fragment. "Happened to" was the whole problem — the failure is directional, and a vouching call separated from its SQL by an intervening `match` yields a **false RED**, which per rule #34 is how guards get weakened or deleted rather than fixed. The nastiest instance is #1703's: a closure whose parameter type shares a name with a real function, **declared first**, took that function's unit name outright, so an assertion reading `$units['validate']` read the closure's body while believing it read `validate()` — no error, no warning, and which one won decided purely by declaration order. The new `tests/php/test-php-source-units.php` (27 assertions, `tests/php/lib/`'s first direct test) was written and run **red before the fix** — 10 red, each for the predicted reason, including one file emitting `case default` *and* `case default#1` where the phantom had stolen the genuine label's name. It pins the anti-over-correction cases too (a real `switch` default still mints its unit; abstract declarations don't swallow the next function; an arrow function doesn't swallow what follows), and derives its corpus from the tree so a type-named unit anywhere under `appWeb/` fails the build by name.

- feat(songs): **deleting a song is now recoverable** (#1694, stage 2 of epic #1692) — until now, Delete in the editor ran a cascade `DELETE` that took the song, its components, credits, media links, tags **and its entire revision history**, with no undo anywhere. A soft delete replaces it: five additive columns on `tblSongs` (`IsDeleted` + who / when / why / note), and `WHERE IsDeleted = 1` **is** the queue — no workflow tables, because the adversarial pass on the plan could not produce a question the row itself did not already answer. The new `/manage/deleted-songs` lists them with **Restore** (one `UPDATE`, and that genuinely is the whole recovery — a soft delete writes nothing else anywhere, so favourites, set lists, Work membership, revisions and redirect chains were never touched and need no repair) and **Purge**, the old destructive cascade, which now lives in exactly one place and is reachable **only** from the deleted state. Nothing can hard-delete a live song in one step any more, by anyone. Purge is gated on its own new `purge_songs` entitlement rather than `delete_songs` — the two are the same role set today, so the split reads as academic, but #1695 widens `delete_songs` back to editors and without a separate key the irreversible operation would silently widen with it (the alternative, a hardcoded role check, is on the review red-flag list). Purge keeps a server-enforced type-to-confirm (the #1218 §2 pattern, hardened: the client arms the button, but the POST itself must carry the typed id, so a replayed or hand-crafted request cannot skip the ceremony) and an optional relink target, chosen at purge time because that is when the redirect write actually happens. The read-path sweep is the bulk of the risk and was done as a census, not a grep, and a CI guard now re-derives it from the tree on every run rather than trusting a list anybody typed (rule #34): **109 `tblSongs`-reading units across 189 files, every one of them either filtered or explicitly marked**. 53 carry a `@deleted-visible` marker because showing hidden rows there is *correct* — `/manage/deleted-songs` itself, obviously, but also `getMissingSongNumbers()`, which deliberately counts a hidden song's number as **occupied**, because `(SongbookAbbr, Number)` on `tblSongs` is a plain index and not unique: filter there and a curator fills the "gap", someone hits Restore, and the songbook quietly has two songs on one number with nothing in the database to stop it. `admin_export` emits `isDeleted` across JSON, CSV and XML behind a column-existence probe, so an un-migrated install exports exactly what it does today. 107 assertions cover the lifecycle cores — 39 on the pure surface (SQL-fragment minting, the reason vocabulary, the one refusal verdict, and nine hostile table aliases including the `$`-vs-`\z` PCRE trailing-newline trap) and 68 across five behavioural modes run against a mysqli double: un-migrated, partially applied, migrated, purge, and the one that matters most — when the `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` probe **itself** fails, reads degrade to today's behaviour but writes **refuse**. "I could not check" must never become a write.

- test(ci): **the bare-`fetch()` guard was scanning two subdirectories and stopping at the first regex literal** (#1696, #1700, #1701) — three defects found in sequence from one small deletion. `songbookIsOfficial()` in `js/constants.js` had **zero callers anywhere in the tree** — written as the enabling helper for #1531 part 2 and never wired — so it reads to the next person as though part 2 were half-built; deleted, along with the registry field only it consumed (the API still sends `isOfficial`, so re-adding is one line when part 2 is real). It survived because `test-orphan-inventory.php` derives its corpus from dispatch surfaces, schema tables and entitlement labels, and a plain exported JS helper is none of those — whether that guard should grow an unimported-export check is now tracked as #1702 rather than left to the next audit to rediscover. Pulling that thread exposed two bugs in `test-api-client-usage.js` itself. Its scan list was `js/modules/**` + `js/utils/**`, one directory level short of the tree it means to cover, so `js/app.js` and `js/constants.js` had never been scanned at all — and `constants.js` did contain a bare `fetch()`, which **must stay one**: `/api?action=songbooks` language-filters its response, and that call populates a NAME LOOKUP, so routing it through `apiFetch()` would filter the registry to the user's chosen languages and make every list view silently fall back to the bare abbreviation for any book outside the filter. It is allow-listed with that reason recorded, so the obvious "widen the scan, fix what it finds" cannot break it. Far more serious: the guard's comment/string stripper had **no state for a regex literal**, so `replace(/"/g, …)` opened a string state that never closed and **blanked the rest of the file** — reported as zero violations, not as an error. Twelve files ended the walk broken, ten of them already in scope, including the modules that actually make API calls; the guard had reported green for a year while scanning part of each. (Checked: the newly-visible regions contained no hidden violations — the defect was the blindness, not a backlog.) Fixed with regex-literal handling plus, more importantly, a new assertion that **fails the build whenever a file's scan derails** — a heuristic has gaps, and this turns every gap into a loud failure somebody must look at instead of half a file's coverage reading as all of it. Nine new self-tests cover the quote-in-regex shapes and, equally, four division cases proving the inverse error is not made; four mutants against the real tree confirm each part can fail, including one placed in the exact position the old scanner could not reach.

- fix(songs): **an old link to a moved song can no longer land on a different song** (#1689) — looking up a song by an id that no longer exists fell back to matching on the song *number* in that songbook. Numbers are not unique in the database and a song's number can be edited independently of its id, so for a song that had been moved, merged or removed, that fallback could match whatever now occupies the number and return it as a perfectly ordinary success — the wrong song, HTTP 200, no indication anything had been substituted, and nothing in any log. The forwarding note that knew where the song had actually gone was only consulted *after* the fallback had already answered. The redirect layer is now asked first, and a claimed id skips the number match entirely so the existing forwarding path answers instead — meaning the caller still learns that the id changed (and can update what it had stored) and a removed song still reports "removed" rather than "not found". A forwarding note that says only "this was removed" counts just as much as one that points somewhere new: without that, the number match would put a different song in the removed one's place. This was always possible, but became much likelier when moving a song between songbooks started re-keying it (#1679). The extra lookup happens only on requests that had already failed to find anything, and on an installation where the forwarding table has not been created yet, behaviour is unchanged.

- feat(accounts): **closing an account disables it rather than destroying it, and owned content works that out at read time** (#1698, closes the orphan half of #1697) — an account is now one of three states: **Active**, **Disabled** (reversible, data intact) or **Erased** (an anonymised tombstone). Before this, "delete" was a hard row delete in two places, which **destroyed** the user's own set lists outright (`ON DELETE CASCADE`) while **orphaning** their published templates (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) into rows literally nobody could edit or remove without database access. Both states are now offered and, per the owner's requirement, clearly distinguished on screen — previously anything not active rendered identically as "Disabled", so an admin could not tell a reversible switch-off from an irreversible erasure. Content owned by a locked account becomes **visible but read-only** rather than vanishing: a collaborator on a disabled owner's set list keeps `canRead`, loses `canWrite`, and is told why, so third parties are not punished for somebody else's account closing. Reactivation is one `UPDATE` and every surface is correct on its next read, because the lock is **derived, never stamped onto rows** — a stamping pass leaves an account half-locked if it is interrupted, has to be exactly inverted on reactivation forever, and silently misses every new table that grows a user foreign key. The erasure path derives what to do with each referencing row from the foreign key's own `ON DELETE` rule rather than a hand-typed table list, and **refuses** rather than improvising when it meets a rule it has no policy for (`RESTRICT`, `NO ACTION`, an empty clause, or a rule from a future MySQL). Every bearer token and the stored Apple refresh token go with the account. Two entitlements arrive with it: `manage_setlist_templates` (the admin override that makes an author-less template manageable again) and `publish_public_templates`, which gates `is_public = 1` in **both** template write paths — the other half of #1697. An erasure requires the username **typed** to confirm (#1218 §2), because a mis-clicked row and the right one otherwise produce an identical "are you sure?".
