Almost none of this is new enforcement: `getAuthenticatedUser()` has always filtered `IsActive = 1` and `manage/includes/auth.php` carries thirteen more enforcement points, so both inactive states were already locked out of every authenticated path — adding a second gate would have meant two checks that must agree with nothing making them. 100 behavioural assertions, mutation-tested; the surfaces were enumerated from `schema.sql`'s `REFERENCES tblUsers` rather than guessed, and the partition over them is proven exhaustive.

- fix(editor): **one stale foreign key no longer blocks every songbook move** (#1690) — the move's cascade pre-check answered ONE schema-wide question and memoised it, so an install with a single non-cascading FK could not move a single song, even though a RESTRICT `fk_media_song` can only endanger a song that actually HAS a media row. It now refuses **per song**, only when that song really has rows in the affected child table (the owner's option A); the `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` catalogue is still read once per request, so a healthy install pays **zero** extra queries. The check was also structurally blind to the opposite drift: a column with no foreign key at all leaves no `INFORMATION_SCHEMA` row, so "everything I found cascades" was vacuously true on an install that never ran `migrate-song-softref-fks.php` — and a move then **silently stranded every `tblSongRevisions` row** for that song, with no error, no rollback and nothing to grep. A missing FK is now detected against `SONG_RELOCATE_EXPECTED_SONGID_FKS`, the 41 declarations derived from `schema.sql` and pinned to it by CI. Two migration probes were fixed with it: `songid-prefix-fixup` sat permanently in the "already applied" expander on any install with clean prefixes (so the one card that repairs those four FKs was never offered), and `song-softref-fks` keyed off a single "representative" constraint, reporting a partial apply as complete. Both now source their constraint names from the same const, so a probe, a refusal and a migration cannot drift.

- feat(push): **iHymns can now actually send a push notification** (#311 / #1671 F6) — `tblPushSubscriptions`, `push_subscribe` and `push_unsubscribe` have existed since #311 and **nothing had ever sent a push**: there was no VAPID keypair, no payload encryption and no service-worker handler, so a subscription was a permanently untested surface that read as working. All three links now exist. `includes/web_push.php` implements VAPID (RFC 8292, ES256) and Web Push payload encryption (RFC 8291 over RFC 8188 `aes128gcm`) directly on `openssl`, because this project has no Composer — and it is proven correct against **RFC 8291 §5's own published example vector**, byte for byte, rather than against itself. Messages are encrypted end-to-end: the push service that relays them cannot read the contents. Settings gains a Notifications card (on/off for this device, plus one checkbox per notification kind), `/manage/notifications` gains VAPID key generation, a broadcast form and "send a test to my devices", and the service worker gains `push` + `notificationclick` handlers that reuse an open tab instead of piling up new ones. **Entirely dormant until an operator generates a keypair**, and per-user opt-ins live in the `ihymns.push` settings namespace so adding a notification kind is one line of PHP. ⚠️ No push has yet been delivered to a real device — the cryptography is verified, the delivery path is not.

- feat(setlists): **set-list templates work, and a service plan now survives syncing** (#301 / #1671 F4) — applying a template to a set list used to produce a running order that the very next sync silently discarded, for signed-in users only; that is why the templates UI was deliberately not built in the previous pass. Set lists now keep their plan (`tblUserSetlists.SlotsJson` — run the "Set-list service plans" migration), the "From Template" menu that had been hidden and unreferenced since #301 works, each song slot is filled from a plain dropdown of the set list's own songs, and "Save as template" turns a set list you have built into a shape you can reuse. Templates can now also be **renamed and deleted by whoever created them** — previously every template, including a public one visible to everybody, was permanent and uneditable from the screen that made it. Over-long service orders are **refused** rather than silently shortened.


- feat(devices): **you can finally see — and sign out — the devices signed in to your account** (#1671 F1, remediation Batch 8) — `action=devices_list` and `action=device_signout` shipped complete and hardened in #1409/#1511 (a CSRF gate, a per-**user** rather than per-IP rate limit, and a DELETE whose `UserId = ?` sits inside the WHERE so it can only ever reach the caller's own tokens) and then had **zero callers** on web, Apple and Android alike. Somebody who signed in on a borrowed laptop had no way to revoke it. Settings → Profile now carries a "Signed-in devices" card listing each session with its platform, app version and when it was last seen, a "This device" badge on the one you are reading, and a Sign out button on every other. The current device deliberately gets **no** button — revoking it works, but it is indistinguishable from the Sign Out three inches up the page. Signing a device out is destructive, so it confirms first, announces the outcome through the shared assistive-technology announcer, and moves focus to a control that still exists rather than dropping it to `<body>` and losing a keyboard user's place. Failure kinds are told apart by **HTTP status**, never by matching the server's sentence (rule #35).

- feat(requests): **you can now find out what happened to a song you requested** (#1671 F2, remediation Batch 8) — `action=my_song_requests` has worked since #280 with **no caller anywhere**, while `song_request_submit` has always linked a submission to the signed-in user and curators on `/manage/requests` have been moving `Status` through pending → reviewed → added / declined and filling in `ResolvedSongId`. All of that was written and never read: you could send a request into silence and never learn the outcome. The `/request` page now carries a "Your requests" list below the form — each request with its status, a plain sentence saying what that status means, and a link straight to the song once one is added. It refreshes the moment a submission succeeds, so a request you just sent appears immediately rather than looking lost. Signed-out visitors get the sign-in prompt instead of a hidden section, because hiding it teaches nobody that signing in is how you track one. `Status` is a growable VARCHAR by design (rule #20), so an unrecognised value renders as itself rather than vanishing.

- feat(editor,song): **musical key, tempo and time signature are real** (#1671 F3 / #298, remediation Batch 8) — #298 shipped the `tblSongKeys` table, both endpoints, and a hidden markup block on the song page, then shipped nothing that read or wrote any of it. The table has had **no writer for its entire life**, the markup was `display:none !important` with not one line of JS referencing its ids, and `transpose.js`'s key display reads `data-key`, which `song.php` only emits `if (!empty($song['key']))` — a field `SongData` **never sets**, so that branch has never once executed in production. The v2 editor's Metadata tab now has a **Musical key** panel (a closed key list, a bounded tempo, a time-signature picker, one explicit Save) and the public song page shows the resulting `Key: Bb · 96 BPM · 4/4` badge and hands the key to the transpose controls so their +/- buttons can finally name the key you have transposed into. The block's two orphan transpose buttons were **deleted rather than wired**: they were a second, divergent copy of controls `transpose.js` already renders, and wiring them would have put two transpose widgets on one page.

- fix(api): **`song_key_save` had three guaranteed 500s and nobody had ever run it** (#1671 F3, remediation Batch 8) — building the UI for a never-called endpoint found each of them within minutes, all the same species: the handler's caps did not match the column definitions, and mysqli runs under `MYSQLI_REPORT_STRICT` so a rejected write **throws** rather than returning false. It bound **NULL into `TimeSignature`, which is `NOT NULL`** — so saving a key without a time signature, the most ordinary thing a curator can do, was error 1048 and an uncaught 500; it capped `original_key` at 10 characters for a `VARCHAR(5)` column (error 1406); and it `(int)`-cast `tempo` into an `INT UNSIGNED` column, turning `"fast"` into a stored 0 and a negative number into error 1264. An unknown `song_id` hit the foreign key and threw 1452 where a 404 belongs. All four now answer with a status and a stable machine-readable `reason`. The decisions live in the pure `includes/song_key.php`, which `tests/php/test-song-key.php` **calls** — 83 assertions, every one mutation-proven to be able to fail. The gate also stopped hardcoding `['editor','admin','global_admin']` and now asks `userHasEntitlement('edit_songs', …)`: the list was byte-identical, so no role gains or loses access today, but since #1590 an operator can retune that entitlement at `/manage/entitlements` and the hardcoded copy meant the control silently did nothing here. 401 and 403 are now distinct, because the editor has to be able to say "your admin session is not linked to the app API" rather than "access denied".

- docs(api): **the OpenAPI page described a `song_key` API that does not exist** (#1671 F3) — the GET parameter was documented `songId` (the handler reads `id`), the POST body `{songId, key}` (it reads `{song_id, original_key, tempo, time_signature}`), the response a bare string (it is an object of three fields), and both were called "the caller's preferred key", implying a per-user setting rather than one shared curator-owned value. A consumer following that page could not have made a single successful call. It drifted precisely because nothing exercised it — documentation is not a mechanism (rule #35). Corrected against the handlers, including the full `reason` vocabulary.

- refactor(settings): **ONE user-preference store, and it can now be shared with a second product** (#1671 F5, remediation Batch 7) — there were TWO stores doing the same job. `action=user_settings` writes a JSON blob on `tblUsers.Settings`; it is live, synced across devices, and fed by a strict whitelist in `js/modules/settings.js`. `action=user_preferences` / `user_preferences_sync` wrote a parallel table, `tblUserPreferences` — the same concept, the same payload shape, un-namespaced, and with **no caller anywhere in the tree** (verified by file-walk across appWeb, appApple and appAndroid, because `rg` cannot see `appWeb/.sql/`). Two stores for one concept is a data-integrity trap rather than a dormant feature: whichever a future client picks, the other holds stale truth forever with nothing to say so. The duplicate pair is **deleted** — cases and OpenAPI paths — and `tblUserPreferences` drops via a manual, `confirm=1`-gated migration card that **refuses to run unless the live table is empty**, so a row written by some out-of-repo caller is a hard stop rather than silent data loss. What survives gains the write contract that makes a shared store possible: **POST may name a `namespace`** (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{1,31}(\.[a-z][a-z0-9_]{1,31})?$` — `ihymns.web`, `ilyricsdb`, `apple`) and the server replaces **only that subtree**, leaving siblings untouched; GET accepts the same parameter to read one back. The merge is at the namespace **root and deliberately not recursive** — a deep merge makes deleting a key impossible, because the client sends the subtree without it and the merge keeps the old value, silently, forever. Caps are **413 refusals, never truncations** (the rule #1649/#1662 established the hard way), and the total-blob backstop is measured against the **result** of the write rather than its input, so an account that is already over it can still shrink itself back under instead of being trapped above the ceiling. Omitting `namespace` is byte-for-byte today's whole-blob replace, so `settings.js` and the shipped native apps are untouched — with the corollary stated in code, docs and the spec: **a namespace-less POST still clears every namespace**, so isolation holds once every writer is namespaced. The decisions live in pure functions in `includes/user_settings.php` and are pinned by `tests/php/test-user-settings-namespace.php`, which **calls them and asserts the answers** rather than pattern-matching the source — the lesson of `test-transaction-fatal.php`. Ten mutations (deep merge, sibling wipe, `$` for `\z`, case-insensitive names, cap-on-input, truncate-not-reject, `[]` for `{}`, null-for-absent, encoder flags, skipped validation) were each proven to turn it red, and three correct-but-textually-different rewrites proven to leave it green.

- fix(editor): **the songbook-move hardening, hardened — five of its own claims were true only inside the function that made them** (#1679 follow-up, remediation Batch 4b review) — an adversarial pass over the previous entry found the mechanism right and its REACH wrong in five places, each the same shape: a property established in one file, defeated in another. **The transaction-fatal re-throw now holds end-to-end.** `songRelocate()` carefully re-throws MySQL 1213 (deadlock) so the caller cannot commit a transaction InnoDB has already rolled back — but ten best-effort `catch (\Throwable)` blocks sit between the move and the commit on the two save funnels, and every one of them ate it again, so the endpoint still answered `ok: true` naming a song id that no longer existed. The two codes now live in ONE predicate, `songRelocateIsTransactionFatal()`, which opens every one of those catches. (Accurately: MySQL's default `innodb_rollback_on_timeout=OFF` rolls back only the failing statement, so the false success strictly needs 1213; 1205 is treated as fatal because with that variable ON it behaves identically.) **The v2 songbook list is the real catalogue.** It was derived from the loaded song index, so a songbook with zero songs contributed no row and a curator could not move the first song into a book they had just created — a regression against v1, which has always read the real list. `load_index` now returns it, and the control re-fills when it arrives, so a deep-linked song is no longer stuck with one option. **The refusal reaches the person who triggered it.** The cascade pre-check's message names the migration card to run, and was routed through an admin-only diagnostic field on an endpoint that admits the `editor` role — so the role most likely to hit it saw "Server error." It now travels in its own ungated `error_hint`, recognised by exception TYPE rather than by matching the sentence. **"This is the ONE mint" was false**: four other paths issue SongIds and all of them probed `tblSongs` only, so an import could still re-issue an id a live redirect claims. The claim CHECK — not the mint — is now shared (`songRelocateIdTaken()`), adopted by the v2 numberless-id allocator, the lyrics-ingest create path and the canonical-id backfill migration; the bulk-importer family is documented as not yet covered rather than half-done. Plus three small ones: the save core now derives the songbook column from ONE snapshot instead of two, the redirect-table probe has a strict mode so a broken probe cannot silently switch the claim check off, and `song_detail`'s OpenAPI path gained the `include` parameter its shared handler has always read (with the 410 body's `gone` flag now documented on both paths). Guards extended and mutation-tested: nine mutants against the PHP suite, four against the JSDOM one — including a derived check that EVERY best-effort catch in the save core re-throws first, so a new one added later is covered without anybody remembering to list it.
- fix(editor): **hardening the songbook move — it could refuse a save it should have refused early, re-issue a redirected id, strand a junction row, swallow a security-relevant failure, move a song nobody asked to move, and fire on a keystroke pause** (#1679 follow-up, remediation Batch 4b) — two adversarial reviews of the move helper found the mechanism sound and six real gaps around it. **The re-key now checks its own assumptions first:** it depends on every foreign key to `tblSongs(SongId)` being `ON UPDATE CASCADE`, which a *fresh* install satisfies but four migration-created FKs (`fk_link_song`, `fk_alt_song`, `fk_media_song`, `fk_work_song_song`) do not — and the migration that adds them shows as "not pending" on any install that never renamed an abbreviation. On such an install the move used to explode mid-transaction and roll back the **whole** save, losing the curator's lyrics, components and credits behind a generic "Failed to save song"; it now refuses before the first write and names the migration to run, the way the two existing mass-rename paths already did. **The editor's id mint no longer re-issues an id a redirect still claims:** moving the highest-numbered song out of a book frees its slot, and re-minting it would have served an old bookmark 200 OK with a *different* song — worse than the 404 the redirect exists to remove. (That first landed as one mint's private probe, and this entry originally said "the id mint" as though there were only one — see the follow-up bullet above for the four other minting paths it did not cover.) **`tblSongbookEntries`' home row now follows the song** (its `SongId` cascades, but the book, number and `IsHome` flag do not — the row was left claiming the song's home is the book it just left, with the vacated number still occupied), including the case where the song is already a member of the destination book. **The content-restriction rewrite is fatal again:** it was the one security-relevant step and the one made best-effort, so a failure silently dropped a restriction — withheld content became readable and the move committed anyway. **A deadlock (1213) or lock-wait timeout (1205) during the cached-count refresh is re-thrown** instead of logged: both roll back the entire InnoDB transaction, after which the caller happily committed nothing and reported success for a song that no longer existed under that id. **A save that never mentions the songbook no longer moves the song:** the old test compared an already-defaulted value, so a partial save re-keyed the song into `Misc`, cleared its number and wrote a permanent redirect. And in the **v2 editor the songbook is now a proper list, not a text box** — as free text with a 500 ms debounce, every keystroke pause that happened to spell a real songbook performed the whole irreversible move; it is now a `<select>` of real songbooks, saved on change, behind a confirmation that says plainly that the song gets a new id, loses its number, and leaves its old link behind as a redirect. The v1 editor also stopped writing the stale number back after a rename, which had been quietly undoing the clear on the very next save. Two new guards: `tests/php/test-song-relocate-hardening.php` (the six server-side properties, ordering included) and `tests/test-v2-songbook-move-ui.js` (the move dialog driven for real in a headless DOM); both mutation-tested, and one of them caught its own first version passing on doc-block prose.
- feat(editor): **moving a song to another songbook now re-keys its ID and leaves a forwarding address** (#1679, remediation Batch 4) — `tblSongbooks.Abbreviation` **is** the SongId prefix (rule #27): `MP-1008` means "MP, number 1008", and the PWA router, the OG-image endpoint and several API validators all parse it that way. Changing a song's songbook wrote `SongbookAbbr` and nothing else, so the song's id claimed one book forever while its column claimed another — silently, with no error and nothing to grep for. A new shared `includes/song_relocate.php` does the move properly: it mints the next free `<Abbr>-NNNN` id in the destination book, re-keys the row in **one** `UPDATE` (all 25+ FKs to `tblSongs(SongId)` are `ON UPDATE CASCADE`, so writers, components, lyric lines, media, tags, favourites, history and revisions all follow atomically — including `tblSongRedirects.NewSongId`, so redirect chains that pointed at the old id repoint themselves), clears `Number` (the owner's stated default — v1 parity, and it dodges a collision with whatever already holds that slot in the target book), rewrites the one soft reference the cascade cannot reach (`tblContentRestrictions.EntityId`, which is polymorphic and un-FK'd — leaving it behind would silently **drop** a content restriction), and writes a `tblSongRedirects` row with the new `Reason='move'` so every existing permalink, bookmark and share still resolves. `PublicId` is deliberately untouched: it exists precisely to survive a move. **Four write funnels** were wired, not the three the plan named — the fourth (the v1 editor's revision restore, which wrote `SongbookAbbr=?` in its scalar UPDATE) was found only by grepping the tree, which is the argument for deriving these lists rather than typing them. Both restore paths (v1's hand-written UPDATE and v2's `ed2_applySongSnapshot()`) now **exclude** the songbook: a restore writes scalars only, so restoring an old abbreviation would recreate the exact mismatch this fixes, as a side effect of an action aimed at the lyrics. The whole-song save reuses the existing `assignedId`/`previousId` response contract (#1380), which is why the v1 editor needed no client change at all; v2's metadata tab hands the new id back to the shell, which re-opens the song and history-replaces the `?song=` URL. On the read side, `?action=song_detail` now **follows** the redirect layer before 404-ing (the HTML fragment has done so since #1343, the JSON path never did) — it serves the current record plus `redirectedFrom`, and answers **410 Gone** for a tombstone rather than a 404, so a native app or PWA offline cache holding a moved id can learn the new one instead of failing forever. New `tests/php/test-song-relocate-funnels.php` derives every per-song songbook write in the tree from the source (three SQL shapes, on comment-stripped tokens, with book-wide abbreviation renames excluded by construction rather than by an allowlist that could go stale) and fails the build if any of them skips the helper; it was mutation-tested against the real files eleven ways, including the exact trap three previous batches shipped — a call downgraded to a doc-comment mention, which it correctly still flags.
- chore(cleanup): **remediation Batch 3 — dead code, dead settings and doc-truth fixes** (orphan inventory §§2.3/2.5/2.8/2.9/3.1/6.2, work items X1–X7/F7/F8) — the "delete it or say why it stays" half of the 2026-07-30 remediation pass, executed under the permanent orphan guard landed in Batch 1. **Deleted**: the v1 editor's `get_translations`/`add_translation`/`remove_translation` trio (`manage/editor/api.php`) — fully implemented, zero JS call sites ever, superseded by the live public `song_translations` action; `includes/pdf_export.php` (Setlist PDF Export, #302) — never `require`d, setlist printing went the browser-print route instead; `places-api.php?action=get` — its own header claimed "used by edit forms that want to re-render a chip", no edit form ever did; and the pre-#398 `setlist_schedule`/`setlist_schedule_save` legacy pair — superseded by the live `setlist_schedule_set`/`_clear`/`_current`/`_upcoming` family, whose own yaml doc had drifted from what the code actually implemented (a second, independent doc-vs-code disagreement found during verification). Each deletion took its `api-docs.yaml` path(s) and its orphan-guard allowlist entry with it in the same commit — an allowlist entry for something no longer dispatched fails the guard as "stale," so guard and code cannot drift apart. **Doc-truth**: six undocumented-dormant public actions (`song_by_identifier`, `person_by_identifier`, `songs_list`, `my_organisations`, `songs_by_tag`, `song_revisions`) plus `custom_tags`'s GET twin gain explicit "Dormant" notes in the OpenAPI spec instead of being presented as ordinary live endpoints with silently zero callers; `manage/help.php`'s API-surface summary drops the three now-deleted verbs. **Schema**: `tblSessions`, `tblUserPurchases`, `tblUserPermissions` and `tblMigrations` — schema.sql-only, no migration ever created them, no app code ever read or wrote them, a guessed 2019-vintage shape in `tblUserPurchases`'s case — are removed from `schema.sql` per owner decision D2 (default: drop all four); the existing generic "Drop Legacy Tables" card on `/manage/setup-database` picks them up automatically since it diffs the live database against `schema.sql` at runtime, so no new migration card was needed — **the drop itself is deliberately left un-run on every environment**, same as every other manual/confirm-gated destructive card. `tblUserGroupMembers` is removed the same way, alongside fixing the `user_access` action's UNION arm that read it (#1670) — that arm threw under STRICT mysqli on any migrated install, 500ing the endpoint every time; the fix repoints group lookup at `tblUsers.GroupId`, the column membership actually lives on. **Settings**: the `captcha_provider`/`captcha_site_key`/`captcha_secret_key`/`ads_enabled`/`ads_provider`/`ads_publisher_id` seed descriptions are reworded from claiming working bot-protection/advertisement features to explicit "RESERVED — not wired yet" text (a new migration reaches existing installs, since `INSERT IGNORE` never does); the `app_status` emit of all six is untouched, since removing a field a native decoder might read defensively is its own class of regression. Two gaps found during verification, not blocking, are filed rather than silently absorbed: #1685 (the `motd`/captcha/ads dead-settings family, only the captcha/ads description half fixed here) and #1686 (the retired `setlist_schedule` GET also exposed an org-wide view the live family's four actions don't replicate — `tblSetlistSchedule.OrgId` is now write-orphaned; nothing calls either pair today so no live behaviour regresses).
- feat(setlists): **no cap, explicit deletion, and an optional expiry — a set list now goes away only when you say so** (#1661) — the 50-set-list cap was never intentional and never specified; it was simply the number somebody typed, and #1649 had to work around it because the server *inferred* deletion from a set list being missing from a sync payload. Those are different facts, and conflating them is what silently ate the tail of large collections. The cap is **removed entirely** — the only limit left is a generous 4 MiB whole-body ceiling that answers **413**, a loud, retryable rejection instead of an invisible, permanent truncation. Deletion becomes something a client **says**: a new `deleted: [id, …]` list writes a permanent tombstone (`tblUserSetlistTombstones`), and **the presence of that key — even as `[]` — is what marks a protocol-2 client**, for which absence-based deletion is retired outright. A tombstoned id is dead **forever**; re-creating mints a fresh client id. That rule is deliberately deterministic rather than a timestamp comparison against the incoming `updatedAt`, because a client clock is not evidence — a device running fast would otherwise resurrect everything it had ever deleted. Separately, each set list may now carry an **optional expiry** (`tblUserSetlists.ExpiresAt`, `NULL` = never expires, which is what every existing set list gets); the **server** converts an expired set list into a tombstone with `reason: "expired"` on the next read, so expiry and deletion propagate through one mechanism and two devices with different clocks can never disagree mid-service. **Native app sync behaviour is completely unchanged** — the Apple and Android clients never send `deleted`, so they stay on the legacy, #1649-guarded path, and their decoders ignore the added response fields. ⚠️ The migration is **web-run**: apply "Setlist tombstones + optional expiry" on `/manage/setup-database` for each environment after deploy; until then every new read is existence-gated and behaviour is byte-for-byte what it is today.
- fix(licensing): **organisation CCLI licences did not work at all — one resolver now answers "does this user hold a valid CCLI licence?"** (#1668) — the question had three answers that disagreed, and the organisation half was severed at the storage layer rather than merely ignored. Every org-licence UI that ships (`/manage/organisations`, `/manage/my-organisations`, the `org_admin_licence_add/_change/_remove` API trio) writes `tblOrganisationLicences`; `getUserEffectiveLicences()` read that table **zero** times, consulting instead `tblContentLicences` (which has no writer anywhere in the codebase, so it is permanently empty) and the legacy `tblOrganisations` columns. A church that entered its CCLI number the only way the site offers contributed nothing to its members' access — and nothing to the Service-Mode Phase-3 congregant unlock, which read the legacy columns alone. Meanwhile the personal half forked three ways: `licences.php` accepted any non-empty `CcliNumber`, while `content_gating.php` and `api.php`'s `tier_check` each ran their own copy of one SELECT that also demanded `CcliVerified` — so two gates could return opposite answers about the same user and the same song. New `userHasValidCcli()` is the single resolver; the two duplicate queries are deleted and both call sites delegate to it. **Validity is deliberately FORMAT-ONLY**: CCLI publishes no verification API and no check-digit algorithm, so nothing here can set `CcliVerified` truthfully at scale and requiring it would deny essentially every genuine licence holder. A number must be all digits and of normal length, judged by the existing `validateCcliNumber()` — the one place that rule lives. `CcliVerified` is kept, still written, and surfaced as an advisory `verified` field, but it gates nothing. **Nobody loses access**: the personal path is not tightened, org members who were wrongly excluded now pass, and `tier_check` accordingly returns org-derived allowances the native apps can see. All of it stays inert while `content_gating_enabled='0'`. The dead `ccli_validation_enabled` setting — whose seeded description called it the CCLI enforcement switch while nothing read it — is removed, with a migration so existing installs shed the false text that an `INSERT IGNORE` seed could never reach. New `tests/php/test-ccli-resolver.php` exercises the decision logic directly and then derives from the source tree that neither the licence query nor the CCLI format rule has a second copy; it was mutation-tested against the real files, which caught two of its own assertions passing because a doc-comment happened to contain the string they searched for.
- feat(editor): **`/manage/editor/` now serves the v2 Song Editor** (#1601 scope item 2) — `manage/editor/index.php` becomes a 302 redirect to the v2 shell, forwarding the query string verbatim, so all six places that link into the editor with instructions in the URL (the admin nav, the dashboard card, Revisions' "Open in editor", Missing Numbers' prefill, Duplicate Songs' link opener, and the public song page's Edit button) land on v2 without each needing its own change; the browser carries a URL fragment across the redirect on its own. 302 rather than 301 so the decision stays reversible server-side instead of being cached indefinitely by browsers that already visited once. Three escape hatches: `?legacy=1` per-visit (also what editor2.php's own "Legacy" button now links to), `tblAppSettings.editor_v2_default='0'` fleet-wide with no migration needed (an absent key means v2, and any DB error while checking it still resolves to v2 rather than throwing on the way in — flippable from a new "Song Editor" card on `/manage/configuration`, not only via a raw SQL statement), or reverting the commit. v1 is deliberately **not** retired (scope item 3) — nothing on this branch was runtime-verified (no MySQL, no browser exist in this environment) and retirement also needs cross-branch coordination, since beta and main share one database and are a month behind on unrelated history. Ships only once every parity gap the #1601 audits found was closed and the P0 write-blocker below was fixed.
- fix(editor): **v2 editor could not write anything — every save returned 403** (#1677) — api2.php rejects any POST lacking `X-Requested-With: XMLHttpRequest` (the same-origin CSRF gate, #1307); v2's own API client sent that header on reads only, never on either write helper. So every mutation — save, create, delete, component upsert/reorder, metadata/credits/tags/links/media writes, media upload, bulk verify/tag, revision restore, the per-line enrichment endpoints — returned "Cross-site POST blocked" the instant the v2 UI touched anything, while browsing and loading worked fine (all GETs). It survived unnoticed because v1 was still the default editor and v2's write paths were barely exercised. Fixed client-side rather than by loosening api2's gate. New `tests/php/test-editor-api2-contract.php` derives both the client's header usage and every action literal it sends from source and cross-checks them against api2.php's real cases, so a future typo'd action name or a dropped header fails the build instead of failing silently.
- feat(editor): **v2 gains a chords editor, an arrangement editor, and per-line translation/annotation panels** (#1627) — the last day-one parity blockers before the cutover could ship. v1 persisted the song's running order (`ArrangementJson`) through its whole-song save; v2's granular per-field save model had no endpoint that touched the column at all, so flipping the default would have frozen every existing arrangement as read-only fossil data. A new `arrangement_update` api2 action plus a shared `includes/arrangement.php` validator fix that — used by both the writer and the #1618 cutover-gate auditor, so the two rules can never silently diverge. The client editor ports v1's pool/strip UI onto move-left/move-right buttons instead of drag-and-drop, sidestepping another unpinned SortableJS CDN load (#1587/#1647) and fixing the same keyboard/touch gaps #1644 closed elsewhere. Chords and per-line language/translation/annotation editing land in the Structure tab, anchored on `tblLyricLines.Id` (rule #21) via a `lineIds` field the editor's read shape had silently been dropping — without it, per-line enrichment had endpoints and storage but no UI could ever reach them. Two opposite-shaped clearing traps in `component_upsert` (an empty chords textarea must send `[]`, not `null`; once per-line languages becomes an array it must never be re-sent as `null`) are both covered by new tests, and both new panels' failure-state UI was rewritten to branch on HTTP status codes rather than pattern-matching the server's error prose — closing a "two things that must silently stay in step" gap of the same shape as #1581.
- feat(editor): **v2 gains `?tab=` deep-links, the sidebar songbook filter + 3-way sort, `bulk_tag_detach`, and the per-format export lines-per-slide option** (#1628) — restoring v1 controls v2 had dropped. `manage/revisions.php`'s "Open in editor" link carries `?tab=history`, which v2 ignored entirely — reintroducing, on the very button #1623 had just fixed, the identical silent no-op that fix removed. Plain text search with a 300-row cap couldn't express "browse songbook X in number order," so the songbook filter and v1's three sort orders return, sharing v1's numeric-not-lexical comparators so the two editors never disagree on ordering. Bulk tagging was add-only in v2 (v1 offered add/remove in one action); `bulk_tag_detach` restores the ability to undo a bad bulk-tag without touching songs one at a time. The export "lines per slide" control was silently wired to a literal 0, so no format ever split a long verse across slides; it now reads live at export time and shares v1's localStorage key so an operator's projector preference survives the cutover. Also documents, rather than hides, one genuine behaviour difference: v2's revision Restore returns to the state a revision row recorded *after* the edit, while the legacy editor's Restore undid the edit and landed one revision further back — both the confirm dialog and the tab's intro copy now say so.
- feat(editor): **v2 honours every deep link other pages already emit** (#1680) — `?songbook=`/`#number=` (Missing Numbers' prefill) and the `?open=` alias. Found while preparing the route flip: `manage/missing-numbers.php` links "Open in editor" with a songbook + number in the URL fragment, which v2 had no handler for at all — the third instance of this exact bug shape, each caught only by hand after the previous one shipped (#1623 fixed `?open=`, #1628 fixed `?tab=`), because an API diff finds missing endpoints and a UI diff finds missing screens — neither enumerates the URLs other pages already point at the editor. An existing (songbook, number) pair now opens rather than silently minting a duplicate song on the same number. New `tests/test-editor-deep-links.js` derives every `/manage/editor/?…` link in the tree and every parameter it carries, then checks each against what the v2 shell actually reads — so a new deep link added anywhere in the app is covered automatically instead of needing its own follow-up fix.
- fix(editor): **de-orphan four v1 `api.php` consumers ahead of retirement** (#1629) — callers outside the editor UI that had nothing to do with the v1/v2 cutover but would have broken invisibly the moment v1 is removed. Content Restrictions' name-first picker (`user_search`/`org_search`, never actually an editor feature) and the admin Songbooks bundle-export button are repointed at their api2/public equivalents; the global bulk-import progress poller's stale-job fallback is repointed at api2's status endpoint; and `/manage/duplicate-songs` gains its own CSRF'd, `edit_songs`-gated `unlink` action so removing a `tblSongLinks` row no longer requires "unlink it in the editor first" — a dead end once v1 retires — clearing the way for #1608's editor-side suggestions panel to retire too.
- feat(editor): **`easyworship_export` gets its own api2 endpoint** (#1678) — the v2 editor's own Export menu called v1's `?action=easyworship_export`, which had no v2 equivalent; retiring v1 as planned would have silently broken a button that lives inside v2 itself. The RTF-building helpers are extracted verbatim into a shared `includes/easyworship_export.php` that both api.php and the new api2 case call, so v1 and v2 stay byte-identical while v1 still exists.
- fix(security): **one shared emitter for the Bootstrap CDN tags — four pages had drifted to three pinned versions, two with no SRI at all** (#1676) — the v2 editor's bespoke `<head>` (`editor2.php`, promoted to the default editor by this same branch), `import2.php`, and the public sign-in gate were all loading Bootstrap CSS/JS from a CDN with no `integrity` attribute, while the shared registry, `channel_gate.php`, and both editors disagreed on which version was even pinned. A new `includes/bootstrap_assets.php` centralises the `<link>`/`<script>` markup — deliberately the smallest reusable unit rather than a whole shared `<head>`, since the existing `head-libs.php` bundle is too coarse for a page that wants only these two tags — and `bootstrap_icons` joins `APP_CONFIG['libraries']`, with `download-vendor.sh` now fetching the icon font files as well as the CSS (a CSS-only vendored fallback would otherwise silently render every icon as an empty box). `tests/test-vendor-sri.js` was rewritten to derive the set of pages needing coverage from the tree instead of a hardcoded list, and found two more affected pages than the originating issue named.
- feat(import): **iHymns interchange JSON importer, writing straight to the database** (#1633) — additive/merge only, never truncating, following the same contract as the existing ZIP importer rather than reviving the retired `migrate-json.php`. Routes through the existing `_bulkImport_upsertSongbook()`/`_bulkImport_saveSong()` pair, so it inherits the "existing rows are sacred" contract and the normalised lyric-line write path (rule #25) for free. `.json` was already claimed by the VideoPsalm importer, so a content sniff (all three iHymns top-level keys present, VideoPsalm's capital-`Songs` key absent — case-sensitive, so a false positive is structurally impossible) redirects a genuine interchange file onto the new path; anything ambiguous stays on the old one. Enforces its own 8 MiB pre-decode ceiling rather than trusting the existing 25 MB cap, since `json_decode()` is not a streaming parser and a decoded JSON graph was measured at roughly 5.8× the file's bytes on this corpus — the same class of OOM risk as #929. Validation is all-or-nothing before any transaction opens, since a malformed interchange file means the file itself is broken, not that one hymn is unusual.
- fix(tools): **repair `npm run build:proto`** (#1634) — three faults. The script pointed at the now-retired legacy editor's proto directory, the same stale-path defect #1631 had already fixed in a sibling script but missed here; the documented `npm run build:proto` command was never actually registered in `package.json`; and the tool's protobuf JSON output was non-deterministic (protobufjs doesn't guarantee stable key order), so regenerating the bundle produced a ~3,300-line diff that was pure reordering noise around any real schema change — exactly the moment a genuine delta most needs to be reviewable. Output is now sorted recursively and verified byte-identical across two consecutive runs; the committed bundle is renormalised once, verified semantically unchanged first.
- feat(setlist): **finish collaboration — notifications, the shared-with-me list, and enforced permissions** (#1638) — collaboration had shipped write-only: an owner could invite someone by email, pick view/edit and see them appear in the modal, but the invitee got nothing. No notification was created, the invitee-facing endpoint had zero callers on any platform, both listing endpoints were owner-only, and `Permission` (view/edit) was never read anywhere — the picker was decorative. The legacy `setlist_collaborators`/`_collaborator_add`/`_collaborator_remove` trio (unfixably broken — it referenced columns that never existed on `tblSetlistCollaborators` and always threw under mysqli's strict mode) is removed from both `api.php` and `api-docs.yaml`. A new shared `includes/setlist_collab.php` resolver answers "may this viewer read/write somebody else's setlist," failing closed on an unrecognised `Permission` value or an ambiguous lookup; collaborative writes go through a single targeted-UPDATE endpoint that structurally cannot delete or create a setlist in the owner's namespace, and shared setlists are held client-side in memory only, never written to the localStorage array the per-user sync payload is built from — closing the same class of cross-account data-loss risk #1649 fixes elsewhere. `setlist.js` gains a "Shared with me" section and a `?shared=` deep link so the new notification's action URL actually lands somewhere.
- fix: **sync the PHP/JS entitlement maps; honour `song_requests_enabled`; fix the bare `?id=` deep-link on Credit People** (#1641) — the JS entitlement mirror had drifted 13 keys behind the PHP source of truth, latent today only because nothing in the JS client currently gates on any of the missing keys — the failure mode is a client-side control that would silently never render for someone actually granted it. `song_requests_enabled`/`max_song_requests_per_day` were seeded in `schema.sql` and honoured only by the older Apple-only `song_request` endpoint; the web's `song_request_submit` read neither and hardcoded a limit of 5, so disabling requests via the admin setting did nothing for the web — the same "documented control that doesn't control" shape as #1668. And the public person page's "Edit this person" button linked `/manage/credit-people?id=<Id>`, which the admin page read only inside an AJAX branch, so a bare `?id=` landed on the unfiltered, unscrolled full list with nothing selected or explained.
- fix(schema): **`ccli_validation_enabled`'s seeded description asserted a licensing control that doesn't exist** (#1668) — nothing in the codebase reads the setting; the real controls are `content_gating_enabled` plus `require_licence:ccli` restriction rows (rule #28). Unlike the rest of the same audit's other dormant, do-nothing settings, this one doesn't merely do nothing — it reads like the master CCLI-enforcement switch, so an operator who set it to `1` would reasonably believe copyright enforcement was live. The seed description now says plainly that the key is unwired and names the real controls; the row is left in place (an `INSERT IGNORE`, so existing installs keep the old text regardless) pending a product decision on whether a distinct toggle is wanted at all.
- fix(security): **admin gates now derive from the same entitlement map the nav advertises; the `/manage` session cookie honours `X-Forwarded-Proto`; `setlist_get` is rate-limited** (#1648) — eight admin pages (three more than the originating audit found, caught only because the fix derives the nav-to-gate pairing from `admin-links.php` instead of hardcoding it) were gated on hardcoded roles while the nav advertised admin-editable entitlements; narrowing an entitlement at `/manage/entitlements` therefore looked like it revoked access while every admin-role user could still reach the page by typing the URL. Behind a TLS-terminating proxy, `$_SERVER['HTTPS']` is unset even on a genuine HTTPS request, so the `/manage` session cookie could be minted without `secure` — it now checks `X-Forwarded-Proto` the way `includes/auth_cookie.php` already did. And the unauthenticated `setlist_get` share-link lookup had no rate limit against its 32-bit share ID; it's now throttled at 120/min like `search`.
- fix(a11y): **Present mode is a real modal dialog; Service Mode section changes are announced instead of silent; the scroll-to-song race is gone** (#1646 items 1 & 3, #1665) — Present mode's fullscreen overlay was a plain `<div>`, so Tab moved a keyboard user onto page content hidden behind it and nothing signalled a screen reader that anything had opened; it now carries `role="dialog"`/`aria-modal`, traps focus, and announces each slide change by its label. A congregant following a live service heard nothing when the leader changed sections — a purely visual scroll with no non-visual equivalent — and the scroll also ignored `prefers-reduced-motion`, since `scrollIntoView`'s `behavior` option isn't overridden by the reduced-motion CSS. The scroll-to-song trigger also fired on a fixed 600ms timer after navigation, so on a slow connection or a long song the elements it queried for hadn't rendered yet and the congregant silently stayed at the top of the page; it's now a bounded wait for the elements to exist, cancelled if a newer broadcast supersedes it.
- fix(a11y): **sortable admin table headers stay `columnheader`s** (#1646 item 2) — `th.setAttribute('role', 'button')` replaces a `<th>`'s implicit `columnheader` role rather than adding to it, breaking the header/data-cell association for a screen reader across all thirteen opted-in admin lists (#842/#844) and, since `aria-sort` is only valid on `columnheader`/`rowheader`, silently disabling the sort-state announcement it was meant to add. Fixed per the ARIA APG table pattern — a real `<button>` nested inside the `<th>`, leaving the header cell's own role alone.
- fix(security): **SRI + a vendored fallback for the SortableJS CDN load** (#1647) — the card-layout drag-reorder feature loaded SortableJS from jsDelivr with no `integrity` attribute despite being pinned to an exact version; the comment explaining why turned out to record that a placeholder hash had been committed once, had silently broken the whole reorder feature, and someone had removed the integrity check to get it working again rather than fixing the hash. The vendored same-origin fallback is what makes keeping SRI safe this time — a bad hash now degrades to a working local load instead of a dead feature, removing the incentive that caused the original regression.
- fix(a11y): **SPA navigation no longer reads the entire new page aloud** (#1645) — `<main>`, where the router injects every page fragment, was `aria-live="polite"`; each navigation therefore queued the whole new page — breadcrumb, toolbar, every lyric line — for announcement, competing with the router's own focus move onto that same element. Replaced with a one-line "you have landed on X" announcement (focus first, then announce, since a live region queues behind speech already in progress) via a new shared `js/utils/announce.js`, extracted from a private announcer `setlist.js` had already built for itself because the global live region had never actually worked for it.
- fix(a11y): **the setlist Arrangement editor is usable by keyboard and touch, and its chip names contain their visible text** (#1644) — every chip was a `<span role="button">`, which has no native activation behaviour, so Enter/Space did nothing despite the chip being focusable and announced as a button; reordering was wired only through HTML5 drag-and-drop, which doesn't fire from touch input on mobile — the device a worship leader arranging a song live would actually use. Chips are now real `<button>` elements with explicit move-left/move-right controls alongside drag-and-drop as an enhancement, with focus explicitly restored to the moved chip after each re-render. Separately, chip `aria-label`s used the unabbreviated section name ("Verse 1, position 2 of 5") over visible text reading "V1" — a WCAG 2.5.3 Label-in-Name failure that breaks voice control — now leads with the visible abbreviation.
- fix(sync): **stop capped per-user syncs deleting the tail of a user's collection; add a sync watermark; warn on truncation** (#1649) — the three per-user sync handlers (set lists, favourites, custom tags) capped an incoming payload with `array_slice()` and then, in replace mode, deleted every server row absent from that already-truncated list — so a user with 60 set lists who synced 50 had the other 10 permanently deleted on the very next auto-sync, with nothing logged and nothing shown. A new shared `includes/user_sync.php` makes a truncated payload skip deletion entirely (it degrades to a merge, since a clipped payload can't assert what doesn't exist) and adds an optional `since` watermark so an older device can no longer delete another device's newer, unseen rows. Client-side, the PWA now sends that watermark, absorbs every sync response instead of discarding it, fixes two amplifiers (an offline-drain overwrite that could wipe the local cache too, and a hardcoded `'replace'` on setlist-sharing that bypassed the reconcile gate every other caller respects), and toasts once per session when the cap actually bites.
- fix(a11y): **high-contrast and colour-vision-deficiency modes were dead across the entire `/manage` admin surface and both editors** (#1643) — the CSS keyed on the `data-ihymns-contrast`/`data-ihymns-cvd` attributes that `admin-theme-init.php` correctly stamps on `<html>` existed only in the file loaded by the public site's shell, so every admin page announced the intent (the attributes were set) and shipped none of the styling to honour it; a curator who picked High Contrast or a CVD mode simply got plain dark theme, with no error anywhere. A new admin-surface CSS block fixes the stylesheet's absence; a hardcoded six-songbook CVD badge selector list (which matched a class nothing in the codebase actually emits) is re-keyed onto the real markup with a generic fallback so a songbook outside the named six still gets a non-colour cue. The audit that found this derived the "which pages need the stylesheet" list from the tree rather than hardcoding it, and caught two more affected pages than the originating report named.
- feat(setlist): **playback mode — tap a song to start, a fixed floating nav bar, arrow-key navigation** (#1533) — extends the #94 setlist-navigation machinery (prev/next, position, disabled ends) rather than building a parallel component. Fixes the bug under the feature: `getNavigation()` looked the list up via `getById()` in `getAll()`, so a SHARED setlist (which has no local record) could never be navigated at all, however it was reached. Replaced with a playlist CONTEXT — `STORAGE_PLAYLIST_CONTEXT`, sessionStorage, deliberately not localStorage, since "I am working through this list" belongs to this tab's session and localStorage would resurrect last Sunday's service the next time someone opened a song and would leak the context between tabs — that carries its own song order, so a shared list and an own list navigate identically; the old `activeSetListId` path survives as the fallback since `applyCustomArrangement()` still reads it. Arming is now on TAP from both the own-list detail and the shared-list view, not only the "Use" button most people never pressed. The bar is now fixed-position on `<body>` rather than an in-flow alert after the breadcrumb (the old one scrolled away exactly when a musician mid-service needs Next) — which meant `renderSongNavigation()` had to tear down BEFORE any early return, since a fixed element doesn't vanish when the SPA swaps page content and the router calls it on every navigation, not just song pages. Also: next-song title, "3 of 12", an explicit exit, arrow-key navigation (bound once, refusing to steal keys from text entry, comboboxes or modifier combos), and an aria-live announcement (the SPA changes the song under you with no document load, so a screen reader otherwise gets silence); `body.has-playlist-bar` reserves padding so the bar can't cover the final lyric line. New `tests/test-setlist-playback.js`, 22 assertions.
- fix(admin): **Revisions Audit "Open in editor" now actually opens the song** (#1623) — `manage/revisions.php` linked to `/manage/editor/?open=<id>`, but `editor.js` reads `?song=` — the established convention (#407) every other `/manage/editor/` link uses — so the editor loaded with nothing selected and no error. Fixed the link and kept `open` as an alias so a bookmarked link starts working instead of failing the same invisible way. Also wires `?tab=history` to the existing `openHistoryModal()`, which nothing previously called from a URL, so the History button's tooltip promise is now true.
- fix(ci): **pass the commit message to `version-bump.yml` as an env var, not shell source** (#1622) — a commit message is attacker-controllable text; interpolating it with `${{ }}` directly into a `run:` body pastes it into the script BEFORE the shell parses anything, so its contents become script source. Found the benign way: the #1611 stopgap commit's message quoted `"DRY RUN — nothing has been changed"`, whose embedded double quote closed the assignment early and the shell took the next word as a command, failing the Version Bump job on beta (exit 127). The same mechanism with a backtick or `$(…)` executes arbitrary code on a runner holding `contents: write`. Fixed with the GitHub-documented pattern — the value arrives as an environment variable the shell never re-parses.
- refactor(web): **migrate every call site to the shared client and DELETE the `window.fetch` monkey-patch** (#1031, second and final commit) — `search.js`, `song-of-the-day.js`, `settings.js` and `router.js`'s four page/data fetches now call `apiFetch`; the global `window.fetch` override `songbook-language-filter.js` installed is gone. Two files deliberately NOT migrated, verified rather than assumed: `place-search.js` is a classic `<script src>` on seven admin pages with zero import/export statements (a dynamic `import()` broke its keyboard test under jsdom), and it turns out `places-api.php` never reads `X-Preferred-Languages` — the premise for migrating it was simply wrong; `service-broadcast.js` has no direct fetch calls at all, its network access goes through an injected `apiCall()` closure. `search.js`'s two hand-rolled `EVT_FETCH_FAILED`/`EVT_FETCH_SUCCEEDED` dispatches are removed — it used to be the only module signalling connectivity by hand, but `apiFetch` now dispatches on every request, so keeping them would double-fire the offline indicator's listeners. All 19 JS suites pass; `requestUrlOf()` and its #1593 regression test move to the new module intact.
- feat(web): **add the shared API client `js/utils/api-client.js`** (#1031, first of two commits) — foundation for retiring the `window.fetch` monkey-patch; this commit is INERT, nothing imports it yet. `apiFetch`/`apiFetchJson` centralise per-request headers (`X-Preferred-Languages`, `X-Requested-With`, injected auth) that `songbook-language-filter.js` previously solved by replacing `window.fetch` globally — a pattern that had already hit two failure modes: a bug in the header logic became a failed REQUEST wherever the caller happened to catch it (#1593 — reading `input.url` on a `URL` object returns `undefined`, breaking ten unrelated call sites), and the patch only applied if `bootSongbookLanguageFilter()` had installed it, which the router imports only on home/songbooks/settings — so on a cold load of `/search` an anonymous user's saved language filter was silently ignored (not previously reported; found while scoping this issue). `apiFetchJson()` also detects the #1566 SPA-catch-all-returns-200-HTML trap and raises a clear error instead of a `SyntaxError` from `.json()`. Auth headers arrive through an injected `setAuthHeaderProvider()` rather than an import, avoiding a `user-auth.js` ↔ `api-client.js` load-order cycle. `STORAGE_LANGUAGE_FILTER` joins `constants.js`, keeping its pre-convention key spelling on purpose since it is already in real users' browsers.
- fix(security): **close eight pre-gating security gaps before the flag can be flipped** (#1388) — every change here is a verified no-op while `content_gating_enabled='0'` on all three docroots; they matter because they stand between the flag and a leak on the day it flips. The theme: payload gating is not asset gating. New `contentGatingMediaAllowed($kind, $userId, $presenceToken)` in `includes/content_gating.php` gates media BYTES, mirroring `contentGatingApply()`'s kind→cap mapping so the two can never diverge — `/song-media/<id>` (`song-media.php`) now applies it as a second gate alongside the existing entity check (403 on denial, previously no tier cap at all), and the `bulk_audio` offline manifest resolves `play_audio` once per requester and empties the manifest when denied. `songbook_export` (`api.php`) previously carried an entity gate only — the widest lyric leak in the API, since one request returned every surviving song's full `components` regardless of tier — and now maps each song through `contentGatingApply()` exactly like `song_detail`. `song-media.php` also now passes the Service-Mode presence token (the 5th parameter has existed since #1335 but was never wired), so a present congregant is no longer denied media that `song.php` deliberately grants them. Service-Mode's `serviceMode_presenceCcliNumber()` now requires a heartbeat inside the freshness window, not just `IsActive` — an operator's shut-down-without-clean-end laptop previously left a standing CCLI unlock live for everyone who had ever joined. First-admin registration TOCTOU closed on both paths (`auth_register` and the magic-link path) with a transaction + `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, so two registrations racing on a virgin install can no longer both become `global_admin`. Logout now clears per-user service-worker caches (a new `CLEAR_USER_CACHES` message) so the next user of a shared device isn't served the previous user's fragments — deliberately spares the offline-download/media caches, which are deliberate user intent. `validateCsrfRequest()` no longer accepts `X-Requested-With` alone when both `Origin` and `Referer` are absent (per the Fetch spec, browsers send `Origin` on every non-GET, so absent-both means "not a browser POST"); the rejection is logged. `test-gating-noop` extended with 15 assertions pinning the new media gate's off-behaviour per kind; the full existing CI suite passes. `/manage/gating-noop-verify` against the live DB remains owner-gated.
- cleanup(web): **delete dead `js/utils/transpose.js`; correct the lyrics-cutover gate count** (#1612, #1615, #1618) — the app's real transposer is the unrelated `js/modules/transpose.js`; the dead file had zero importers anywhere except its own header example, a doc comment in `format-export.js`, and — the dangerous one — the service-worker precache list, removed in the same commit (this SPA's catch-all `.htaccess` answers an unmatched path with 200 + the HTML shell, so `cache.addAll()` over a deleted path would have silently cached an HTML page under a `.js` URL, the same failure class as #1566). Separately, `verify-lyrics-cutover.php`'s header and the drop migration both claimed "10"/"G1–G13" gates; enumerating the actual `gate()` registrations found NINE implemented (G1, G2, G3, G5–G10). Each of the four missing IDs was traced rather than assumed folded in: G11 is correctly split across this verifier and the drop migration's Stage 0(b); G13 is subsumed by G2 (which already compares `ChordsJson`); the ArrangementJson-ordinal half of G4' and the G12 Id-stability smoke are implemented nowhere — a substantive gap in the drop's precondition, tracked separately as #1618 rather than papered over here.
- fix(deploy): **What's New no longer truncates mid-sentence** (#1589) — `deploy.yml` built `public_html/data/whats-new.md` from the first THREE `## ` sections of `CHANGELOG.md` under a 48 KB byte cap, but `## [unreleased] — alpha` had grown to 70,627 bytes on its own, so `head -c` cut *inside section one*: testers saw a single giant blob, truncated mid-word, that never reached a second release. A section-count budget cannot bound output size, because a section has no bounded size. The extractor is now ENTRY-based — at most 10 top-level bullets per release, 5 release headings (counted on headings actually EMITTED, so an empty `## [unreleased] — alpha` never burns a slot), 50 entries overall — with the byte budget checked *before* an entry is written, so the output always ends on an entry boundary instead of mid-sentence. Headings print lazily, only when one of their entries is emitted, so a release with nothing under it leaves no orphan heading. `head -c` + `iconv -f UTF-8 -t UTF-8 -c` are kept as backstops (`markdown_lite.php`'s `ENT_SUBSTITUTE` depends on never being handed a partial UTF-8 sequence), and `LC_ALL=C` pins `length()` to bytes rather than characters. The real changelog now yields 17,278 bytes across 5 releases instead of 49,152 bytes across 1.
- feat(ci): **version bumps now close the changelog's unreleased section** (#1589) — `version-bump.yml` renames `## [unreleased] — alpha` to `## [<newversion>] — <date>` and reopens a fresh empty `## [unreleased] — alpha` above it, staged in the **same commit** as the version bump so #1596's post-bump deploy dispatch actually ships it. This bounds section size permanently and makes the What's New page self-healing rather than degrading with every entry. Four skip-not-fail guards make it idempotent: `CHANGELOG.md` must exist; exactly one line must equal `## [unreleased] — alpha` verbatim (`grep -xF`, case-sensitive — the file also holds historical `## [Unreleased]` headings a loose match would hit); that section must hold at least one top-level `- ` entry (a re-run finds it empty and no-ops, so no empty version heading is ever emitted); and `## [<newversion>]` must not already exist. The rewrite lands in a temp file whose post-conditions (non-empty, one unreleased heading, one new-version heading, exactly one more `## ` than before) are re-verified before it replaces the original. Alpha-only, because `changelog.yml` rewrites the same file on beta; the retry loop now aborts a conflicted rebase instead of burning its remaining attempts against a half-rebased worktree. New guard `tests/test-whats-new-extraction.js` lifts the awk program **out of `deploy.yml`** and runs it against the real `CHANGELOG.md` (13 assertions, no copied logic), and is proven to fail against the pre-fix workflow.
- perf(api): **stop `bulk_songs` re-hydrating a whole songbook once per song** (#1598) — a comment claimed the bulk-download loop avoided per-song hydration; it didn't, because `includes/pages/song.php` unconditionally re-fetched and clobbered the injected song, then called `getSongs($songbook)` — a full book hydration — purely to compute prev/next, once per song in the loop. For a 3,517-song book that's roughly 3,517 whole-book hydrations in one request, which is why downloading a large songbook for offline use timed out or exhausted memory while small books worked fine. Fixed additively (an injected song/prev/next now survive) with the single-song path asserted unchanged. Prerequisite for #1597's offline-download fixes below to be worth anything.
- fix(offline): **seven verified defects in bulk offline downloads, several destroying data while displaying success** (#1597) — bulk-downloaded songs and recently-viewed songs shared one 2,000-entry cache budget, so opening a single song after downloading a 3,517-song book trimmed roughly 1,500 downloaded entries as if they were merely "recent"; the service-worker's cache keep-list never protected the audio cache, so every deploy silently deleted every downloaded audio file; offline navigation fallback existed only for song pages, so a perfect download of anything else 503'd; eviction and size-reporting used the wrong URL shape and silently missed files; a song's progress bar could hang forever from a count mismatch between per-song and per-book totals; `navigator.storage.persist()` was never called, leaving Safari free to evict a whole download after seven days; and the Settings "include audio offline" toggle dispatched an event nothing had ever wired a listener to. Downloaded songs and recency now have separate budgets and the media cache is protected across deploys; offline navigation falls back for every page type.
- ci: **serialise deploys per branch, then bump the version on alpha pushes too** (#1595, #1596) — `deploy.yml` had no concurrency group, so two pushes to the same branch within the deploy window could run two simultaneous `lftp --reverse --delete` mirrors against one docroot and interleave destructively (one run's delete pass, working from a stale listing, removing a file the other had just uploaded) — both report success. Deploys now queue rather than cancel (`cancel-in-progress: false`, deliberately the opposite of the Apple workflow's setting, since cancelling a half-finished `--delete` mirror is unsafe). Separately, `version-bump.yml` ran on `beta` only, so `alpha` — where nearly all work lands — could go long stretches without a version bump; one prior batch shipped 98 commits under one unchanged version string and needed a manual bump. Alpha is now included, with the bump commit's `[skip ci]` (correctly kept, to avoid re-running the whole CI matrix for a one-line string change) compensated for by an explicit `deploy.yml` dispatch, safe only because of the concurrency fix above.
- feat(a11y): **consolidate all nine site typeaheads onto one shared keyboard + ARIA combobox helper** (#1594) — a reported "location picker is mouse-only" bug turned out to be the healthiest of nine divergent typeahead implementations; the more likely cause was Chrome's address-autofill overlay intercepting keystrokes on an address-shaped field (mitigated with an autocomplete token Chrome doesn't recognise as an address hint). The audit found three genuine defects along the way — Tab silently discarded the highlighted option instead of committing it, `aria-activedescendant` was never set so the highlight was invisible to a screen reader, and Escape inside the Credit People drawer closed the whole Bootstrap offcanvas instead of just the suggestion panel — and generalised the fix into a shared `js/modules/combobox-a11y.js` (key semantics + ARIA bookkeeping only, so each of the nine call sites keeps its own fetch/render/commit logic), now used by location search, tag entry, credit-person/place pickers, service broadcast, work-member search, comparison and general search, with deliberate asymmetries called out in code (service broadcast gets no Tab-commit, since broadcasting a song on a stray Tab is worse than the convenience it offers).
- fix(lang-filter): **a URL-object bug in the global fetch header patch silently killed ten unrelated call sites, not just Song of the Day** (#1593) — `songbook-language-filter.js`'s `window.fetch` monkey-patch resolved a request's URL with `input.url`, correct for a string or `Request` but silently `undefined` for a `URL` object (whose property is `href`); the very next line called `.startsWith()` on that `undefined` and threw, escaping the module's own `try`/`catch` one line later. Because the patch wraps every fetch on the site, this wasn't a missing header — it was a failed request wherever the caller's own catch block happened to swallow it, and Song of the Day's caught error cleared its own markup, making the card silently vanish with no error visible anywhere. The fix (`requestUrlOf()`, handling string/URL/Request explicitly, wrapping the whole patched body so a future header bug degrades to an unheadered request rather than a failed one) also incidentally fixes the same latent crash in search, related-songs, shuffle, the numeric keypad and compare — none of which had been reported yet. Song of the Day now also tells a genuine "no song matches your filter" apart from a caught error, instead of rendering the two identically as nothing.
- test(importers): **repair and wire the OpenSong, VideoPsalm and FreeShow importer suites into CI** (#1538, #1575) — all three had been failing for a purely mechanical reason (each scraped its own source text for a code block that had since moved) but were never referenced by `test.yml`, so nothing ran them and the ongoing red went unnoticed — a suite nobody runs is a worse gap than a suite that's absent, because it looks like coverage exists. Adds a new 62-assertion FreeShow parser suite against real archived `.show` fixtures and wires all three into CI.
- fix(csp): **extract Request-a-Song's inline `<script>` — the SPA fragment inline-script allowlist is now empty** (#1572) — the last surviving case of #1565's failure shape: an executable inline script inside a shared-cache SPA fragment, silently refused by the enforcing nonce CSP since the fragment response can never carry the document's per-request nonce. The form's entire client-side path, including the offline submission queue (#337), had therefore never run — every submission silently fell through to the no-JS fallback. Extracted to a real ES module wired from the router, reading its prefill inputs from `data-*` attributes the fragment now emits. `tests/php/test-fragment-inline-scripts.php`'s allowlist is now genuinely empty, making the guard absolute against any future violation.
- feat(service-mode): **congregant join QR code on the projection screen** (#1339) — typing a rotating join code off a projected screen was the friction; the QR removes it, opening the app on the correct same-origin host (respecting the per-environment `Channel` wall, rule #26) with the code still shown on-screen and readable, since the QR is `aria-hidden` and never the only way to join. Uses a vendored, exactly-pinned QR encoder (SRI-hashed, falling back to a local copy per the #1587 pattern) rather than a hand-rolled one, verified by an independently-written from-spec QR decoder run against the library's real output in CI — proof the payload actually decodes correctly, not just that it produced something. Scanning the projected code with a real phone at projector distance remains unverified pending owner testing.
- feat(song): **render per-line translations on the public song page, behind a "Show translation" toggle** (#1089 / #1100 P1) — corrects a standing belief that per-line translation had no write path; it did (the v2 editor's enrichment panel has written them for a while) — what was missing was any reader. Nothing in `song.php` or the JS modules displayed an approved translation once a curator entered one, so the feature was invisible end-to-end on the side that mattered. The toggle and its rows render only when a song actually has approved translations, wired as a real ES module from the router (never a fragment inline script, since `page=song` is shared-cache and can never carry a nonce — rule #30) and kept deliberately distinct from the unrelated whole-song-translation section that already exists lower on the same page. The full bilingual side-by-side reader remains open as #1100.
- feat(export): **ChordPro export now emits inline `[chord]` markers — the format's entire point** (#1080) — ChordPro export had been lyrics-only since it shipped, which is the chord-chart format with its purpose removed. Chords are aligned to lyrics by word index (there are no stored character offsets in this schema; the app's chord model is one chord line per lyric line, not a positioned overlay), verified code-point-safe against both a diacritic-heavy Portuguese fixture and a genuine astral-plane character, which matters given roughly two-thirds of the catalogue's songbooks are non-English. Also fixed the same gap in the v2 editor's own ChordPro export, which mapped components without their chords and would have kept producing lyrics-only files even after the public page was fixed.
- perf(songbook): **render the songbook listing page from the scoped slim index instead of hydrating every song in the book** (#1037, two commits) — `includes/pages/songbook.php` called `getSongs($bookId)` — full lyric-component hydration for every song — purely to list titles; for Mission Praise's ~3,500 songs that's one of the two most expensive public pages on shared hosting, on every uncached view. First moved to the existing slim index (with a captured-and-reproduced baseline proving every UI element survives — the unofficial-songbook badge, display-abbreviation handling, language badges, offline-download hooks — since a silently dropped badge is exactly this project's most common regression shape); then, since the slim index took no filter argument, the page was still pulling all ~14,500 catalogue rows and filtering to one book in PHP — `getSongsSlimIndex()` gained an optional songbook-scoped `WHERE`, purely additive for the index's other callers.
- fix(auth): **rate-limit password reset, harden per-account login lockout, add a real `/health` liveness probe** (#1028, #1027, #1022) — `auth_forgot_password` generated reset tokens with no rate limiting at all, so a known account could be flooded on every request; the limiter is keyed carefully to avoid becoming the account-enumeration oracle the endpoint's identical-200-either-way response was designed to prevent (#898). Login lockout is per-IP only (`tblLoginAttempts` has no `UserId` column), hardened as far as possible without a schema change — the deeper fix needs an owner-gated migration and is tracked rather than guessed at here. The pre-existing `action=health` is a songbook-completeness report, not a liveness check; a new, genuinely minimal `/health` returns 200 when the database answers and 503 otherwise, leaking no version, hostname, schema or error detail — something an uptime monitor can watch to catch the next outage before a tester does.
- fix(sql): **`migrate-json.php` now requires `confirm=1`** — the legacy pre-#1010 JSON bootstrap importer TRUNCATEs `tblSongComponents`/`tblSongComposers`/`tblSongWriters`/`tblSongs`/`tblSongbooks` and re-imports the (now months-stale) repo `data/songs.json`, and was reachable from `/manage/setup-database` via a single unconfirmed GET (`?action=migrate`) — its only guard was a client-side `confirm()` dialog, which does nothing against a bookmarked link, a replayed URL, or a curl/automation request. Now gated server-side exactly like its sibling destructive scripts (`restore.php`, `drop-legacy-tables.php`): `--confirm` (CLI) / `?confirm=1` (web) below an unconditional safe preview, plus the setup-database card's existing type-to-confirm speed-bump (#1298). commit `82c4ee15` (cherry-picked to `beta` as `cd678484` and `main` as `4df899ce`).
- fix(service-mode): **ad-hoc sessions no longer start already-expired** (#1576). `service_session_start`'s no-schedule-selected path hardcodes a placeholder 10:00/90-minute window; `serviceMode_occurrenceEndUtc()` capped the computed end at a hard now+4h ceiling but had no floor, so an evening ad-hoc start produced an `ExpiresAt` already in the past — every presence row was inserted expired and congregants saw "The service has ended." seconds after joining, with no visible failure on the operator's projection page. Adds an `$isAdHoc` floor (still subject to the existing hard ceiling), scoped to the ad-hoc path only — a genuinely-past *scheduled* occurrence still reports honestly expired. Also fixes the projection page's date field defaulting to the UTC calendar date instead of the browser's local date. +79 assertions in `tests/php/test-service-mode-occurrence.php`. commit `c911abcc`.
- ci(apple): **run `swift test` serially** (#1558) — the Apple `build` job's dominant CI failure (2 of 3 runs on PR #1585) was cross-suite Keychain contention: Swift Testing runs different `@Suite` types concurrently by default, and several suites drive the real macOS Keychain through `LANRemoteIdentityFactory`; on an unsigned, entitlement-less `swift test` binary with no unlocked login keychain, concurrent `SecItemAdd`/`SecItemCopyMatching` load makes the daemon intermittently return `errSecItemNotFound`. Each suite's own `.serialized` trait only prevented overlap within itself, not against other suites. Workflow-only change (no test-code edit); the proper fix — a Keychain round-trip probe that lets headless CI cleanly skip these suites — is tracked in #1558/#1559 pending a machine that can run `swift test`. commit `79f17670`.
- fix(deploy): **exclude `data/audio` and `data/music` from the docroot mirror** (#1584, commit `8ef7b587`) — the deploy mirrors `public_html/` in `--reverse --delete` mode with no exclusion for `data/`, and the song audio (`/data/audio/<SongId>.mp3`) + sheet music (`/data/music/`) exist on the SERVER only (git tracks zero files under `appWeb/public_html/data/`), so every deploy was telling lftp the media library shouldn't exist. Got sharper once #1583's What's New generator started writing `public_html/data/whats-new.md` before the mirror ran — `data/` gained a local counterpart, so the delete became surgical instead of wiping the whole directory. Scoped to the two media subdirectories, not a blanket `^data/` exclude (which would also stop What's New deploying). **Not yet verified** whether the media survives on any of the three live servers — needs host access, tracked in #1584.

- feat(web): **What's New page + clearer environment signalling** (#1583). Alpha testers had no way to see what changed between deploys, and therefore no way to know what to test — `CHANGELOG.md` lives at the repo root and is never deployed (`deploy.yml` mirrors only `public_html/`). A new deploy step extracts the three most recent version sections into `public_html/data/whats-new.md` (hard-capped, UTF-8-safe), and a new `/whats-new` SPA fragment renders it through a purpose-built `includes/markdown_lite.php`.
